# Verification of the MecwayAsterBridge

*Nederlandse versie: [Verificatie.md](../Verificatie.md)*

This report shows with reproducible tests that the chain Mecway model (`.liml`) →
MecwayAsterBridge → Code-Aster 17.4 → results in Mecway gives correct answers. Each
test compares a FEM quantity with an analytical solution or a recognised benchmark, in
the style of the NAFEMS benchmarks (NAFEMS, 1990): small, focused and with a tolerance
justified beforehand. In addition, the Code-Aster installation itself was tested with
19 official validation tests (§10).

Contents: 1 Method · 2 Running · 3 Summary · 4 Linear static (LS) and error estimator (FS) · 5 Nonlinear static
(NL) · 6 Dynamics (DY) · 7 Modal (MO) and buckling (BU) · 8 Thermal (TH) ·
9 Assumptions and not covered · 10 Official Code-Aster validation tests · 11 References

## 1. Method

- **Models** are generated by Python scripts (`TestFiles/Verificatie/tests_*.py`, with
  `verif_lib.py`) and stored as ordinary Mecway models in
  `TestFiles/Verificatie/modellen/` (they can be opened in Mecway).
- **Solving**: `TestFiles/Verificatie/_run_one.py` takes each model through the same
  steps as a run from Mecway (`bridge_from_liml` → `bridge_mesh` →
  `bridge_to_aster`/`bridge_thermal` → Code-Aster), without the Mecway API. The results
  come from the same results file that Mecway loads.
- **Models written by Mecway**: the generator writes the `.liml` itself, not Mecway.
  With `--mecway-opslaan` (§2) Mecway first opens each model and saves it again
  (its own XML layout and order, units on every value, defaults such as `colorrgb`, an
  empty `<solution>`), so the bridge reads the file as a user saves it in Mecway.
  Outcome (7 October 2026, bridge 8742df0): all 270 test cases could go through
  Mecway, and all 1611 checks are identical to the normal run (same status, same FEM
  value); the `.comm` is the same, apart from the last digits in 15 cases (Mecway
  writes numbers with 15 significant digits).
- **Settings**: the defaults of `CONFIG_BRIDGE` (`Scripts/bridge_utils.py`), unless
  stated otherwise for a test.
- **Comparison**: per check the FEM value, the reference, the deviation and the
  tolerance. Status OK, NOK, FOUT (no result, or the requested time was not reached) or
  BEKEND (known: a deviation caused by a documented limitation, with its number in
  `KNOWN_BUGS.md`).
- **Mesh convergence**: series with decreasing element size h. Per series the error per
  mesh, the order p from successive errors and a Richardson extrapolation from the last
  three meshes (order and limit, independent of the reference). A limit that differs
  from the reference points to a model error of the theory (e.g. the clamped solid beam
  against beam theory), not to discretisation.
- **Tolerance policy**: the finest mesh gets tol_f = model error of the analytical
  theory + rounding of the reference + generously the estimated discretisation error. A
  coarser mesh with element size h gets tol_f·(h/h_f)^p (p = expected order, capped at
  50%); a coarse mesh thus only tests whether the error fits convergence towards the
  reference. Where the FEM solution is exact (e.g. Euler beam with a tip load,
  homogeneous stress) the tolerance is 0.1% or less. Tolerances were not widened
  afterwards; any deviation from this rule is justified with the test.
- **Reference values** come from the literature (§11) or from an accurate calculation of
  our own (series expansion, Runge-Kutta, the same discrete scheme in 1D), which is in
  the test module.

## 2. Running

```bash
cd TestFiles/Verificatie
python run_verificatie.py            # everything (about an hour)
python run_verificatie.py LS NL-02   # only ids starting like this
python run_verificatie.py --report   # only regenerate the report
```

Requires CPython 3 and a working Code-Aster installation (`exe_aster` in
`Scripts/bridge_utils.py`). Output: `TestFiles/Verificatie/resultaten/resultaten.md`
(all checks and the mesh convergence), `resultaten.csv`, `resultaten.json` and the
generated `.comm` per test in `resultaten/comm/` (absolute paths in it replaced by
`<WORKDIR>` and `<REPO>`, so the repository holds no machine-specific paths). Working files are in
`%TEMP%/mab_verificatie` (can be set with the environment variable `VERIF_WORK`). Heavy cases outside the standard regression (WE-10b, ~1 hour) only run with `VERIF_ZWAAR=1`.

**Through Mecway** (`python run_verificatie.py --mecway-opslaan [ids]`, or
`VERIF_MECWAY=1`): each generated model first goes through Mecway,
`mecway.exe <model>.liml script TestFiles/Tools/mw_save_as.py` (Mecway manual §16.1:
open the model, run the script, exit; no window and no solve, so it also works for
analyses Mecway cannot solve itself, such as XFEM, welding and fatigue). The script
only calls `mw.save_as()`. The bridge then solves that Mecway copy (in
`VERIF_WORK/_mecway/`); `run_verificatie` first checks that Mecway did not change node
numbers, coordinates or node selections. Output in `resultaten/mecway/` (the normal
results are left unchanged) and `resultaten/mecway/vergelijking.md` (only that file is in the repo): per check the
status and FEM value next to the normal run, and whether the `.comm` (without comments
and paths) is the same. `--mecway-opslaan --vergelijk` only regenerates that
comparison. It costs about 2 s of Mecway per model on top of the normal run. What
Mecway changes when saving: units on values without a unit (`816.0` →
`816 Pa.s/m`), numbers written differently (`1e+08 Pa` → `100000000 Pa`, 15
significant digits), selections in a different order, defaults added (`colorrgb`,
`selfcontact`, an empty `<mechanical type="Isotropic" />` on a thermal-only material,
a `<hardeningcurve strain="0" stress="0 Pa" />` with bilinear plasticity) and some
attributes dropped (`automatictimestepping="false"`, the constant values next to a
TempDependent table). Node and element numbers stay the same. After its first loop
AD-02 already runs on models that Mecway itself refined and saved; WE-10b (only with
`VERIF_ZWAAR=1`) was not part of the comparison.

Adding a test: a `Case` in a `tests_<group>.py` with a function that builds a `Model`
and a list of `Check`s (see `verif_lib.py` and the existing modules).

## 3. Summary

Status 28 October 2026. The whole table comes from one revision: the regression in
`TestFiles/Verificatie/resultaten/resultaten.md`, a full run on bridge `8b13519`
(after V2026.4: among others the mass and reaction check, cable pretension, buckling
with constant loads, hysteretic damping, adaptive time steps, line search, smoothed
contact normals, the section properties, VTU export, the *failed* field, the hex8
warnings for rubber, the heat flux per element node, the result formulas (S33) and the
principal stresses and strains (S34); Code-Aster 17.4): 442 test cases, 2382 checks, 2376
OK, 6 BEKEND (known), 0 NOK/FOUT. Compared with the run on `5ef612d` no status change and
no FEM value more than 0.1% different; new are 8 checks in FX-02 (principal values and the
formulas `s1`, `tr`, `e1`).

No NOK or FOUT (WE-10b, only with `VERIF_ZWAAR=1`, not counted: 9 of 9 OK). Official Code-Aster tests: 19 of 19 OK (1598 TEST_RESU checks, 0 NOOK).

Added after this run (not yet in a full regression, not in the table): Bolt pretension
PT-01..05, 5 cases, 22 checks OK (28 October 2026, step S15); PT-06..10, 5 cases, 17
checks OK (28 October 2026, step S15b).

**Known limitations** (status BEKEND):
- KNOWN_BUGS 43: Lagrange contact (`contact_algo` = STANDARD) on a line contact between
  hex20 solids stops (NL-03a, 3 checks). Use PENALISATION (NL-03b: exact).
- KNOWN_BUGS 83: K_I from `CALC_G_XFEM` on a curved front +5.3 % (BR-10, 1 check,
  tolerance 5 %). Use K from `POST_K1_K2_K3` (+1.6 %) or from G (+1.0 %) there.

**Bridge errors found by this verification and fixed**:
- Error estimator: Code-Aster's `ERME` computes `FORCE_FACE` with the reversed sign (block under
  uniform tension: 15% instead of 0); the bridge reverses it in the load of the estimator (AD,
  [Error_estimator.md](Error_estimator.md) §2.3; FS unchanged, ≤ 3e-4).
- Mecway's *tangent modulus* for bilinear plasticity was read as a slope in total
  strain; it is the plastic modulus H. Now `D_SIGM_EPSI` = E·H/(E+H) (NL-01).
- Contact on quadratic faces did not pass the patch test with the default integration
  (up to 4.6% error); now `INTEGRATION='NCOTES'` for a quadratic slave face (NL-03c–f).
- Springs and dampers only acted in global X; now along their own axis (LS-06).
- *Node temperature* (`temperature3`) was ignored in a mechanical analysis (only an
  INFO); now a temperature per node in the nodal field (LS-08).
- A temperature gave no thermal stress in `DYNA_NON_LINE` (the field from t = 0 counted
  as the initial state, KNOWN_BUGS 94, fixed); now an `EVOL_THER` from the reference (DY-70). A
  time table of a *Temperature* was not translated; now in L30 and D31 (NL-70..74,
  DY-71).
- Bonded contact between shells stopped with `FACTOR_11` (the rotation about the
  plate normal was tied as well); now without that rotation (LS-07). A skewed or
  curved master and a quadratic shell (quad8/tri6) as master still stopped with
  `FACTOR_11` and `CHARGES2_31`; now the bridge writes that tie itself (LS-07f–k).
- In explicit dynamics, rounding in `DEFI_LIST_INST` gave an extra solver step.
- Temperature-dependent ρc: `THER_NON_LINE` interpolates the enthalpy linearly between
  the table points; the bridge now refines the table (energy balance from −2.8% to
  0.002%, WE-04b).
- Two weld sources (`#ASTER-LASBRON`) acted as twice the first one: Code-Aster 17.4
  computes two `AFFE_CHAR_THER_F` loads with `SOURCE` in one analysis with the function
  of the first; the weld sources are now in one load (WE-11), now also with the
  Mecway sources as a formula or time table (TH-40 .. TH-42).
- Filler material: with the consistent heat capacity a not-yet-born node heated up
  through the mass coupling (+138 K); now `3D_DIAG` (WE-13).

## 4. Linear static (LS)

Mecway S30 → bridge `lin_static_3d` → `MECA_STATIQUE`. Module
`tests_lineair_statisch.py`, run with `python run_verificatie.py LS`. Material
everywhere steel, E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3 (G = 80.77 GPa); SI units. The column "orde p"
in `resultaten.md` is computed from the error relative to the reference; when the
reference has a model error (LS-02), the order from the differences between successive
meshes is given below.

### LS-01 Cantilever, beam elements

- **Purpose**: beam elements (`POU_D_E`), `fixed` and a point load.
- **Model**: L = 1 m, round Ø 20 mm, 10 line2.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: clamped at x = 0, tip load P = 100 N transverse.
- **Reference**: δ = PL³/3EI, θ = PL²/2EI (Young & Budynas, 2002, table 8.1, case 1a).
- **Result**: δ, θ and the reaction deviate 0.00% (tolerance 0.1%).
- **Interpretation**: Euler-Bernoulli elements are exact for this load; the translation
  of cross-section, material and load is correct.

### LS-02 Cantilever, solid elements

- **Purpose**: solid elements (hex8 `3D`, hex8 `LINE_QUAD`, hex20), `traction`, nodal
  stresses (`SIGM_NOEU`) and reactions.
- **Model**: 1 × 0.1 × 0.1 m (axis x), cross-section n × n and length 10n cubes. Series
  hex8 `3D` (default; n = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16), hex8 `LINE_QUAD` (n = 1, 2, 4) and hex20
  (n = 1, 2, 4).
- **Boundary conditions and load**: `fixed` at x = 0; `traction` −1 MPa in z on the end
  face (P = 10 kN).
- **Reference**: Timoshenko beam δ = PL³/3EI + PL/(κGA), κ = 5/6:
  1.9048e-3 + 0.0149e-3 = 1.9196e-3 m (shear 0.77%). σ_xx = Mc/I = 48.0 MPa at
  x = 0.2 m, top face (outside the disturbance of the clamp). Reaction ΣRz = P.
  Measured: mean DZ of the end face; `stressxx` at the node nearest to
  (0.2; 0.05; 0.1).
- **Result**: hex20 n = 4: δ −0.81% (tol. 1%), σ_xx 0.00%. `LINE_QUAD` gives the same as
  hex20 on every mesh (same HEXA20 mesh). hex8 `3D`: δ −35.7 / −13.1 / −4.3 / −1.7 /
  −0.9% (n = 1…16). The reaction is exact on every mesh.
- **Interpretation**:
  - Quadratic: the differences between successive meshes (18.8e-6 and 4.7e-6 m) give
    p = 2.0 and the Richardson limit δ∞ = 1.9057e-3 m: +0.05% above Euler-Bernoulli and
    −0.72% below Timoshenko. That is a model error of beam theory: a fully held end
    face prevents warping and lateral contraction and stiffens about as much as the
    shear term adds. The singular edges of the clamp limit the order to 2. Mecway
    headless (internal solver and CCX, C3D20) gives δ = 1.904148e-3 (n = 4) and
    1.899446e-3 m (n = 2) on the same meshes, equal to the bridge to 7 digits.
    tol_f = 1%.
  - hex8 `3D` (fully integrated, without incompatible modes) is much too stiff with few
    elements through the height (KNOWN_BUGS 1); Mecway (incompatible hex8) gives −1.5 /
    −1.3 / −1.0% (n = 1, 2, 4) on the same meshes. The series converges with p ≈ 1.9 to
    the same limit (Richardson n = 4/8/16: 1.9067e-3 m). The nodal stress converges
    slowly and not monotonically (+0.8 / +1.3 / +0.9% at n = 4/8/16), as expected for an
    extrapolated nodal stress of a linear element (O(h)). tol_f: 2% (δ), 3% (σ).

### LS-03 Square plate, simply supported, uniform pressure

- **Purpose**: shells (DKT and `COQUE_3D`), `surfacepressure` on shell faces,
  `noderotation` and the von Mises fields of shells.
- **Model**: a = 1 m, t = 10 mm (a/t = 100), plane z = 0, n × n elements. Series quad4
  → DKT (n = 4, 8, 16, 32) and quad8 → `COQUE_3D` (n = 2, 4, 8, 16).
- **Boundary conditions and load**: all edge nodes DX = DY = DZ = 0 (three
  `displacement`s) and "hard" simple support: `noderotation` 0 about x on x = 0 and
  x = a, about y on y = 0 and y = a. Pressure 10 kPa on the top face.
- **Reference**: Navier series (Timoshenko & Woinowsky-Krieger, 1959, §30, table 8;
  m, n ≤ 401): w = αqa⁴/D with α = 0.0040624, D = Et³/12(1−ν²) → 2.1124e-3 m;
  M = βqa² with β = 0.047886 → σ = 6M/t² = 28.73 MPa. At the centre σ_x = σ_y and
  τ_xy = 0, so von Mises = |σ_x|; compared with `vonmisesu1`/`vonmisesb` (the bridge
  only sends von Mises of shells to Mecway).
- **Result**: DKT n = 32: w −0.003%, σ +0.06%. `COQUE_3D` n = 16: w +0.05%, σ +0.41%.
- **Interpretation**: DKT converges with p ≈ 2.1–2.3 (w) and 2.0 (σ) to 2.11241e-3 m and
  28.732 MPa (−0.0005% and +0.002%). `COQUE_3D`: σ with p ≈ 2.0 to 28.717 MPa (−0.05%),
  w to 2.11351e-3 m (+0.052%); that difference is the shear deflection of the Mindlin
  formulation, 2π²D/(κGta²) = +0.056%. Mecway (`COQUE_3D` mesh n = 16): 2.1146e-3 m
  (+0.05% relative to the bridge). tol_f: w 0.2%, σ 0.5% (DKT) and 1% (`COQUE_3D`).
  Two remarks on supports: (1) Mecway's `pinnedsupport` is "mean displacement zero" over
  the selection (the bridge translates it as `LIAISON_DDL`); an edge held node by node
  needs `displacement`. (2) With only DZ = 0 ("soft" support) `COQUE_3D` converges to
  +0.91% (w) and +1.06% (σ): the boundary layer of a Mindlin plate, an effect of order
  t/a. DKT (Kirchhoff) does not notice it. The hard support belongs to the Navier
  solution.

### LS-04 Thick-walled cylinder under internal pressure (Lamé)

- **Purpose**: `symmetry` (`LIAISON_OBLIQUE`), `frictionless` (`FACE_IMPO`) and pressure
  on a curved face.
- **Model**: a = 0.1 m, b = 0.2 m, quarter model (0–90°), axial thickness 0.01 m, one
  element in z; hex20, n radial × 2n tangential (n = 1, 2, 4, 8), mid-side nodes on the
  circle.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: `symmetry` on y = 0 and x = 0; `frictionless` on
  z = 0 and z = L (plane strain, ε_z = 0); pressure 100 MPa on the inner wall.
- **Reference** (Lamé; Timoshenko & Goodier, 1970): A = pa²/(b²−a²),
  B = pa²b²/(b²−a²); σ_θ(a) = A + B/a² = 166.67 MPa; σ_zz = 2νA = 20 MPa;
  u_r(a) = (1+ν)/E·[(1−2ν)Aa + B/a] = 9.0794e-5 m. Measured at the node (a, 0, 0):
  `displx`, `stressyy` (= σ_θ) and `stresszz`.
- **Result** n = 8: u_r −0.001%, σ_θ −0.18%, σ_zz +0.42%.
- **Interpretation**: u_r converges with p ≈ 3.8 to 9.07937e-5 m (exact), σ_zz with
  p ≈ 1.8 to 19.98 MPa. σ_θ on the inner wall (+0.10 / −0.62 / −0.46 / −0.18%) is still
  pre-asymptotic: the node lies on the symmetry plane and σ_θ ∝ 1/r² is extrapolated to
  the loaded edge. Further into the wall the error at the corner nodes is ≤ 0.15%, at
  the mid-side nodes 1–2% (n = 4), the usual picture of hex20 nodal stresses. tol_f:
  u_r 0.1%, σ_θ 0.5% (p = 1), σ_zz 1%.

### LS-05 NAFEMS LE1, elliptic membrane

- **Purpose**: a recognised benchmark with a stress concentration; shells as a membrane
  and solids; pressure on shell edges.
- **Model**: inner ellipse (x/2)² + y² = 1, outer ellipse (x/3.25)² + (y/2.75)² = 1,
  thickness 0.1 m, quarter model, point D = (2, 0). Mesh n × 2n (n = 2, 4, 8, 16),
  graded towards D in both directions (piecewise linear, so mid-side nodes are at the
  middle of the edge; a smooth grading puts them at the quarter point and gives a
  singular element at D). Series quad8 → `COQUE_3D` (DZ = 0 on all nodes) and hex20, one
  element thick (DZ = 0 at one node).
- **Boundary conditions and load**: `symmetry` on y = 0 and x = 0; pressure −10 MPa
  (tension) on the outer edge. On shell edges this becomes consistent nodal forces;
  check ΣR_x = −2.75 MN and ΣR_y = −3.25 MN (= p·t·2.75 and p·t·3.25).
- **Reference**: σ_yy(D) = 92.7 MPa, plane stress (NAFEMS, 1990, test LE1). Shell:
  `vonmisesm` (at D σ_xx = τ_xy = 0, so von Mises = σ_yy); solid: `stressyy`.
- **Result** n = 16: `COQUE_3D` 92.654 MPa (−0.05%), hex20 92.660 MPa (−0.04%).
- **Interpretation**: both series converge with p ≈ 3.8–4.1 to 92.66 MPa (Richardson);
  the remaining difference is within the rounding of the reference (92.65…92.75).
  tol_f = 0.5%. Mecway (`COQUE_3D` mesh n = 8): u_x(D) equal to 0.06%. Code-Aster 17.4
  itself has no LE1 test (it does have LE3 = ssls105a, §10).

### LS-06 Cantilever with a spring at the tip

- **Purpose**: Mecway spring (line2 with material *Spring*) along its own axis
  (`DISCRET` with `REPERE='LOCAL'`).
- **Model**: beam as LS-01 (10 line2, Ø 20 mm); a spring k = 1000 N/m from the tip to a
  fixed point 0.5 m away, (a) along Y, (b) at 45° in the XY plane.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: beam clamped, spring point `fixed`; P = 10 N in Y at
  the tip.
- **Reference**: beam and spring in parallel: (a) δ = P/(3EI/L³ + k), spring force kδ;
  (b) the tip moves (axially almost rigid) only in Y, the spring stretches δ/√2 and
  gives kδ/2 in Y: δ = P/(3EI/L³ + k/2).
- **Result**: (a) δ and spring force deviate 0.00% (tol. 0.1%); (b) δ 0.00% (tol. 0.2%).
- **Interpretation**: the spring acts along its own axis. Before the fix a spring always
  acted in global X, even when it lay along Y (KNOWN_BUGS 47 describes what has not yet
  been compared with Mecway).

### LS-07 Bonded contact between shells

- **Purpose**: Mecway's bonded contact (`bondedtie`) between plates with non-matching
  meshes → `LIAISON_MAIL` `TYPE_RACCORD='COQUE'`; does the seam carry a moment?
- **Model**: (a) cantilever plate strip 200 × 20 × 2 mm (DKT quad4) in two parts with
  their own nodes: A (x = 0..100 mm, 10 × 4) and B (x = 100..200 mm, 10 × 7), bonded
  along the seam (slave = edge of B, master = shell faces of A); (b) the same strip as
  one mesh 20 × 4. (c–e) T-joint: plate 100 × 60 mm, long edges clamped, with a rib
  60 × 40 mm (plane y = 30 mm) whose bottom edge is bonded onto the plate: (c) rib 13
  elements long against 20 in the plate, foot line on a mesh line of the plate; (d) foot
  line in the middle of the plate elements (15 across the width); (e) shared nodes.
  Example model (a): `TestFiles/Voorbeelden/Voorbeeld_T.liml`.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: (a, b) `fixed` on the edge x = 0, P = 1 N in −Z on
  the free edge; (c–e) 1 N in +Y on the top edge of the rib (the rib bends out of its
  plane; if the bond were a hinge, the model would be a mechanism).
- **Reference**: (a, b) Euler beam δ = PL³/(3EI), φ = PL²/(2EI) with E: with free side
  edges M_y = 0 and the bending stiffness per width is E t³/12; the clamping prevents
  the anticlastic curvature over a strip ~b (b/L = 0.1), which makes the plate slightly
  stiffer (tolerance 2 %). Also Mecway 34 (own solver) on model (b) (tolerance 0.5 %)
  and the rotation on both sides of the seam (equal, 0.5 %). (c, d) model (e) (same
  scheme) and Mecway 34 on (e).
- **Result**: (a) δ −1.07 % against Euler, +0.08 % against Mecway; φ −0.74 % and
  −0.00 %; rotation B/A across the seam 0.99992. (b) δ −1.03 %, Mecway +0.12 %.
  (c) +0.10 % below (e), +0.42 % against Mecway. (d) −5.2 % (tolerance 7 %). (e) +0.51 %
  against Mecway.
- **Interpretation**: the seam is rigid, also in bending: the bonded strip is within
  0.04 % of one continuous mesh. Before the fix in `bridge_mesh._coque_ddl`,
  `LIAISON_MAIL` also tied the rotation about the plate normal (only a fictitious
  stiffness in DKT) and Code-Aster stopped with `FACTOR_11`; now that rotation is left
  out when the master is normal to a global axis (otherwise see (f–k)). In (d)
  `LIAISON_MAIL` interpolates the plate rotation linearly across the kink under the
  rib: 12 mm elements −11 %, 4 mm −5 %, 1.3 mm −2 %; put the foot line on a mesh line.
  Mecway's own solver does not solve (a), (c) and (d): with a shell edge as slave it ties
  no rotations (mechanism, tip 3.7e6 m), so Mecway is only compared on the continuous
  meshes.
- **Model (f–k)**: (f) strip (a) rotated 30° about the x axis (skewed master), load
  along the plate normal; (g) thin-walled tube R = 20 mm, t = 2 mm, L = 200 mm, clamped,
  1 N in −Z on the free edge, made of two parts (16 and 24 elements around) bonded at
  x = 100 mm (curved master); (h) the same tube as one mesh, 24 around; (i) quad8 plate
  100 × 40 mm (`COQUE_3D`, 10 × 4), clamped at x = 0, with a quad8 rib (height 20 mm)
  whose foot line lies in the middle of the plate elements (y = 25 mm), 10 N side load
  on the top edge; (j) the same with shared nodes (plate 10 × 8); (k) model (i) rotated
  30°.
- **Reference (f–k)**: (f) model (a) and Euler; (g) model (h) and Timoshenko
  (I = πR³t, shear area πRt); (h) Timoshenko and Mecway 34; (i) model (j); (j) Mecway 34;
  (k) model (i) (the same model in the XY plane) and (j).
- **Result (f–k)**: (f) δ 0.94220 mm, equal to (a); rotation B/A 0.99992. (g)
  2.7306e-7 m: +0.11 % from (h), +0.27 % from Timoshenko (the tube with 16 around as
  one mesh: 2.7707e-7 m; the bonded tube lies in between). (h) +0.16 % from Timoshenko,
  +0.15 % from Mecway. (i) −1.7 % below (j) (the kink under the rib, as in (d); plate
  10 × 12: −0.5 %, 20 × 20: +0.1 % from (j)). (j) +1.2 % from Mecway (its own quad8
  shell). (k) −0.05 % from (i).
- **Interpretation (f–k)**: with a skewed or curved master the bridge writes
  `LIAISON_DDL` per slave node (displacements and the two rotations in the tangent plane
  of the master) and sets `NPREC` to −1: otherwise MUMPS wrongly reports `FACTOR_11`
  (measured: NPREC 8 and 10 stop, 12 and −1 give the same solution). With a quadratic
  master the bridge only writes the displacements (`LIAISON_MAIL` asked for DX of the
  QUAD9 centre node, `CHARGES2_31`); the rotations stay with `LIAISON_MAIL`, so the
  centre node counts. With the rotations without the centre node, (i) was 8.7 % too
  flexible.

### LS-08 Node temperature (thermal stress per node)

- **Purpose**: Mecway's *Node temperature* (`temperature3`, a temperature per node, also
  the result of *Transfer temperatures from solution*) with a *Thermal stress* → nodal
  field `TEMP` in `AFFE_VARC`, like *Temperature* (`temperature2`).
- **Model**: α = 1.2e-5 1/K, E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3, Tref = 293.15 K. (a) bar as TH-03b
  (1 × 0.1 × 0.1 m, 4 hex8, u_x = 0 at both ends, transversely free), Node temperature
  Tref + 100 K on all nodes. (b) free beam 100 × 10 × 10 mm (10 × 1 × 4 hex20) with
  T = Tref + 100 K · z/h on every node (linear through the thickness); u_x = 0 at x = 0,
  u_y = 0 at y = 0, u_z = 0 at the origin. (c) free bar 100 × 10 × 10 mm (4 hex8):
  `temperature2` Tref + 100 K on the nodes at x = 0 and Node temperature Tref + 200 K on
  those at x = L; the nodes in between get no temperature. (d) as (b) with hex8 and
  `elem_hex8` = `LINE_QUAD`.
- **Reference**: (a) σ_xx = −EαΔT, u_y = (1+ν)αΔT·b, reaction EαΔT·A. (b, d) linear
  temperature in a free body: stress-free, u_z = −αg(x² + y² − z²)/2 with g = ΔT/h
  (Timoshenko & Goodier §150): u_z(L, 0, 0) = −6.0e-4 m. (c) nodes without a temperature
  at Tref, element means +50 and +100 K: u_x(L) = α·150 K·L/4 = 4.5e-5 m. In addition
  Mecway 34 (own solver, headless) on (a)–(c).
- **Result**: all quantities exact (≤ 2e-10); max |σ_xx| in (b) and (d) 0.04 Pa.
  Mecway: (a) reaction 2.52e6 N, (b) −6.0000e-4 m, (c) 4.5000e-5 m, all three equal.
- **Interpretation**: Node temperature works like Temperature; nodes without a
  temperature are at the reference, also next to nodes with a temperature (c, equal to
  Mecway). Mecway 34 interpolates the nodal temperature inside the element (b: exact
  even with one layer of hex20), not the element average of the manual (KNOWN_BUGS 17).
  In (d) the midside nodes added by `LINE_QUAD` get the interpolated temperature; with
  the reference temperature the beam would be wrong. Mecway itself solves (d) with
  incompatible hex8: −6.28e-4 m (+4.7 %). Also compared headless (8 hex8, Node
  temperature +50 K, `LINE_QUAD`): modal with prestress 284.96 Hz against Mecway
  286.18 Hz, buckling 3.3969 against 3.4010; in the bridge and in Mecway `temperature2`
  and `temperature3` give the same result (also static, nonlinear static and D30).
### LS-60 – LS-63 Element offset (shells and beams)

- **Goal**: Mecway's *Element offset* (`<offset>` on an element selection,
  `bridge_offset.py`). Shell: mid-surface at distance w along +W (element normal);
  DKT/DST get `EXCENTREMENT` (+ `INER_ROTA`), COQUE_3D has no offset (`CALCULEL2_31`)
  and is placed on its own nodes with a rigid arm (`LIAISON_DDL`) to each Mecway node.
  Beam: axis at (x, y, z) global from the nodes, also with rigid arms.
- **Model**: (60a/b) clamped strip 200 × 20 × 2 mm, 20 × 4 quad4 (a, DKT) or quad8 (b,
  COQUE_3D), w = 10 mm, 100 N tension (+X) on the nodes of the free edge. (61) two DKT
  layers on the same nodes, w = +10 and −10 mm, 100 N tip load. (62) T-beam: flange DKT
  1000 × 50 × 5 mm, web RectBar 5 × 100 mm on the centre line with offset z = −52.5 mm,
  100 N tip load. (63) composite beam as Mecway's sample *CompositeBeam* (manual §11.2):
  web RectBar 0.2 × 0.01 m (E = 5.2 GPa), flange 6 × 2 quad8 (t = 0.01 m, E = 2.6 GPa)
  with w = 0.105 m, torque 6 N m.
- **Reference**: (60) uniform curvature P e/(EI): tip w = P e L²/(2EI) = 7.143 mm,
  σ = P/(BT) ± 6Pe/(BT²) = 77.5 / 72.5 MPa; (61) I = 2(BT³/12 + BTe²) (parallel axis),
  w = PL³/(3EI) = 0.1582 mm; (62) T-section (parallel axis), 0.18108 mm; (63) angle
  ML/(J(G_f + G_w)) = 0.108 rad (manual), rotation about the shear centre. Plus Mecway 34
  (own solver, headless) on each model; reactions: equilibrium.
- **Result**: (60a) 7.085 mm (Mecway 7.090, −0.07 %), (60b) 7.097 mm (Mecway 7.096,
  +0.02 %), rotation within 0.2 % of Mecway, von Mises bottom/top 77.50 / 72.50 MPa;
  (61) 0.15657 mm (parallel axis −1.0 %, Mecway 0.16090: −2.7 %); (62) 0.18100 mm
  (parallel axis −0.05 %, Mecway 0.18490: −2.1 %); (63) 0.10918 rad (Mecway 0.10913)
  and lateral displacement 11.407 mm (Mecway 11.405); sum of the reactions equals the
  load everywhere (moment about the X axis in 63: 6.000 N m).
- **Interpretation**: the sign of w and the rigid arm agree with Mecway; the differences
  in 61/62 are in the plate (Mecway's MITC4 slightly softer), the bridge is closer to the
  parallel-axis value there. With rigid arms the nodes carry the reaction (the forces in
  the arm itself cancel). With a *Fixed support* on a node selection Mecway computes
  model 63 with a finer mesh differently from the faces (KNOWN_BUGS 108c); the bridge
  gives the same in both. Without `POSTTRAITEMENTS='FORCE'` CompositeBeam stopped with
  `FACTOR_57` (error bound 5e-6); the bridge switches that refinement on whenever there
  are rigid arms.

### LS-64 – LS-67 Element offset: combinations

- **Goal**: element offset together with other input (`tests_offset.py`). (64) two
  shell elsets that are each partly offset: each elset gets its own rest group without
  `EXCENTREMENT` (before, the second elset got the same group name and the first no
  `COQUE`: `CALCULEL_6`). (65) the extra result fields of offset quad8 shells (cells on
  their own nodes, mapped back to the Mecway elements via the `BROFL` node groups;
  before, they were silently missing). (66) bonded contact with an offset quad8 strip as
  master (before, a false `FACTOR_11`). (67) a load on the edge face of an offset shell.
- **Model**: strip 200 × 20 × 2 mm as LS-60. (64) two elsets of 20 × 2 quad4 (DKT) across
  the width, w = 10 mm on the elements with x < L/2, 100 N tension on the nodes of the
  free edge. (65) LS-60b (quad8) with `shell_stresses`, `shell_forces`, `strain` and
  `safety_factor` (S = 250 MPa). (66) LS-07a with quad8, w = 1 mm on part A (master),
  1 N end load. (67) LS-60a with the 100 N as a load on the edge face.
- **Reference**: (64) curvature P e/(EI) only over x < L/2: tip 3 P e L²/(8EI) =
  5.357 mm; σ at L/4 as LS-60, at 3L/4 only P/(BT) = 2.5 MPa. (65) at x = L/2:
  σxx bottom/top 77.5 / −72.5 MPa, N_u = P/B = 5000 N/m, εxx bottom = σ/E, factor of
  safety S/σ_vM = 3.226; each of the 80 elements has a value. (66) P L³/(3EI) =
  0.95238 mm; Mecway does not solve the bonded model (LS-07a), hence the continuous
  strip (0.94148 mm). (67) Mecway 34: tip 0 (the load acts at the offset edge). Also
  Mecway 34 headless on 64.
- **Result**: (64) 5.268 mm (−1.7 %; Mecway 5.277 mm, −0.16 %), σ 77.19 MPa (L/4) and
  2.500 MPa (3L/4). (65) all 80 elements, values equal to the theory within 1e-5, also
  the nodal values. (66) 0.94266 mm (−1.0 % vs Euler, +0.13 % vs Mecway continuous).
  (67) 7.085 mm against 0 in Mecway: **KNOWN** (KNOWN_BUGS 108).
- **Interpretation**: (67) the bridge applies a load on a face selection to the edge
  cell on the Mecway nodes (`FORCE_ARETE`, also with `EXCENTREMENT`), Mecway at the
  offset edge; the difference is a moment w × F per length (WARNING). A load on a node
  selection acts at the nodes in both (LS-60). For bonded contact on offset shells the
  bridge reports which surface it ties to (KNOWN_BUGS 108).

### LS-20 Configurations (load cases G, Q and ULS)

- **Goal**: the setting *Configuration* (`configuration`) chooses which configuration
  the bridge solves; `auto` follows the rule (the only one, the only one without
  suppressed items, else the first).
- **Model**: bar 1 × 0.1 × 0.1 m, 4 × 1 × 1 hex20, steel (ρ = 7850 kg/m³), symmetry
  planes x = 0, y = 0, z = 0. Configurations G (gravity 9.81 m/s² along x), Q (traction
  1 MPa on the end face) and ULS (own load items 1.2 g and 1.5 q, as in Mecway: a
  configuration has no factor); each load item is suppressed in the other two
  (`<suppressed configuration=".."/>`).
- **Reference**: end displacement u = ρgL²/(2E) + qL/E (uniaxial) and reaction force
  ρgAL + qA (equilibrium), with the factors of the chosen configuration. Also the
  bridge's check line ([UserGuide](UserGuide.md) sec. 4.1): mass ρAL = 78.5 kg and sum
  of `REAC_NODA` in X = −(ρgAL + qA).
- **Result**: LS-20a/b/c (G, Q, ULS chosen): reaction force exact, u ≤ 0.06% off (with
  G, u = 0 at x = 0 slightly restrains the lateral contraction). ULS = 1.2 G + 1.5 Q to
  4.6e-16 relative (all nodes, recomputed from the three runs). LS-20d (`auto`, every
  configuration has suppressed items): G with a WARNING. LS-20e (copy with only ULS,
  setting Q that is not in the model): a WARNING about that, then ULS; no WARNING about
  an ambiguous choice any more. With Mecway's IronPython:
  `pick_configuration` + `splice_results` put the results into the solution of the
  chosen configuration and keep those of the others.

### LS-50 Cantilever with line3 elements

- **Purpose**: quadratic beam elements (line3). Code-Aster has no quadratic beam element;
  the bridge splits each line3 element at its midside node into two SEG2 cells
  (`POU_D_E`), with the orientation per half (KNOWN_BUGS 107). Module `tests_balken.py`.
- **Model**: L = 1 m, 5 line3 elements along X, rectangular bar (RectBar); (a, b)
  20 × 20 mm, (c) 20 × 10 mm with orientation vector (0, 0, 1) per element (V = Z).
- **Constraints and load**: fixed at x = 0; (a) P = 100 N in −Z at the tip; (b) line
  pressure q = 100 N/m in −Z on an element selection with all elements (`FORCE_POUTRE`);
  (c) P = 100 N in −Y at the tip (bending about V, I = a b³/12).
- **Reference**: Euler beam (Roark table 8.1 cases 1a and 2a): δ = PL³/(3EI),
  φ = PL²/(2EI), w(x) at the midside node x = 0.1 m, δ = qL⁴/(8EI); reaction =
  equilibrium. Also Mecway 34 headless: (1) the same beam with 10 line2 elements on the
  same nodes in Mecway's own solver (which has no line3 beam; tolerance 0.1 %) and (2) the
  line3 model with CCX (B32R; tolerance 4 %, see interpretation).
- **Result**: all quantities exact (< 1e-6) against Euler and against Mecway with line2;
  the midside nodes too. Mecway/CCX: −2.3 % (a), −3.0 % (b), −3.2 % (c).
- **Interpretation**: two Euler cells per element are exact for an end load and a uniform
  load; the midside node gets ordinary results. The orientation vector works per half
  (without it, (c) would be four times as stiff). CCX expands the B32R into one C3D20R per
  element (one element across the section) and is therefore 2–3 % stiffer than theory;
  that is a property of CCX. Mecway/CCX cannot solve a round bar (Mecway writes
  `SECTION=PIPE` with inner radius 0, CCX: "nonpositive jacobian"), hence a rectangle.

### LS-51 Flexible joint on beam

- **Purpose**: Mecway's *Flexible joint on beam* (`<flexiblejoint><rotu/><rotv/><rotw/>`,
  manual v33 sec. 9.12): the end of a beam element rotates freely about the chosen local
  axes. Translation: an own node for that end, coupled to the Mecway node with
  `LIAISON_DDL` (translations and the rotations that are not free, in the local axes as in
  Mecway: U along the beam, V from the orientation, W = U × V).
- **Model**: L = 1 m, Ø 20 mm, 10 line2 (e: 4 line3), both ends fixed, P = 100 N at
  mid-span; joint on the end face of the element right of the middle. (a) beam along X,
  load −Y, joint about W (= Z); (b) the same, joint about V (= Y); (c) joint about W on
  both sides of the middle node; (d) joint about U, V and W on the fixed end face at
  x = L; (e) as (a) with line3 elements; (f) beam along Z, load +X, joint about V
  (Mecway: V = Y for a vertical beam; bending about Y).
- **Reference**: (a, c, e, f) two cantilevers L/2 with P/2 at the tip: δ = PL³/(48EI),
  rotation left of the hinge PL²/(16EI); (b) no effect, δ = PL³/(192EI) (Roark 1d);
  (d) fixed-pinned: δ = 7PL³/(768EI), moment at the fixed end 3PL/16 (Roark 1c). Also
  Mecway 34 (own solver) on the same model; (c) middle node: rotation 0, as in Mecway.
  Mecway does not solve (e) (line3 only with CCX, the joint not with CCX).
- **Result**: all checks exact (< 1e-9) against theory and against Mecway. A modal
  analysis of (a) with density also gives the same frequencies as Mecway
  (57.887 / 92.089 / 253.905 Hz); Code-Aster solves model (c) modally, Mecway does not.
- **Interpretation**: the local axes and the choice of free rotations agree with Mecway;
  (b) and (f) would fail with swapped axes. If the node has no other element with
  rotations (c, d), it gets a POI1 `DIS_TR` without stiffness and the remaining
  rotation(s) set to 0 (unless a constraint fixes them, d). A fixed constraint on an
  *element selection* containing the element with the joint stops with `FACTOR_11`
  (KNOWN_BUGS 106); put it on nodes or faces.

### LS-52, LS-53 Flexible joint with element offset and section forces per element

- **Purpose**: (52) flexible joint together with an element offset (`tests_balken.py`).
  Earlier a joint on an offset element got an own node that was in no cell, and a node
  with only an offset beam and a beam with a joint had no degrees of freedom (both
  `CHARGES2_31`); after that a joint on an offset element was not translated (rigidly
  attached to the arm, KNOWN_BUGS 108d, fixed). (53) the section forces per element
  (`beam_forces` as element values) next to a joint and on line3 beams; earlier the
  element with the joint and every line3 beam were silently missing.
- **Model**: LS-51a (joint about W right of the middle) with an offset z = e = 10 mm
  (global, perpendicular to the bending plane) on (52a) the right half, so also on the
  element with the joint, or (52b) the left half; (52c) as 52a with U, V and W free
  (ball joint). (53a) LS-51a, (53b) LS-51e (4 line3), with `beam_forces`.
- **Translation**: the joint sits at the end of the rigid arm, between the offset point and
  the element, as in Mecway 34: Mecway node → rigid arm → joint → element. An own node at
  the offset point with the joint relations (local axes of the beam) replaces the offset
  point in the cell of the element; the arm is rigidly attached to the Mecway node. (The
  fork by MishalTPC, `github.com/mishal0488/MecwayAsterBridge`, commit 773d59f, puts the
  joint at the Mecway node, before the arm; with a rotation perpendicular to the arm free
  that gives a different distribution of the torsion than Mecway.)
- **Reference**: (52a, 52b) hinge: δ = PL³/(48EI) = 1.2631 mm; Mecway 34 (own solver)
  gives 1.2636 mm in both, and rotx at the middle −9.8486e-5 rad, at x = 0.8 m
  −3.9394e-5 rad. (52c) where the joint sits shows in the torsion: the right half passes
  the force F2 through the ball joint at the end of the arm; the arm is rigidly attached to
  the middle node, so the torque T = e F2 goes to the left half and the right half carries
  no torsion (rotx at x = 0.8 m 0). Each half is a clamped beam L/2 with
  k = 3EI/(L/2)³ (`POU_D_E`); the torsion moves the point of application by e θx:
  F1 = F2 (1 + a), a = e² k (L/2)/(GJ), δ = F1/k = 1.2641 mm, rotx = −T x/(GJ): at the
  middle −1.9690e-4 rad, at x = 0.2 m −7.8758e-5 rad. Mecway 34 (own solver, headless)
  gives the same deflection and the same rotations for (52c). (53) each half is a
  clamped beam L/2 with P/2: M = 0 in the hinge, (P/2) h further along the element,
  V = P/2.
- **Result**: (52a) 1.2636 mm (+0.04 %, equal to Mecway), rotx at the middle and at 0.8 m
  +0.00 % against Mecway; (52b) likewise; (52c) δ +0.00 % (also against Mecway), rotx at
  the middle and at 0.2 m +0.00 % (also against Mecway), rotx at 0.8 m −7e-33 rad;
  reactions = load. rotz at the middle and at 0.2 and 0.8 m is also equal to Mecway
  (~1e-12 relative, not as a check). (53a) all 10 elements, M in the hinge 2e-13 N m, 5.000 N m at
  x = 0.6 m, V = 50 N; (53b) all 4 elements with three values: 0 / 6.25 / 12.5 N m.
- **Interpretation**: a joint on an offset element works as in Mecway, also with a
  rotation perpendicular to the arm free (52c): deflection and rotations equal. An offset
  line3 element with a joint is not translated yet (WARNING; the element is then rigidly
  attached to the arm). A line3 beam gets its element values from both SEG2 halves
  (end 1 and middle from the first, end 2 from the second).

### LS-54 Bar with one line3 truss element

- **Goal**: quadratic truss (line3, `truss="true"`, GitHub issue #6; `tests_balken.py`).
  Before, Code-Aster stopped with `MODELE1_6` (`BARRE` on SEG3).
- **Model**: one line3 element along X, L = 1 m, round bar D = 10 mm; root fixed, tip
  uy = uz = 0, F = 1000 N along X at the tip. The bridge splits the element into two SEG2
  cells (`BARRE`) and couples the midside node across the chord to the end nodes
  (`LIAISON_DDL`); without that coupling the midside node is a mechanism (`ALGELINE3_81`).
- **Reference**: u(x) = F x / (E A).
- **Result**: tip and midside node exact (< 1e-6); transverse displacement of the midside
  node 0.

### LS-54b, LS-54c Line3 cable (*Tension only*)

- **Goal**: quadratic element with material *Tension only* (`MODELISATION='CABLE'`), also
  split into two SEG2 cells with the midside node coupled across the chord (GitHub issue #6;
  `tests_balken.py`).
- **Model**: (54b) as LS-54a with *Tension only* (E = 210 GPa, D = 10 mm); (54c) two line3
  cables in series along X (1 m each), both ends fixed, F = 1000 N along +X at the node in
  between: the left cable is in tension, the right one would be in compression. Nonlinear
  static (`CABLE` only with `GROT_GDEP`).
- **Reference**: (54b) u(x) = F x / (E A); (54c) only the tension cable carries: u = F L/(E A),
  reaction left −F, right 0 (Code-Aster keeps a compressive stiffness `EC_SUR_E` = 1e-4 E, so
  0.1 N). Mecway 34 gave no solution headless for *Tension only*, not on line2 either (own
  solver *Static 3D* and *Nonlinear static*: empty solution; CCX: Mecway stopped), while the
  same line2 bar as an *Isotropic* truss did solve: hand calculation only.
- **Result**: (54b) tip −7.5e-5 relative (Green-Lagrange strain of `GROT_GDEP`, O(ε)),
  reaction exact; (54c) u −1.8e-4, reaction left −999.9 N, right −0.09997 N (= 1e-4 F).

### Extra result fields (LS-30 .. LS-32, NL-30 .. NL-32, MO-30, DY-30)

Fields `beam_forces`, `shell_stresses`, `shell_forces`, `strain`, `safety_factor` and
`contact` ([`UserGuide.md`](UserGuide.md) §4.1), tested under Mecway's own variable
names (`TestFiles/Verificatie/tests_resultaatvelden.py`). The names, axes and signs
were first checked headless in Mecway 34 on the same models; the comparison with
Mecway's solution follows the table.

| Case | Model | Checked | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LS-30 / LS-30b | cantilever 1 m (4 POU_D_E, 20 x 30 mm), tip force N 1000 / V_v 200 / V_w -300 N and T 50 N.m; straight and at 30° | `tensileforce`, `sheary`, `shearz`, `torsion`, `bendingmomv` (root), `bendingmomw` (middle) | statics | all exact (≤ 1e-12); Mecway headless: same values and signs |
| LS-31 | shell strip 100 x 20 x 2 mm skewed in space (quad4, DKT), tension 600 N + transverse 3 N | `forceperlengthu`, `momentu`, `stress<ij>u1/m1/b1` (global), `lstrain<ij>u/b`, `factorofsafety` at x = L/2 | plate theory, σ = N/t ± 6M/t², uniaxial e1 e1^T | ≤ 0.8% (lstrainxxb), the rest ≤ 0.2% |
| LS-31b | same, quad8 (COQUE_3D) | same + `shearuw` (mean over the width) | -P/b = -150 N/m | ≤ 0.6%; Q exact |
| LS-31c | same, quad4 with `orientationu*` along the width | `forceperlengthv` = N_u, `momentv`, `forceperlengthu` ≈ 0 | same | exact |
| LS-31d | same with the U axis from an *Element orientation* card | as LS-31c | same | exact, equal to LS-31c |
| LS-32 / LS-32b | hex8 bar uniaxial tension 100 MPa; cube pure shear 50 MPa | `lstrainxx/yy/zz`, `lstrainxy` (γ), `factorofsafety` | σ/E, -νσ/E, τ/G; S/σ, S/(√3 τ) | exact |
| NL-30 / NL-30b | contact patch test hex8 matching (STANDARD, `CONT_NOEU`) and hex20 (PENALISATION, `LAGS_C` only) | `contactpressure` (mean, smallest), `contactstate` | p = F/A = 10 MPa, state 1 | exact |
| NL-30c | two beams with their tips touching (NL-03b) | `contactstate` tip 1, start of the slave zone 0; `contactpressure` there | open: 0 | state exact; pressure 448 Pa (0.06% of the peak, numerical) |
| NL-31 | hex20 bar, 5% imposed strain | `greenlagrangestrainxx` | (λ² - 1)/2 = 0.05125 | exact |
| NL-32 | model of LS-30 in STAT_NON_LINE | as LS-30 | statics | exact |
| MO-30 | cantilever (20 POU_D_E), mode 1 | M(0) / w(L) (magnitude) | E I λ1² / L² (Blevins table 8-1) | exact (14767) |
| DY-30 | beam of LS-30, load built up in 2 s (DYNA_NON_LINE) | `bendingmomv` root at t = 2.5 s | statics 300 N.m | +0.05% |

Comparison with Mecway (headless, Mecway 34, same models, internal solver; contact with
CCX):

- beams (LS-30, LS-30b): all six section forces equal at every node (≤ 1e-11);
- shell strip in the XY plane at 30° and skewed in space (= LS-31, quad4 and quad8):
  membrane forces equal (< 1e-6), at x = L/2 global stresses and strains 0.0 .. 0.2%
  apart, moments < 1%; near the clamp up to ~15% (DKT/COQUE_3D against MITC4/MITC8,
  see KNOWN_BUGS 2); the shear force varies over the width in both (Mecway quad8
  -253 / -150 / -47 N/m, the bridge -247 / -150 / -53);
- total strain bar (S30): `lstrainxx` 4.7619e-4 in both; simple shear: Mecway's
  `lstrainzx` = τ/G, so γ (the bridge computes EPXY x 2);
- contact patch test (CCX): `contactpressure` 9.99974 MPa (Mecway) against 10.000 MPa;
  `greenlagrangestrain*` equal (zz -4.76e-5);
- `factorofsafety`: Mecway does not write it headless (it computes it when displaying);
  a `factorofsafety` written by the bridge is shown by Mecway as the item *failure*.

Mecway reads the spliced results (IronPython `bridge_results_liml.splice_results`, then
headless `mw.open` and `solution.node_value`) and itself creates the items *tensile
force*, *shear force*, *bending moment*, *torsion moment*, *stress*, *strain*, *force
per length*, *moment per length*, *failure*, *contact stress* and *contact state*.
Route `tabel` (LS-30, LS-32, NL-31 with a fake solution in CPython): `beam_forces` and
`strain` (also Green-Lagrange) equal to route `liml`.
### LS-40 .. LS-44 Formulas in x, y, z

- **Purpose**: Mecway formulas in x, y, z (KNOWN_BUGS 45, solved; limits KNOWN_BUGS 104)
  for pressure, traction, force, line pressure and prescribed displacement/rotation →
  `FORMULE` of X, Y, Z in `AFFE_CHAR_MECA_F`. Module `tests_formules.py`.
- **Model**: block 1 × 2 × 0.1 m (hex8 4 × 8 × 1), bottom `fixed`; cantilever beam
  (10 line2, Ø 20 mm, L = 1 m); plate 1 × 2 m, t = 10 mm (quad4 4 × 8), edge y = 0
  clamped.
- **Load**: (40a) pressure `3*y^2` kPa on the top (position in m); (40b) the same as
  `3e-6*y^2` with position in mm; (41) pressure `3*y^2` on the half y < 1, `10*x` on
  the half y > 1 and traction X = `2*x+1` kPa, three function loads in one run; (42a)
  prescribed displacement Z = `0.001*x+0.002*y` mm on the top; (42b) the same along
  (1, 1, 0) = `0.001*(1+x)` mm (`LIAISON_OBLIQUE`); (42c) prescribed rotation about Y =
  `0.001*(1+x)` rad at the beam tip; (43a) line pressure Z = `-1000*x`
  N/m on the beam; (43b) the same with gravity (ρ = 7850 kg/m³); (44a) pressure
  `3*y^2` Pa on the plate (DKT), (44b) the same with `COQUE_3D`.
- **Reference**: the integral of the formula: 40: reaction ∫3y² dA = 8 kN, moment about
  X ∫3y³ dA = 12 kN·m, about Y −4 kN·m; 41: 1 + 5 = 6 kN, 0.75 + 7.5 = 8.25 kN·m,
  reaction X −4 kN; 42: the formula at the nodes (42c: θ = 0.002 rad, pure moment w = θL/2); 43: reaction q₀L/2 (+ ρAgL), tip
  deflection 11q₀L⁴/(120EI) (+ ρAgL⁴/(8EI)) (Roark table 8.1); 44: reaction 8 N and
  Mecway 34 (internal solver) for the deflection of the free edge.
- **Result**: 40a/b, 41, 42, 43 exact (≤ 1e-6). 44a: reaction 8.0625 N (+0.78%,
  tolerance 1%), deflection +1.3% against Mecway (tolerance 2%); 44b: reaction exact,
  deflection +0.63% (tolerance 1%; with a constant pressure shells are also ~0.7% from
  Mecway, KNOWN_BUGS 2).
- **Interpretation**: Code-Aster integrates the formula on solid faces, `COQUE_3D` and
  beams at the integration points (exact for these polynomials); DKT takes a function at
  the nodes (trapezoidal rule: +0.78% on this coarse mesh, O(h²)). Mecway 34 (headless,
  `mw_solve.py`) gives the same reactions and moments for 40 and 41 (8000 N,
  12000 N·m; 6000 N, 8250 N·m), also with position in mm. Mecway 34 does not apply
  force, line pressure or prescribed displacement/rotation with x, y, z in the formula
  (reactions 0, internal solver and CCX); the bridge does, with a WARNING. Pressure on
  shells goes through `FORCE_COQUE PRES` (`PRES_REP` with a function stops on DKT and
  COQUE_3D with `ELEMENTS4_92`). Three `AFFE_CHAR_MECA_F` loads, each with its own
  `FORMULE`, in one `EXCIT` each use their own function (unlike thermal `SOURCE`, [Thermal.md](Thermal.md) §3); two
  functions in one `DDL_IMPO` load (test run with two selections) as well.

### LS-70 … LS-78 Beam orientation (static)

Cantilever and frame with and without a Mecway orientation (*orientation vector* and
*angle*); explanation, model and results in §7, *Beam orientation*. All tip
displacements and σ_xx max/min at the clamp ≤ 1e-7 against Euler-Bernoulli, the frame
equal to Mecway (≤ 3e-8).

### Result formulas (FX-01 .. FX-05)

Mecway's result formulas (*Solution > Formula*, STAPPENPLAN S33, GitHub issue #8): the
bridge evaluates them in the job (`bridge_formula`, `aster_results_export._formula_vars`).
References: the analytical solution, converted to the units of the formula.

- **FX-01** (TH-01a, wall with heat generation): `T` in °F at x = L/2 (ref
  (T0 + qL²/8k)·1.8 − 459.67 = 125.33 °F), `qf.x` in Btu/h/ft² at x = L/4 (−7925.0), the
  formula of issue #8 `qf.x/3600/(12^2)` (−0.015287 Btu/s/in²) and
  `sqrt(qf.x^2+qf.y^2)*x` (W/m², x in mm; 6.25e5). Result: all within 1e-6 (the TH-01a
  values are exact). **Mecway 34 itself** (headless, same model): the four formulas equal to
  the bridge within 4e-14 relative, also on the nodes; in the Mecway GUI the formulas on the
  bridge result are valid and show the same contour.
- **FX-02** (bar in tension, P = 1 MPa, hex20): `s.xx` in MPa (1), `u.x` in mm at the end
  (PL/E = 0.0047619), the sum of `FR.x` in kN over the fixed face (−PA = −10), `e.xx·1e6`
  (P/E = 4.7619, with `strain` in *fields*) and `x` in mm (1000); with the fields
  `principal_stresses` / `principal_strains` (S34) also `principal1` = P, `principal3` = 0,
  `tresca` = P, `lprincipalstrain1` = P/E, `lprincipalstrain3` = −νP/E and the formulas `s1`,
  `tr` (MPa) and `e1·1e6`. Result: within 1e-9. On a cantilever (LS-02, not uniform) the
  bridge's principal values per element node equal the eigenvalues of its own element-node
  tensor and the node values the mean of the element-node values (within 1e-16 relative), the
  rule measured in Mecway 34; Mecway recognises the fields (*principal stress*, *Tresca
  stress*, *principal strain*) when it opens and saves the result.
- **FX-03** (TH-02a, transient): `t` in min at 10 s and 40 s (0.16667, 0.66667) and
  `(T−300)/max(t,1)` at the surface (2.5 K/s at 40 s). Result: within 1e-7.
- **FX-04** (principal values of shells, S34b; shell strip of LS-31, quad4, skew in space,
  tension 600 N and transverse force 10 N): at x = L/2 top σ = N/t + 6M/t² = 52.5 MPa,
  middle 15 MPa, bottom −22.5 MPa (uniaxial): `principal1u1`, `principal1m1`,
  `principal3b1`, `trescau1`, `trescab1` within 0.05%, `principal2u1` ≈ 0 (0.04% of σ),
  `lprincipalstrain1u1` = σ/E, `lprincipalstrain3u1` = −νσ/E (bottom the other way round)
  within 0.22%; without the field `strain` (the bridge requests `EPSI_ELNO` of the shells
  itself). Mecway's rule (eigenvalues of the global tensor `stress<ij>u1` per element node,
  node = mean) on the bridge's own stresses: within 3e-16 relative. **Mecway 34 itself**
  (headless, internal, same model): the same rule within 3e-13; at x = L/2 the dominant
  principal values within 0.05% of the bridge, `tresca` and the values ≈ 0 up to 1% of the
  maximum apart, because Mecway's shell (MITC4) has transverse shear stress at the top and
  bottom and DKT does not; Mecway recognises the bridge's fields (*principal stress*,
  *Tresca stress*, *principal strain*, `solution.node_value("principal1u1")`).
- **FX-05** (Green-Lagrange, S34b; bar of NL-31, hex20, 5% imposed strain, L30):
  `greenlagrangeprincipalstrain1` and the formulas `GLe1`, `e1` and `e.xx` = (λ² − 1)/2 =
  0.05125. Result: within 1e-6. Mecway 34 (CCX, headless) on the same model: 0.051248, and
  it rewrites `e1` to `GLe1`.

Measured during the build (not in the regression): `sqrt(u.x^2+u.y^2+u.z^2)/x` leaves out
the 9 nodes at x = 0, as Mecway does (180 of 189 nodes); a formula with `t` in a static
analysis and a formula with `e.xx` without the field `strain` are left out with a warning.

### 2D and axisymmetric (PL-01 … PL-12)

Mecway's *Static 2D* (S20, `C_PLAN`/`D_PLAN`), *Static Axisymmetric* (SA0, `AXIS`) and
their *Dynamic Response* (D20, DA0; `dyna_impl_3d` with NEWMARK, PETIT and one step per
Mecway step) (STAPPENPLAN S16a, S16b, `bridge_plane`, [`2D_and_axisymmetric.md`](2D_and_axisymmetric.md)).
The 3D counterparts AD-01 (Kt) and LS-04 (Lamé) give the references and the tolerances;
"= Mecway" is Mecway 34's own S20/SA0/D20/DA0 solution of the same model (headless,
tolerance 1e-6).

- **PL-01** (plate with a hole, plane stress, t = 1 mm, quad8, the geometry of AD-01,
  symmetry x = 0 and y = 0, traction 100 MPa): Kt_n against Peterson (2.519) −27.7 /
  −14.9 / −6.8 / −2.6 / −0.79% for n = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 (AD-01 hex20: −27.2 / −14.3 / −6.3 /
  −2.5 / −0.75%), tolerances as AD-01 (2% for n = 16). Σ R_x on x = 0 = −σ·W/2·t = −5000 N
  exact (the thickness factor). n = 16: u_y(0, r) and u_x(L/2, 0) = Mecway (≤ 1e-11).
- **PL-02** (the same plate in plane strain, n = 16): Kt −0.75%; σ_zz = ν(σ_xx + σ_yy) at the
  hole within 1e-6; Σ R_x exact; displacements = Mecway.
- **PL-03** (thick-walled cylinder, plane strain, quarter ring quad8 n × 2n, a = 0.1 m,
  b = 0.2 m, 100 MPa): n = 8: u_r −0.0003%, σ_θ −0.18%, σ_z +0.42%: the same as LS-04
  (hex20); tolerances as LS-04. Mecway: see KB125 (pressure on curved edges).
- **PL-04** (the same cylinder axisymmetric: ring of 10 mm with Y fixed on both ends,
  quad8 n × 1): n = 8: u_r −0.0004%, hoop stress (`stresszz`) −0.41%, σ_r (`stressxx`)
  +0.97% (−p at the loaded wall, extrapolated from the integration points; it converges as
  h^1.8: 27.7 / 10.5 / 3.4 / 0.97%), σ_z (`stressyy`) +0.42%; tolerances σ_θ 0.6% (p = 1.5),
  σ_r 1.2% (p = 2). Σ R_y over the top = σ_z·π(b² − a²) exact (360 degrees). u_r = Mecway.
- **PL-05** (strip 100 × 20 mm, plane stress, t = 10 mm): nodal force, force on an edge,
  traction and gravity together: Σ R_x = −501.53 N exact, mass ρLHt exact, `principal1`
  +0.009% against F/(Ht) + ρgL/2, `tresca` = `principal1` − `principal3`; u_x = Mecway.
- **PL-06** (ring r = 0.1 … 0.2 m, h = 20 mm, axisymmetric): nodal force, force on an edge,
  radial traction, gravity along Y and centrifugal force about Y: Σ R_y = −3144.23 N exact,
  mass ρπ(b² − a²)h exact; u_r and u_y = Mecway.

- **PL-10** (Dynamic Response 2D, strip 1 m × 50 mm, plane stress, ν = 0, quad8 40 × 1,
  step traction 10 MPa, Newmark step T/200): period of the tip 4L/c = 7.709e-4 s −0.06%,
  time mean = static PL/E +0.002%; history = Mecway (3.2e-8).
- **PL-11** (Dynamic Response Axisymmetric, the ring of PL-04-n4 with density, step
  pressure, Rayleigh α = 8e4 rad/s): u_r after 0.3 ms −0.05% against Lamé (still
  settling; tolerance 0.2%); history = Mecway (3.1e-8).
- **PL-12** (Dynamic Response 2D, cantilever 100 × 10 mm, nodal force as a time table
  0 → −100 N in 1 ms, every 5th step written, "Solver steps per Mecway time step" = 1):
  history = Mecway (1.6e-8). PL-11 and PL-12 have *Generate solution every n time steps* >
  1 (n = 10 and 5); the bridge computes with Mecway's step. Before that fix it computed one
  step per output interval: history 2.7% (PL-11) and 4.6% (PL-12) off Mecway.

Measured during the build (not in the regression): tri3, tri6, quad4 and quad8 (cantilever in
plane stress) and eleven single load cases in S20/SA0 give the same displacements and reactions as
Mecway (≤ 1e-11); nodal stresses are equal where they are uniform or linear in the element,
elsewhere they differ by the extrapolation (KB3). After opening a bridge result Mecway shows
the items *displacement*, *stress*, *von Mises stress*, *reaction force*, *principal stress* and
*Tresca stress* and reads the values with `solution.node_value`.

### Conclusion linear static

All 49 cases (122 checks) are within tolerance. Where the theory is exact (Navier, Lamé,
LE1, Euler beam), the translated models converge with the expected order to the
reference; in LS-02 the difference with Timoshenko is a model error, the same in Mecway
and CCX. Verified: `traction` on solid faces, `surfacepressure` on solid, shell and
shell-edge faces, `fixed`, `displacement`, `noderotation`, `symmetry` (solid faces and
shell edges), `frictionless`, `force`, springs, the element choices hex8
`3D`/`LINE_QUAD`, hex20, DKT, `COQUE_3D` and `POU_D_E`, and the result fields
`displ*`, `stress*` (`SIGM_NOEU`), `vonmisesu1/m/b` and `reactionforce*`.

### Error estimator (FS)

Mecway S30 → `lin_static_3d` with `error_estimator` on (default) → `CALC_ERREUR`
`ERME` ([Error_estimator.md](Error_estimator.md)). Module `tests_foutschatter.py`, run
with `python run_verificatie.py FS`. The global estimate comes from the block
Code-Aster writes in the `.mess` (`verif_lib.error_summary`), the field `errorestimate`
from the result file. The order in a series is p = ln(η_coarse / η_fine) / ln 2 of
the absolute global estimate η between two successive meshes (h halved).

#### FS-01 Cantilever, hex8 (model of LS-02-h8)

- **Purpose**: does the estimate decrease with the expected order on refinement, does
  it follow the true error, and is the largest error where you expect it?
- **Model**: LS-02 (1 x 0.1 x 0.1 m, traction P = 10 kN on the end face, clamped end
  face fixed), hex8 `3D`, n = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 elements over the height.
- **Reference**: (1) deflection: Timoshenko beam (Young & Budynas, 2002, table 8.1
  case 1a, plus shear), as LS-02. (2) True relative error in the energy norm: with a
  uniform traction the external work is P·w (w = mean deflection of the end face), so
  ‖u − u_h‖²_E = P (w_∞ − w_h) (Galerkin orthogonality) and the relative error is
  sqrt((w_∞ − w_h)/w_∞). w_∞ = 0.001906 m is the limit of the 3D model (Richardson on
  LS-02-h20: 0.0019057 m; on LS-02-h8 n = 4…16: 0.0019067 m). (3) Linear elements:
  energy error O(h), order 1 (Verfürth, 1996). (4) The error is largest at the
  singular edges of the clamped end.
- **Checks**: deflection (tolerance as LS-02); effectivity index = estimated / true
  relative error, expected O(1) and roughly constant (Ainsworth & Oden, 2000):
  2.0 ± 1.0; order of η 1.0 ± 0.2 (from n = 4); x of the node with the largest
  `errorestimate` at x = 0 (± one element).
- **Result**:

| n | nodes | global error (estimate) | η | order p | true error | effectivity | deflection vs w_∞ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 44 | 79.5% | 2.33e6 | – | 59.4% | 1.34 | −35.3% |
| 2 | 189 | 61.1% | 1.51e6 | (0.62) | 35.3% | 1.73 | −12.5% |
| 4 | 1025 | 37.9% | 8.22e5 | 0.88 | 18.9% | 2.01 | −3.6% |
| 8 | 6561 | 20.6% | 4.24e5 | 0.96 | 9.7% | 2.11 | −0.95% |
| 16 | 46529 | 10.7% | 2.17e5 | 0.96 | 4.8% | 2.22 | −0.23% |

  On every mesh the largest error is at the clamped end (x = 0).
- **Interpretation**: the global error decreases monotonically; η converges with order
  0.88 → 0.96, so towards 1 as expected for linear elements (n = 1 → 2 is not yet
  asymptotic). The relative error itself (η / sqrt(η² + ‖σ‖²)) has a lower order on the
  coarse meshes (0.69 between n = 2 and 4) because it saturates towards 100%; from
  n = 4 on 0.88 and 0.94. The effectivity index is O(1) and rises only slowly
  (1.3 → 2.2): the estimator is a reliable, slightly pessimistic measure of the energy
  error. The deflection error is much smaller than the energy error (roughly its
  square).

#### FS-02 Thick-walled cylinder, hex20 (model of LS-04)

- **Purpose**: order with quadratic elements and a smooth solution; location of the
  largest error.
- **Model**: LS-04 (a = 0.1 m, b = 0.2 m, p = 100 MPa, quarter cylinder, symmetry on
  the side faces, frictionless on the ends, outer wall free), hex20, n = 1, 2, 4, 8
  elements across the wall (2n around).
- **Reference**: Lamé (Timoshenko & Goodier, 1970); quadratic elements and a smooth
  solution: energy error O(h²), order 2; the largest stress gradient at the inner
  wall.
- **Checks**: u_r inner wall (as LS-04); order of η 2.0 ± 0.3; radius of the node with
  the largest `errorestimate` = a (± one element).
- **Result**: global error 12.5 / 3.44 / 0.894 / 0.239%; order 1.87 / 1.95 / 1.91; the
  largest error at the inner wall on every mesh.
- **Interpretation**: order 2 as expected. The free outer wall gets the bridge's
  pressure ~0 (two to sixteen free faces); the symmetry and frictionless faces have
  no boundary term.

#### FS-03 Code-Aster test case sslv153a through the bridge

- **Purpose**: the same estimate as Code-Aster's own test case, through the whole
  chain (`.liml` → bridge → Code-Aster), with a mixed mesh.
- **Model**: the mesh of sslv153a (`sslv153a.mmed`: 11 nodes, one hexa8, penta6,
  pyram5 and tetra4), put into the `.liml`; `elem_hex8` = `LINE_QUAD` (the test does
  `CREA_MAILLAGE LINE_QUAD`); E = 400,000, ν = 0.3; fixed on MURHEXA and MURPENT;
  pressure 13, 14, 15 and 16 on CHARTETR, CHARPYRA, CHARPENT and CHARHEXA. The groups
  LIBRE and LIBRE2 (pressure 1e-14 in the test) are ordinary faces without a load in
  the `.liml`: the bridge turns them into free faces itself.
- **Reference**: TEST_RESU of sslv153a (primal problem, NON_REGRESSION): DZ at node
  MESURE −0.018667672103377813, `ERREST` of `ERME_NOEU` 9339.361119203888; the global
  error 51.518% from the `.mess` of the test case (§10).
- **Result**: all three equal within 1e-6 relative.
- **Interpretation**: the free-face finder finds exactly the traction-free boundaries
  of the test case, the loads are merged correctly and `ERME_NOEU` reaches the results
  as a nodal field.

#### Conclusion error estimator

All 10 cases (32 checks) are within tolerance. The estimate decreases with the
expected order (1 for hex8, 2 for hex20), follows the true energy error with an
effectivity index of 1.3…2.2, points at the clamped end and the inner wall as the
largest error, and equals Code-Aster's own test case. Not covered: tet and wedge
elements separately (they are in sslv153a and sslv111f), shells and beams (no
estimate). The display in the Mecway GUI was checked by hand.

### Fracture mechanics (BR)

Mecway S30 with `CRACK_` selections → `lin_static_3d` → `MECA_STATIQUE`, then
`DEFI_FOND_FISS`, `CALC_G` `OPTION=('G', 'K')` and `POST_K1_K2_K3`
([Fracture_mechanics.md](Fracture_mechanics.md)). Module `tests_breukmechanica.py`, run
with `python run_verificatie.py BR`; XFEM (BR-07..14) at the end of this chapter. The values come from the table
`<model>_verif_scheur.csv` of the run (`bridge_fracture.read_csv`); "middle" is the
front point closest to half the front length. Default settings (R_INF/R_SUP automatic,
`LINEAIRE`), except BR-04.

#### BR-01 Centre crack in a plate, plane strain, both lips

- **Goal**: K_I and G of a through crack under tension, with a crack that is open as
  after Mecway's *Disconnect elements* (double nodes on the lips).
- **Model**: plate with half width b = 0.1 m, half crack length a = 0.05 m (a/b =
  0.5), half height H = 0.3 m (H/b = 3), thickness 0.05 m; half model in x (symmetry
  through the crack centre), both halves in y; the lower half has nodes of its own at
  y = 0 for x < a. DZ = 0 on both side faces (plane strain). hex20, at the front h =
  a/40 = 1.25 mm, geometrically growing; 2 elements through the thickness (5 front
  nodes). Tension σ = 100 MPa on y = ±H. E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3. 8997 nodes, 1536
  elements.
- **Reference**: K_I = σ √(πa) F(a/b), F = √(sec(πa/2b)) (1 − 0.025 (a/b)² +
  0.06 (a/b)⁴) = 1.1862 (Tada, Paris & Irwin, 2000, p. 2.2; accurate to 0.1 %):
  K_I = 47.014 MPa·√m. G = K_I² (1 − ν²)/E = 9578.2 J/m².
- **Checks**: K_I (`CALC_G`) and G within 3 %; G/(K_I²/E') within 2 % (theta method
  against interaction integral); K_I (`POST_K1_K2_K3`) within 5 % (check method);
  spread of K_I along the front < 1 %; K_II < 1 % of K_I.

| Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| K_I `CALC_G` | 46.967 MPa·√m | 47.014 | −0.10 % |
| G `CALC_G` | 9539.0 J/m² | 9578.2 | −0.41 % |
| G/(K_I²/E') | 0.9979 | 1 | −0.21 % |
| K_I `POST_K1_K2_K3` | 45.376 MPa·√m | 47.014 | −3.5 % |
| spread of K_I along the front | 1.6e-5 | 0 | – |
| K_II | −1.5e-7 Pa·√m | 0 | – |

- **Interpretation**: `CALC_G` is practically exact on this mesh; `POST_K1_K2_K3`
  (extrapolation of the lip displacement) is 3.5 % low without quarter-point
  elements. With quarter-point nodes (outside the regression: the mid-side nodes of
  the edges from the front at 1/4) it became −0.1 %, `CALC_G` +0.02 %; a mesh study is
  in [Fracture_mechanics.md](Fracture_mechanics.md) §3.

#### BR-02 The same as a half model with symmetry (SYME='OUI')

- **Model**: BR-01 for y ≥ 0 only, *Symmetry* (DY = 0) on the ligament (x > a), only
  `SCHEUR_LIP_BOVEN`. 4549 nodes, 768 elements.
- **Result**: K_I, G, G/(K_I²/E') and K_I (`POST_K1_K2_K3`) equal to BR-01 (in all
  digits): with `SYME='OUI'` Code-Aster computes K and G for the whole crack.

#### BR-03 Thick plate with free side faces

- **Goal**: plane strain in the middle of a thick plate and the edge effect.
- **Model**: as BR-01 with thickness B = 4a = 0.2 m, half model in the thickness
  (symmetry on the mid-plane z = 0, free surface z = 0.1 m), 10 elements through the
  half thickness (refined towards the surface). 35,221 nodes, 7680 elements.
- **Reference**: in the middle of a thick plate plane strain applies: K_I from Tada
  (as BR-01), tolerance 3 %, also at a distance a from the surface.

| Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| K_I `CALC_G` mid-plane | 47.337 MPa·√m | 47.014 | +0.69 % |
| G `CALC_G` mid-plane | 9690.4 J/m² | 9578.2 | +1.2 % |
| G/(K_I²/E') mid-plane | 0.9980 | 1 | −0.20 % |
| K_I `POST_K1_K2_K3` mid-plane | 45.706 MPa·√m | 47.014 | −2.8 % |
| K_I `CALC_G` at a from the surface | 48.033 MPa·√m | 47.014 | +2.2 % |

- **Interpretation**: at the mid-plane the state is plane strain (G/(K²/E') = 0.998).
  Towards the free surface K_I rises up to +6 % (at 0.9 x the half thickness) and
  drops at the surface node itself: there the singularity is no longer 1/√r and K is
  not reliable ([Fracture_mechanics.md](Fracture_mechanics.md) §1.6).

#### BR-04 Code-Aster test case forma07a through the bridge

- **Model**: the mesh of forma07a (`share/aster/tests/forma07a.mmed`, Code-Aster
  17.4): penny-shaped crack with radius a = 2 m in a block, quarter model, hex20 and
  penta15 (wedge15 at the front), with the quarter-point nodes of the test
  (`MODI_MAILLAGE NOEUD_QUART`) already in the `.liml` (converted with Code-Aster,
  `IMPR_RESU FORMAT='ASTER'`). `LEV_SUP`/`LEV_INF`/`LFF` as `SCHEUR_LIP_BOVEN`/
  `_ONDER`/`SCHEUR_FRONT`; `FACE_AV`/`FACE_LAT` *frictionless*, node D DZ = 0;
  *traction* ±1 MPa on `FACE_SUP`/`FACE_INF`; E = 200 GPa, ν = 0.3; R_INF/R_SUP =
  0.2/0.5 m as in the test, with `#ASTER-OPTIE`
  ([ASTER_lines_examples.md](ASTER_lines_examples.md) example 16). 5227 nodes, 1216
  elements. The bridge recognises the quarter-circle front and gives `CALC_G`
  `FORM_FISS='CERCLE', RAYON=2.0`.
- **Reference**: TEST_TABLE of forma07a: K1 MAX of `POST_K1_K2_K3` VALE_CALC
  1,607,011.55098 (NON_REGRESSION) and VALE_REFE 1.5957e6 (analytical 2σ√(a/π),
  PRECISION 0.8 %); G_REF = 11.58648 J/m² from the `.comm` of the test. The test case
  itself runs on this installation with `DIAGNOSTIC JOB : OK`.

| Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference (tol.) |
|---|---|---|---|
| K1 MAX `POST_K1_K2_K3` | 1,607,011.551 | 1,607,011.55098 (VALE_CALC) | 1e-11 (1 %) |
| K1 MAX `POST_K1_K2_K3` | 1,607,011.551 | 1,595,700 (analytical) | +0.71 % (0.8 %) |
| K_I `CALC_G` middle | 1,583,312 | 1,595,700 | −0.78 % (1 %) |
| G `CALC_G` middle | 11.684 J/m² | 11.586 | +0.84 % (1.5 %) |

- **Interpretation**: the bridge reproduces forma07a exactly. Without the curvature
  correction K from `CALC_G` gave +5.6 % on this front (measured during the build),
  with `FORM_FISS` +0.1 % (R automatic) and −0.8 % (R = 0.2/0.5 m).

#### BR-05 Centre crack with linear elements (hex8)

- **Model**: BR-01 with hex8 (`3D`, fully integrated) and 8 elements through the
  thickness. 7533 nodes, 6144 elements.

| Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| K_I `CALC_G` | 46.700 MPa·√m | 47.014 | −0.67 % |
| G `CALC_G` | 9391.3 J/m² | 9578.2 | −1.95 % |
| G/(K_I²/E') | 0.9937 | 1 | −0.63 % |

- **Interpretation**: `CALC_G` is usable with hex8 too. `POST_K1_K2_K3` is ~10 % low
  with hex8 (no check). With only 2 elements through the thickness (3 front nodes) G
  and K oscillated per node with `LINEAIRE` (§2.3 of
  [Fracture_mechanics.md](Fracture_mechanics.md); the bridge then warns).

#### BR-06 Two fronts as two cracks (suffix)

- **Model**: the plate of BR-01 over the full width x ∈ [−b, b] (no symmetry in x):
  the centre crack has two fronts, entered as two cracks
  `SCHEUR_FRONT_L`/`SCHEUR_LIP_BOVEN_L`/`SCHEUR_LIP_ONDER_L` (x < 0) and likewise `_R`.
  Rigid body motion held with DX and DY at x = b, y = 0 and DY at x = −b, y = 0.
  17,728 nodes, 3072 elements.
- **Result**: per front K_I −0.10 % and G −0.41 % from Tada, equal to BR-01; the table
  has the cracks `_L` and `_R`.

#### XFEM (BR-07 .. BR-14)

Mecway S30 without a crack in the mesh, with an `#ASTER-XFEM` line (BR-13: a face
selection `XFEM_`) → `DEFI_FISS_XFEM`,
`MODI_MODELE_XFEM`, `MECA_STATIQUE`, `CALC_G_XFEM` `OPTION='CALC_K_G'` and
`POST_K1_K2_K3` `FISSURE=` ([Fracture_mechanics.md](Fracture_mechanics.md) §7).
Module `tests_breukmechanica_xfem.py`; the values come from `<model>_verif_xfem.csv`
(`bridge_fracture.read_csv`), "middle" is the front point closest to half the front
length. Default settings (R_INF/R_SUP automatic, `smoothing` per shape), except
BR-11. Tolerance on K_I and G: 5 % (S6b done criterion).

##### BR-07 Centre crack in a plate as HALF_PLANE (hex8)

- **Model**: the plate of BR-01 (a = 0.05 m, b = 0.1 m, H = 0.3 m, thickness 0.05 m,
  plane strain, σ = 100 MPa), half model in x, but **without a crack in the mesh**:
  `#ASTER-XFEM vorm=HALFVLAK, front=(0.05, 0, 0), normaal=(0, 1, 0), richting=(1, 0,
  0)`. hex8, uniform h = a/20 = 2.5 mm around the front, the crack plane y = 0 and the
  front x = a run through the middle of the elements; 4 elements through the
  thickness. 5280 nodes, 3968 elements.
- **Reference**: Tada et al. (2000) p. 2.2 as BR-01: K_I = 47.014 MPa·√m, G = 9578.2 J/m²;
  symmetry about y = 0 for the opening of the lips.

| Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference (tol.) |
|---|---|---|---|
| K_I `CALC_G_XFEM` | 46.975 MPa·√m | 47.014 | −0.08 % (5 %) |
| G `CALC_G_XFEM` | 9561.2 J/m² | 9578.2 | −0.18 % (5 %) |
| K_I `POST_K1_K2_K3` | 46.903 MPa·√m | 47.014 | −0.24 % (5 %) |
| G/(K_I²/E') | 0.99991 | 1 | −0.01 % (2 %) |
| spread of K_I along the front | 0.0041 | 0 | (0.01) |
| opening upper lip / lower lip in Mecway (x = 0) | −0.99993 | −1 | 0.01 % (1 %) |

- **Interpretation**: XFEM with hex8 is as accurate on this mesh as the meshed crack
  with hex20 (BR-01). The last check shows that Mecway gets the physical displacement
  of each lip (`POST_CHAM_XFEM` + `PROJ_CHAMP`), not the XFEM degrees of freedom.

##### BR-08 Idem with hex20

- **Model**: BR-07 with hex20. 19,739 nodes, 3968 elements.
- **Result**: K_I +0.10 %, G +0.17 %, `POST_K1_K2_K3` −0.13 %, G/(K_I²/E') 0.9997.
  Code-Aster 17.4 has quadratic XFEM elements (alarm `XFEM_63`: the level set was
  adjusted on the quadratic elements).

##### BR-09 Crack plane through nodes, front on element boundaries (hex8)

- **Model**: BR-07 with a layer of nodes at y = 0 and nodes at x = a (the level sets
  are 0 there). 5115 nodes, 3840 elements.
- **Result**: K_I −0.06 %, G −0.05 %, `POST_K1_K2_K3` +1.1 %, G/(K_I²/E') 1.0008.

##### BR-10 Penny-shaped crack in a block

- **Model**: quarter model of a block 5a × 5a × 10a (a = 20 mm), symmetry x = 0 and
  y = 0, tension σ = 1 MPa on z = ±5a; `#ASTER-XFEM vorm=CIRKEL, centrum=(0, 0, 0),
  r=20 mm, normaal=(0, 0, 1), naam=penny`. hex8, uniform h = a/20 up to r = 1.5a and
  |z| < 5.5h, the crack plane through the middle of the elements; the front cuts the
  elements obliquely. 48,672 nodes, 44,764 elements.
- **Reference**: infinite medium (Tada, Paris & Irwin, 2000, p. 24.2; Sneddon, 1946): K_I = 2σ√(a/π) =
  0.15958 MPa·√m; opening at the centre δ = 8(1 − ν²)σa/(πE) = 2.2069e-7 m (the nodes
  are at ±h/2, which adds σh/E ≈ +2 %; the block is finite).

| Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference (tol.) |
|---|---|---|---|
| K_I `POST_K1_K2_K3` middle | 0.16219 MPa·√m | 0.15958 | +1.6 % (5 %) |
| K from G: √(G E/(1 − ν²)) middle | 0.16112 MPa·√m | 0.15958 | +1.0 % (5 %) |
| K_I `CALC_G_XFEM` middle | 0.16797 MPa·√m | 0.15958 | +5.3 % (5 %; BEKEND, KNOWN_BUGS 83) |
| crack opening (displz in Mecway) | 2.2796e-7 m | 2.2069e-7 | +3.3 % (5 %) |

- **Interpretation**: `CALC_G_XFEM` has no curvature correction (unlike `CALC_G` with
  `FORM_FISS='CERCLE'` for the meshed crack, BR-04); on a curved front K from
  `POST_K1_K2_K3` or from G is the value to use (the bridge warns). A mesh study
  (h = a/10, a/5) is in [Fracture_mechanics.md](Fracture_mechanics.md) §7.4.
  The check on K_I from `CALC_G_XFEM` has the same 5 % tolerance as the other XFEM
  checks and is marked BEKEND (known, KNOWN_BUGS 83): a wider tolerance cannot be
  justified by the discretisation error, since the deviation decreases with the mesh
  (+15 / +10 / +5.3 % at h = a/5, a/10, a/20) but is a limitation of the method on a
  curved front, not of the translation.

- **Reference**: TEST_TABLE of forma07b.com2: K1 MAX of `CALC_G_XFEM` (LAGRANGE,

  (NON_REGRESSION), VALE_REFE 1.5957e6 (analytical 2σ√(a/π), PRECISION 1 %).

| Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference (tol.) |
|---|---|---|---|
| K1 MAX `CALC_G_XFEM` | 1,671,075.9 | 1,671,075.88 (VALE_CALC) | < 1e-6 (1 %) |
| K1 MAX `POST_K1_K2_K3` | 1,607,458.6 | 1,607,458.57 (VALE_CALC) | < 1e-6 (1 %) |

- **Model**: BR-07 with the setting `med_export` (*MED file for ParaView*). 5280 nodes,
