#### Conclusion fracture mechanics

All 6 cases (26 checks) are within tolerance: K_I from `CALC_G` within 0.8 % of Tada
and of forma07a, G within 2 %, G and K_I²/E' consistent within 0.7 %, and the double
nodes on the lips, the half model, several cracks with a suffix, a curved front and
`#ASTER-OPTIE` work.
XFEM (8 cases, 37 checks): K_I and G on a straight front within 0.2 % of Tada with hex8
and hex20, also with the front and the crack plane through elements or through nodes,
with two fronts and with the crack from a face selection `XFEM_` (BR-13, equal to
BR-09); the MED file `_xfem.rmed` for ParaView has the fields on the mesh that follows
the crack (BR-14); the penny crack within 1.6 % (`POST_K1_K2_K3`, K from G),
`CALC_G_XFEM` +5.3 % (no curvature correction; BEKEND, KNOWN_BUGS 83); forma07b reproduced exactly; Mecway gets
the physical displacement per lip (opening +3.3 % from Sneddon). Not covered with XFEM:
tet10, wedge15, pyr13, `group=`, an ELLIPSE with a ≠ b, reactions on nodes of cut
elements (KNOWN_BUGS 63). Not
covered: a closed front, tet10, an interface between materials, thermal strain,
pressure on the lips (KNOWN_BUGS 63); the workflow in the Mecway GUI (*Disconnect
elements*) was NOT checked.

### Adaptive refinement (AD)

Mecway S30 → `lin_static_3d` with `adapt_refine` on → after each run
`bridge_adapt.after_run` chooses the elements (by default with Dörfler's criterion,
Dörfler, 1996) and `bridge_adapt.refine_in_mecway`
refines them in Mecway ([Error_estimator.md](Error_estimator.md) §6). Module
`tests_adaptief.py`, run with `python run_verificatie.py AD`. The loop of the main
script runs offline with the same functions: the bridge and Code-Aster as in every
case, the refinement in **headless Mecway** (`mecway.exe <model> script <script.py>`,
with `bridge_adapt.refine_in_mecway(mw, ...)`). The intermediate models are
`TestFiles/Verificatie/modellen/AD-02-L0.liml` … `AD-02-L5.liml` (open them in Mecway);
if headless Mecway is not available (`mecway.exe` not found or the refinement fails),
the loop falls back to those stored models; the check "lus met Mecway gedraaid" (loop
run with Mecway: the fraction of refinement steps that Mecway actually did, reference
1, tolerance 0) is then NOK, so AD-02 never passes silently on old models.

- **Model**: quarter plate with a circular hole (r = 10 mm) in tension 100 MPa, width W =
  100 mm (d/W = 0.2), length 300 mm, hex20; a slab of 10 mm with symmetry on both z
  faces (plane strain). Plane strain and plane stress give the same Kt (the stresses of
  a plane problem with boundary loads do not depend on ν); checked with the same plate
  1 mm thick in plane stress: n = 32 −0.08%.
- **Reference**: Kt_n = σ_xx(0, r) / σ_nom with σ_nom = σ W/(W − d); Peterson (Pilkey &
  Pilkey, 2008, chart 4.1): 2 + 0.284(1 − d/W) − 0.600(1 − d/W)² + 1.32(1 − d/W)³ =
  2.519; as a check Heywood (1952) 2 + (1 − d/W)³ = 2.512 and Roark (Young & Budynas, 2002, table 17.1, case 2a)
  2.508. Done criterion S7: Kt within 2% after the loop, with fewer elements than
  uniform refinement.

| ID | Model | Result |
|---|---|---|
| AD-01-n1 … n32 | uniform: 2n × 2n hex20 per block, 1 layer (12·4^k elements) | Kt −27.2 / −14.3 / −6.34 / −2.47 / −0.75 / −0.14% (n = 1 … 32); within 2% from n = 16 |
| AD-02 | the loop: coarse mesh (12 hex20), target 1.5%, Dörfler 0.5, max. 6 loops | 5 loops, all 5 refinements by headless Mecway (fraction 1); Kt +0.82% with 15 810 elements; uniform Refine x2 to 2%: 49 152 (factor 0.32); the global error decreases in every loop; 98% of the refined elements within 3r of the hole |
| AD-03 | beam hex20 with fixed on a node selection | the loop stops with an ERROR before refining |

Per loop (AD-02):

| Loop | Elements | Nodes | Global error | Kt_n | vs Peterson | Refined |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 12 | 127 | 4.57% | 1.834 | −27.2% | 2 |
| 1 | 88 | 424 | 3.60% | 1.872 | −25.7% | 7 |
| 2 | 422 | 1 408 | 2.92% | 2.202 | −12.6% | 21 |
| 3 | 1 666 | 4 306 | 2.40% | 2.385 | −5.33% | 60 |
| 4 | 5 088 | 12 464 | 1.81% | 2.370 | −5.93% | 162 |
| 5 | 15 810 | 35 233 | 1.11% | 2.540 | +0.82% | – (target reached) |

Further checks in AD-02: √(Σ η_e²) from the result file = the global η from the `.mess`
(≤ 1e-3; the loop reads the right element values); σ_xx at the end face 100 MPa
(+0.01%: the traction on the face selection follows the refinement). All meshes are
conforming (every interior face belongs to two elements).

Observations:
- Mecway's Refine x2 on everything also divides the thickness; that does not change Kt
  (plane strain), so uniform refinement costs 12·8^k elements for the same Kt as AD-01:
  49 152 for within 2% (n = 16). The loop gets there with 15 810 (+0.82%).
- The loop refines in steps: Kt only improves when the elements at the peak get their
  turn (loop 3 → 4 slightly worse, −5.3 → −5.9%); the global error decreases in every
  loop (ratio ≤ 0.82).
- Measured outside the regression (same plate, [Error_estimator.md](Error_estimator.md)
  §6.5): a thin plate (thickness 1/40 of the element width) gave nearly flat hex20 in
  loop 2 and a rising estimate (3.7 → 19.6%): the loop stops with a WARNING; tet10
  stopped after the first loop; with a buffer layer (*Also refine the neighbours*) the
  thick plate reached Kt within 2% only at 22 956 elements.
- A Code-Aster issue in the estimator found and worked around: `FORCE_FACE` counts in
  `ERME` with the reversed sign (block under uniform tension: 15% instead of 0); without
  the correction the loop chose the elements at the loaded end face in the first loop
  ([Error_estimator.md](Error_estimator.md) §2.3).
- The Mecway GUI itself (the loop with the wait for Code-Aster in each loop) is NOT checked; headless the step of
  the main script was checked on a model with results: `after_run` in Mecway's
  IronPython, `solution.element_node_value("errorestimate", 1, 1)` = the value from the
  result file (11963.69), `refine_x2` and `save_as` with a solution in the model.

#### Adaptive refinement with HOMARD inside the job (AD-10 … AD-12)

Step S35b ([Error_estimator.md](Error_estimator.md) §7): the same quarter plate with a
hole (d/W = 0.2, slab 10 mm, 12 hex20, 1 layer), thermal and with plasticity, with
`adapt_homard`; Code-Aster refines the mesh itself in one run and the result comes back
onto the 12 coarse elements. The quantity is the one at the node (0, R) at the edge of the
hole.

- **Reference**: uniformly refined through the bridge (step S35a,
  `TestFiles/Tools/homard_s35/run_s35.py references`): 32 × 32 hex20 per block (12 288
  elements in the plane). Thermal (T = 0 / 100 °C on x = 0 / x = L, k = 50 W/m/K, hole
  insulated): q_x = −67 465.3 W/m² (n = 16: −67 326.6, so converged within 0.2%).
  Plasticity (bilinear, yield 250 MPa, plastic modulus 2000 MPa, traction ramp to
  150 MPa, `GDEF_LOG`): σ_xx = 291.007 MPa, p = 0.00150735 (n = 16: 291.665 MPa,
  0.00151098). Uniform in 3D (every layer refined too, as HOMARD does): thermal n = 4
  (768 elements) −2.66%, n = 8 (6144) −0.87%; plasticity n = 8 (6144) +2.4% / +0.8%.

| ID | Model | Result |
|---|---|---|
| AD-10 | thermal, target 0.15% (ZZ), max. 10 loops, hole recognised automatically as a cylinder | q_x −66 878 W/m² (−0.87%, tolerance 1.5%) with 2621 elements after 8 loops: 0.43 x uniform n = 8 at the same accuracy; stopped on the target |
| AD-10b | as AD-10, with face selection `Gat` and `#ASTER-RAND Gat = CILINDER 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 10 mm` | q_x equal to AD-10 (< 1e-4); the log reports `#ASTER-RAND` |
| AD-11 | plasticity, target 0.4%, max. 10 loops, symmetry as `FACE_IMPO DNOR` | σ_xx 293.69 MPa (+0.92%, tolerance 3%), p 0.0015262 (+1.25%), 4364 elements after 9 loops (0.71 x uniform n = 8, which gives +2.4%); after 8 loops (1932 elements) already +2.3%; all 5 time steps in Mecway |
| AD-12 | thermal with the cold side as a node selection (more than one node) | with `adapt_homard` the bridge gives an ERROR that names the selection and does not compute |

Per loop (AD-10 / AD-11, global ZZ error): 1.92 / 3.75% (12 elements), 1.71 / 3.59 (71 /
66), 0.83 / 2.12 (84 / 84), 1.24 / 4.19 (366 / 352), 0.62 / 1.90 (370 / 356), 0.36 / 1.31
(667 / 474), 0.30 / 0.96 (1188 / 758), 0.23 / 1.33 (1782 / 1880), 0.147 / 0.47 (2621 / 1932),
– / 0.375 (– / 4364).

Observations:
- The global error sometimes rises for one loop (HOMARD's transition elements); the loop
  does not stop on that (only after two rising loops).
- The target for the peak is much smaller than the default 1%: at 1% AD-10 stops after 2
  loops (−6%).
- Without boundary following q_x rose in S35a to −81 404 (+21%) after 9 loops: the kinks
  of the coarse polygon act as re-entrant corners.
- The node values on the coarse mesh equal the refined result at the same nodes (AD-10:
  −66 878.0 against −66 878.5 in the prototype); the element-node values miss the jump
  between coarse elements ([Error_estimator.md](Error_estimator.md) §7.5).

### Fatigue (VM)

Wöhler curve per material (`#ASTER-WOHLER`), rainflow and Miner on blocks or a signal
for linear static (`#ASTER-BLOCK`, `#ASTER-SIGNAL`) and on the time steps of
`STAT_NON_LINE`/`DYNA_NON_LINE` ([Fatigue.md](Fatigue.md); `bridge_fatigue.py`, job
module `aster_fatigue.py`). Module `tests_vermoeiing.py`, run with
`python run_verificatie.py VM`. The fields `damage` and `life` of the `.liml` route and
the cycle table `<model>_verif_vermoeiing.csv` are read.

- **Model (all cases)**: bar 0.1 × 0.01 × 0.01 m, 2 hex8 (VM-06: a cube of 0.01 m, 1
  hex8), E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3, symmetry planes x = 0, y = 0, z = 0 (nodal displacement
  0 in the normal) and a traction on x = L (VM-07 also on y = A): uniaxial,
  homogeneous stress σ_xx = traction at every node, so the damage is equal at every
  node (the checks require that within 10⁻⁹).
- **References**: Miner (D = Σ n/N) with the given curve, by hand in the test module
  (independent of the bridge); ASTM E1049-85 fig. 6 (rainflow); Code-Aster test cases
  szlz100a (`POST_FATIGUE`, `VALE_REFE`) and szlz105a (`CALC_FATIGUE`, `VALE_REFE`);
  Code-Aster's own `POST_FATIGUE`/`CALC_FATIGUE` in the same job (`#ASTER-POST`, line
  `VERIF_CA_D` in the `.mess`).

- **Interpretation**: the bridge equals the hand calculation, ASTM E1049 and
  Code-Aster's own `POST_FATIGUE` and `CALC_FATIGUE` to machine precision; the
  differences with `VALE_REFE` of szlz100a/szlz105a are the rounding of those values.
  Measured during the build (not a check in the regression): Code-Aster's
  `POST_FATIGUE`/`CALC_FATIGUE` extend `PROL_*='LINEAIRE'` linearly in (S, N), also with
  `INTERPOL='LOG'` (a small cycle gave, in a first version of VM-06 with
  `PROL_GAUCHE='LINEAIRE'`, 3.85·10⁻⁸ extra damage in `CALC_FATIGUE`, = 1/N(0) with
  N(0) = 2.6·10⁷ extended linearly); the bridge extends log-log ([Fatigue.md](Fatigue.md)
  §3).
- **Mecway**: no fatigue, so no comparison with a Mecway solution. Mecway's API reads
  `damage` and `life` from the results of VM-01a (static) and VM-06 (101 time steps, the
  same value in every step) (headless `mecway.exe <model> script`: `node_value` equal
  to the bridge); the GUI display was NOT checked (KNOWN_BUGS 66).

#### Multiaxial, shells and Manson-Coffin (VM-08 .. VM-11, S8b)

Multiaxial: `#ASTER-FATIGUE criterion = DANG_VAN|MATAKE|CROSSLAND` with `CALC_FATIGUE
FATIGUE_MULTI DOMA_NOEUD` (critical plane in Code-Aster, N from SIGEQ with the bridge's
curve); shells: the uniaxial route on `res_sup`/`res_inf` (`damage_top`/
`damage_bottom`); strain: Manson-Coffin on `EPSI_NOEU` ([Fatigue.md](Fatigue.md)
§1.7–1.9).

- **Models**: VM-08: the cube of sslv135a (10 mm, 1 hex8, E = 200 GPa, ν = 0.3),
  symmetry planes and tractions on x = L and y = A with the history of sslv135a (σ_xx =
  0, +100, 0, −100, 0 and σ_yy = 0, −200, 0, +200, 0 MPa at t = 0..4 s, step 0.5 s),
  nonlinear static (`PETIT`), Wöhler table WHOL of sslv135a (`PROL_GAUCHE = LINEAIRE`),
  a = 1, c = 1.5; VM-08d only the ramp 0 → t = 1. VM-09: cube 10 mm, 2 × 2 × 2 hex8,
  3-2-1 supports and tractions on four faces: homogeneous σ_xx = 120, τ_xy = 80 MPa
  (tension + shear, proportional), linear static with `#ASTER-SIGNAL 0, 1, 0, −1, 0` or
  two blocks, table T1 or Basquin, a = 0.3, b = 110 MPa, c = 1.5. VM-10: free plate
  100 × 20 × 2 mm, 10 × 2 DKT quad4, consistent nodal moments m = σ t²/6 (m h/2 at the
  corners, m h in the middle) on both ends, 3-2-1 supports: pure bending σ = ±150 MPa;
  Basquin + Goodman (Su = 600 MPa), `#ASTER-BLOCK 0.5, 0.5, 1e5`. VM-11a: the bar of
  VM-01 with an imposed elongation 0, +0.6, −0.6, 0 mm (t = 0..3 s, step 0.25 s),
  bilinear plastic (yield strength 250 MPa, H = 10 GPa), `PETIT`; VM-11b: elastic, σ =
  100 MPa, `#ASTER-SIGNAL 0, 2, -2, 0`, `strain = VMIS_SG`. Curve VM-11: Sf = 900 MPa,
  b = −0.09, ef = 0.3, c = −0.55, E = 210 GPa.
- **References**: sslv135a `VALE_REFE` (node N1, `DOMA_NOEUD`): Matake SIGEQ1 = 300 MPa,
  NBRUP1 = 10946, ENDO1 = 9.1356·10⁻⁵; Dang Van 275 MPa, 14903, 6.7100·10⁻⁵. By hand
  (in the test module): Mohr R = √((σ/2)² + τ²) = 100 MPa, P_max = σ/3 = 40 MPa,
  N_max = σ/2 = 60 MPa, √J₂ = √(σ²/3 + τ²) = 105.83 MPa; Dang Van c (R + a P_max) =
  168 MPa, Matake c (R + a N_max) = 177 MPa, Crossland c (√J₂ + a P_max) = 176.75 MPa;
  block 0..1 of Dang Van: c (R/2 + a P_max) = 93 MPa. Plate σ = 6m/t²; Goodman top
  S_eq = 75/(1 − 75/600), bottom 75/(1 + 75/600) MPa. Manson-Coffin: N with Newton in
  ln 2N from ε_a = Sf/E (2N)^b + ef (2N)^c, ε_a = u/L = 0.006 (VM-11a) and ε_eq =
  2/3 (1 + ν) · 2σ/E (VM-11b).

| Case | Quantity | Bridge | Reference | Difference | Tolerance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM-08a (Matake) | SIGEQ / life / D | 300.0 MPa / 10946.1 / 9.13565·10⁻⁵ | sslv135a 300 / 10946 / 9.13565·10⁻⁵ | 8·10⁻¹⁶ / 1.2·10⁻⁵ (rounding of NBRUP1) / 1·10⁻¹⁴ | 1 % |
| VM-08b (Dang Van) | SIGEQ / life / D | 275.0 MPa / 14903.2 / 6.70996·10⁻⁵ | sslv135a 275 / 14903 / 6.70996·10⁻⁵ | 6·10⁻¹⁶ / 1.5·10⁻⁵ / 4·10⁻¹⁵ | 1 % |
| VM-08c (Dang Van, non-periodic) | D | 6.70996·10⁻⁵ | 1/N(275 MPa), one cycle | 4·10⁻¹⁵ | 1 % |
| VM-08d (ramp 0 → 1, Matake) | SIGEQ | 112.5 MPa | 1.5 · 150/2 (N_max < 0); without the copy 56.25 MPa | 0 | 10⁻⁶ |
| VM-09a (Dang Van, signal) | SIGEQ / (τ_a + aP)/b / D | 167.9996 MPa / 1.01818 / 8.3333·10⁻⁷ | 168 / 1.01818 / 8.3334·10⁻⁷ | −2.3·10⁻⁶ / −2.3·10⁻⁶ / −1.0·10⁻⁵ | 0.1 % |
| VM-09b (Dang Van, two blocks, Basquin) | SIGEQ / D | 167.9996 MPa / 1.30488·10⁻⁵ | 168 / 1.30491·10⁻⁵ | −2.3·10⁻⁶ / −2.3·10⁻⁵ | 0.1 % |
| VM-09c (Matake) | SIGEQ / ratio / D | 177.10 MPa / 1.0733 / 1.0571·10⁻⁶ | 177 / 1.0727 / 1.0543·10⁻⁶ | 5.8·10⁻⁴ / 5.8·10⁻⁴ / 2.6·10⁻³ | 1 % |
| VM-09d (Crossland) | SIGEQ / ratio / D | 176.745 MPa / 1.07118 / 1.04752·10⁻⁶ | same | 3·10⁻¹⁵ / 3·10⁻¹⁵ / 2·10⁻¹⁴ | 0.1 % |
| VM-10 (shell, bending) | σ top / D top / D bottom / D | 150.0 MPa / 1.22783·10⁻⁵ / 9.9470·10⁻⁷ / 1.22783·10⁻⁵ | 6m/t² / Goodman, Basquin | 7·10⁻¹³ / 8·10⁻¹² / 6·10⁻¹² / 8·10⁻¹² | 0.1 % |
| VM-11a (Manson-Coffin, plastic) | strain amplitude / D | 0.006000 / 7.42636·10⁻⁴ | u/L / 1/N, N = 1346.6 | 0 / 1.6·10⁻⁹ | 10⁻⁶ / 0.1 % |
| VM-11b (Manson-Coffin, `VMIS_SG`) | D | 1.92211·10⁻⁸ | 1/N(ε_eq) | 0 | 0.1 % |

- **Interpretation**: Code-Aster's SIGEQ follows the formulas c (Δτ/2 + a max(P_max, 0))
  (Dang Van), c (Δτ/2 + a max(N_max, 0)) (Matake) and c (√J₂,a + a max(P_max, 0))
  (Crossland, through `AMPCIS`); the bridge puts SIGEQ on its own Wöhler curve, equal
  to sslv135a. The small deviations for Dang Van (−2·10⁻⁶) and Matake (+6·10⁻⁴) come
  from the search direction of the critical plane in Code-Aster (a grid of normals):
  with Dang Van a small angle error counts quadratically (Δτ is maximal), with Matake
  linearly (N_max changes with the angle); Crossland is plane-independent and exact.
  The shell follows plate theory exactly (DKT with consistent edge moments,
  anticlastic free); top and bottom differ only by the sign of the mean (Goodman). The
  strain in the displacement-controlled bar is exactly u/L, also with plasticity.
- **Measured during the build** (not a check in the regression): `CALC_FATIGUE
  FATIGUE_MULTI` skips `NUME_ORDRE` 0 of `STAT_NON_LINE` (alarm `PREPOST4_27`; hence
  the bridge copies, VM-08d); `DOMA_NOEUD` without `EPSI_NOEU` gives `<EXCEPTION>
  CALCULEL_29`; `D_VAN_B`/`MATAKE_B` do not enter SIGEQ; below the table with `EXCLU`
  Code-Aster gives `NBRUP1` = 0 and `ENDO1` ≈ 10⁻³⁰⁸; `PERIODIQUE` counts one cycle per
  history (two periods: the same damage), `NON_PERIODIQUE` two; `MATAKE_MODI_AV` picks
  a different plane than `MATAKE_MODI_AC` (damage 3.5·10⁻⁴ instead of 9.1·10⁻⁵ on
  sslv135a); `EPSI_NOEU` is the linear strain also with `GDEF_LOG` (0.0060000 at u/L =
  0.006); with `GDEF_LOG` the reverse loading of VM-11a did not converge (KNOWN_BUGS
  82). Smoke tests outside the regression: Dang Van in a dynamic run (`dyna_impl_3d`,
  diagnostic OK) and a model with a solid and a shell (both damage, diagnostic OK).

#### Conclusion fatigue

All 25 cases (52 checks) are within tolerance. VM-01..07 (S8), with differences at
machine precision: constant amplitude (table, Basquin, power form with Se) and a
two-level spectrum (with Goodman and repetitions) according to Miner, the rainflow
cycles of ASTM E1049 exactly, periodic rainflow and the damage equal to `POST_FATIGUE`
and `CALC_FATIGUE` (static, nonlinear static and dynamic), and szlz100a/szlz105a in 3D.
VM-08..11 (S8b): sslv135a in 3D equal to `VALE_REFE` (Matake, Dang Van, periodic and
non-periodic), the critical-plane criteria recomputed by hand under tension + shear
(Dang Van and Crossland ≤ 2·10⁻⁵; Matake 0.26 % due to Code-Aster's search grid,
tolerance 1 %), the shell in pure bending on the top and bottom side and Manson-Coffin
(plastic and elastic) at machine precision. Not covered: beams, multiaxial on shells
and per Gauss point, a non-proportional load with an analytical reference, a
non-homogeneous stress field with an analytical reference, `dyna_expl_3d` (same code
as `dyna_impl_3d`) and the Mecway GUI.

## 5. Nonlinear static (NL)

Mecway L30 → bridge `nonlin_static_3d` → `STAT_NON_LINE`. Module
`tests_niet_lineair.py`, run with `python run_verificatie.py NL`. Default settings:
`deformation` = `GROT_GDEP`, converted by the bridge per element to `GDEF_LOG` (plastic
solids), `GREEN_LAGRANGE` (elastic solids) and `GROT_GDEP` (`COQUE_3D`);
`convergence_resi_rela` = 1e-5 (the bridge default; the Code-Aster default 1e-6 stalls
in NL-01, see there); the solver steps are Mecway's *Time step size* (0.1 s, as in the
samples) plus the kinks of the load tables (NL-20).

### NL-01 Tensile bar, bilinear isotropic hardening, unloading

- **Purpose**: bilinear plasticity (`BilinearIsotropic`), large strains (`GDEF_LOG`) and
  elastic unloading.
- **Model**: bar 100 × 10 × 10 mm, 2 × 1 × 1 hex20; E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3,
  σy = 355 MPa, *tangent modulus* H = 10 GPa.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: symmetry planes x = 0, y = 0, z = 0 (lateral
  contraction free); prescribed elongation at x = L: 0 → 0.10 mm (t = 1 s, elastic) →
  1.00 mm (t = 2 s, plastic) → 0.85 mm (t = 3 s, unloading).
- **Reference**: homogeneous uniaxial state, exactly computable. With `GDEF_LOG`
  (additive logarithmic strain; the dual stress is the Kirchhoff stress τ in uniaxial
  tension without rotation): τ = σy + H·p, p = ln(1+e) − τ/E, elastic unloading;
  F = A0·τ/(1+e), σ = τ/J with J = exp((1−2ν)τ/E). Tolerance 0.2% (every element is
  exact; what remains is the Newton tolerance and J − 1 < 0.1%).
- **Result**: F, σ_xx and p at t = 1, 1.5, 2 and 3 s (after unloading: F = 12 071 N)
  deviate 0.00%, with the default settings. NL-01b (same model with the Code-Aster
  default `convergence_resi_rela` = 1e-6, `convergence_criterion` = `RELA_OF_MAXI` and
  `convergence_resi_maxi` = 0.05 N) also runs to t = 3 s, 0.00% off.
- **Interpretation**:
  1. *Tangent modulus*. Mecway's *tangent modulus* is the plastic modulus H (stress
     against plastic strain), not a slope in total strain. Decisive (Mecway/CCX
     headless, *tangent modulus* 100 GPa, 1% strain): CCX 915.8 MPa, read as H
     914.5 MPa, read as a slope in total strain 1181 MPa. The bridge now gives
     Code-Aster `D_SIGM_EPSI` = E·H/(E+H) (slope in total strain; EDF R&D, U4.43.01);
     before that fix the peak force was 0.87% too high.
  2. *Unloading*. With the Code-Aster default 1e-6 and only `RESI_GLOB_RELA`, Newton
     stalls at RESI_GLOB_RELA 1.5–2.3e-6 (residual force ~1e-2 N at 12 kN); the step is
     subdivided 6 times and the run stops at t = 2.92 s (`MECANONLINE9_7`). Up to that
     moment every step is exact, so the norm is just too strict for this state. That is
     why the bridge default is 1e-5 (NL-01). With 1e-6 it continues with `RELA_OF_MAXI`
     and an absolute limit above the stalled residual (NL-01b, 0.05 N; not with
     1e-3 N) or with `DEFORMATION` = `PETIT`.
  3. *Strain measure*. With `PETIT` the bridge gives exactly the small-strain formula
     F = A0·(σy + H·p); the difference from the logarithmic strain is 1.1% at 1% strain.

### NL-20 Mecway's step size with kinks of the load table in between

- **Goal**: nonlinear static computes with Mecway's *Time step size*
  (`<timesteps stepsize>`) and still follows the load path
  exactly.
- **Model**: NL-01 with `<timesteps timeperiod="3 s" stepsize="0.4 s">`; the kinks of the
  imposed elongation (t = 1 and 2 s) fall between two steps.
- **Reference**: output times 0.4; 0.8; 1.0; 1.2; 1.6; 2.0; 2.4; 2.8; 3.0 s (Mecway's
  steps plus the kinks); F, σ_xx and p as NL-01.
- **Result**: the output times match exactly; F, σ_xx and p at t = 1, 2 and 3 s 0.00%
  off (the same values as NL-01 with 30 steps).
- **Interpretation**: the kinks as an extra solver step keep the load linear per step, so
  radial return stays exact. Mecway's own output times were not checked headless
  (Mecway solve was not available when this case was added).

### NL-21 Mecway's *Automatic time stepping*

- **Goal**: with *Automatic time stepping* the bridge computes with `DEFI_LIST_INST
  METHODE='AUTO'` and delivers Mecway's output times.
- **Model**: NL-01 with `automatictimestepping="true"`: (a) internal solver, step size
  0.4 s over 3 s (as NL-20); (b) CCX format, *time points* every 0.5 s.
- **Reference**: (a) the times of NL-20; (b) output times 0.5 .. 3.0 s, 6 solver steps
  (the fixed list without the option: 30); F, σ_xx and p as NL-01.
- **Result**: times exact, F, σ_xx and p 0.00 % off; (b) 6 solver steps.
- **Interpretation**: the times in the list are mandatory solver points (17.4: also
  with `METHODE='AUTO'`), `ARCHIVAGE` is not; that is why the output times and the kinks
  are in the list. With the option on NL-90 and NK-04 (temporarily, not in the suite):
  NL-90 equal within 0.01 %, NK-04 P_u/Pcr 0.480 vs 0.488 (within the tolerance).

### NL-04 Hardening curve with a different E than the *Elastic* tab

- **Goal**: E with a hardening curve in total strain (GitHub issue #2).
- **Model**: bar as NL-01 (hex20), *Young's modulus* 200 GPa, curve (0, 0),
  (0.001, 210 MPa), (0.011, 310 MPa); elongation up to strain 5·10⁻⁴ (t = 1) and 5·10⁻³
  (t = 2). NL-04b: the same curve as *Kinematic* (linear, so `VMIS_CINE_LINE`). NL-04c:
  the same curve without the point (0, 0).
- **Reference**: Mecway uses the slope of the first segment (E = 210 GPa), measured with
  Mecway/CCX headless: σ = 104.97 MPa at t = 1 (nonlinear) and 105.00 MPa (linear static,
  CCX); 248.1 MPa at t = 2. Analytical (uniaxial, log strain): F and σ with E = 210 GPa and
  the curve as total strain. NL-04c (without (0, 0)), Mecway 34/CCX headless: 104.98 MPa
  at t = 1 (nonlinear), 105.00 MPa (linear static, CCX) and 248.1 MPa at t = 2, so there
  too E = slope origin → first point (210 GPa, not 200 GPa).
- **Result**: NL-04, NL-04b and NL-04c: F at t = 1 and 2 and σ_xx at t = 1 0.00% off
  (NL-04c against Mecway −0.03%); the bridge
  gives a WARNING showing both values of E. Without this change Code-Aster stopped with
  `MATERIAL1_5` (slope > 1% different from E) and the kinematic curve was not translated.
- **Interpretation**: at t = 2 CCX is 0.7% below the analytical log-strain value
  (249.9 MPa): CCX uses a different large-strain formulation for plasticity.

### NL-50 .. NL-53 Hyperelastic (Neo Hooke, Mooney-Rivlin)

- **Goal**: `ELAS_HYPER` from Mecway's Neo Hooke and Mooney-Rivlin (GitHub issue #5).
- **Model**: cube 10 mm (one hex8), symmetry planes, prescribed elongation up to λ = 1.5
  (tension) or 0.7 (compression). Neo Hooke C10 = 1 MPa; Mooney-Rivlin C10 = 0.8 and
  C01 = 0.2 MPa; D1 = 10⁻⁹ m²/N (K = 2 GPa, about 1000 G). NL-50/50b Neo Hooke
  tension/compression, NL-51/51b Mooney-Rivlin, NL-52 Neo Hooke in *Dynamic (implicit)*
  (1 s, ρ = 1000 kg/m³), NL-53 in *Dynamic (explicit)* (20 ms).
- **Reference**: incompressible, uniaxial: P = 2(λ − λ⁻²)(C10 + C01/λ), Cauchy σ = λP,
  F = P·A0. Mecway/CCX headless (same model): σ = 3.163 / −1.877 / 2.952 / −2.038 MPa.
- **Result**: F and σ_xx within 0.11% (static and implicit; σ equal to Mecway/CCX within
  0.01%), explicit +0.7% (inertia at 20 ms).
- **Interpretation**: K = 2/D1 agrees with Mecway; the remaining difference is the
  compressibility (finite K). `SIXX` is the Cauchy stress.

### NL-02 Cantilever, large deflection (elastica)

- **Purpose**: large displacements and rotations with solids (`GREEN_LAGRANGE`) and
  `COQUE_3D` (`GROT_GDEP`).
- **Model**: L = 1 m; (a) solid 10 × 10 mm, 20 × 1 × 2 hex20; (b) strip 50 mm wide,
  10 mm thick, 20 × 1 quad8 with `elem_shell` = `COQUE_3D`. E = 210 GPa, ν = 0 (the
  elastica does not depend on ν; this avoids lateral contraction at the clamp).
- **Boundary conditions and load**: clamped at x = 0; tip force in −z with a fixed
  direction (`force` on the nodes of the end face, `FORCE_NODALE`), time table
  PL²/EI = 1, 2, 3 at t = 1, 2, 3 s.
- **Reference**: inextensible Euler elastica θ'' = −α cos θ, solved numerically (RK4 and
  shooting) in the test module; equal to Bisshopp & Drucker (1945) and Mattiasson
  (1981): α = 1: v/L 0.30172, u/L 0.05643; α = 2: 0.49346 / 0.16064; α = 3: 0.60325 /
  0.25442. Measured at the node on the axis at the tip. Tolerance 1% (discretisation;
  shear ~1e-4 and axial strain ~1e-5 are negligible).
- **Result**: (a) hex20 −0.12 … −0.57% (slightly stiff, growing with the load);
  (b) `COQUE_3D` +0.01% at all levels.
- **Interpretation**: large displacements work for solids and `COQUE_3D`, and the force
  keeps its direction. DKT and the beams `POU_D_E`/`POU_D_T` stay geometrically linear in
  the bridge (KNOWN_BUGS 11); the same elastica with `POU_D_T_GD` beams: NL-83.

### NL-03 Contact

**NL-03a/b: two cantilevers with their tips on each other.**

- **Purpose**: line contact between hex20 solids, Lagrange (a) and penalty (b).
- **Model**: two beams 100 × 10 × 5 mm (b × h), both clamped at x = 0, hex20 20 × 1 × 1
  (lower beam 22 elements, 10 mm overhang). The top face of the lower beam touches the
  upper beam only at x = L (no gap) and drops 0.5 mm towards the clamp: the contact is a
  line at x = L. Frictionless; slave = underside of the upper beam (last 20 mm), master
  = top of the lower beam. E = 210 GPa, ν = 0 (with ν = 0.3 the 3D model gave 1.35% less
  deflection than beam theory because of plate action at the clamp, with ν = 0 0.01%).
- **Boundary conditions and load**: P = 10 N in −z on the end face of the upper beam;
  (a) `contact_algo` = STANDARD, (b) PENALISATION with kn = 1e13 Pa/m.
- **Reference**: C = L³/3EI + L/(κGA) (Young & Budynas, 2002, table 8.1, case 1a, plus
  shear); equal stiffness, so contact force R = P/2 and tip of the upper beam
  (P − R)·C = 7.6305e-5 m. The mean (w_upper + w_lower)/2 = P·C/2 holds for any contact
  law. Tolerance 0.5%.
- **Result**: (a) no usable result: the edge penetrates between the integration points
  (R ≈ 0) and at t ≈ 0.03 s Code-Aster stops with `MECANONLINE9_4` (matrix not
  invertible); status BEKEND (KNOWN_BUGS 43). (b) tip −0.01% (mean) / +0.07% (upper),
  R = 4.996 N (−0.07%).
- **Interpretation**: Lagrange contact fails for this line contact on hex20 (QUAD8
  faces). Also tried earlier, without success: `AUTO` (zero pivot on `LAGS_C` of a
  corner node in the first step, also with master and slave swapped), `GAUSS` (same
  picture as `NCOTES`) and `NCOTES` with swapped roles (runs, but R = 4.74 N and upper
  tip +7%). Penalty is exact: R = P/(2 + k_b/K_c) with k_b = 1/C gives K_c ≈ 5e7 N/m, so
  the effect of kn = 1e13 Pa/m is < 0.1%. A softer kn lets the beams penetrate and
  shifts load to the upper beam (the mean stays the same).

**NL-03c–f: contact patch test.**

- **Purpose**: contact between non-matching quadratic meshes, Lagrange and penalty, and
  the translation of Mecway's *normal stiffness*.
- **Model**: two blocks 20 × 20 × 10 mm on top of each other, lower block 2 × 2 × 1
  hex20, upper block 3 × 3 × 1 hex20; E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: symmetry planes x = 0 and y = 0 (both blocks),
  bottom z = 0 held in z; 4000 N uniformly on the top (p = 10 MPa). (c, e) STANDARD,
  (d, f) PENALISATION with kn = 1e13 Pa/m; (e, f) with the integration set explicitly as
  `#ASTER-ZONE INTEGRATION='NCOTES'`.
- **Reference**: uniaxial compression, contact pressure p everywhere: compression
  p·H/E = 4.762e-7 m per block; with penalty an extra p/kn = 1.0e-6 m (Mecway: contact
  pressure = *normal stiffness* × penetration). Tolerance 0.5% (the state can be
  represented exactly).
- **Result**: all displacements and the penetration deviate ≤ 0.01%, in c–f.
- **Interpretation**: `COEF_PENA_CONT` = Mecway's *normal stiffness* is translated
  correctly (NL-03d/f; Mecway/CCX headless on the same model: −1.95245e-6 m against the
  reference −1.95238e-6 m). With the former default integration (`AUTO`, for these faces
  equal to `SIMPSON`) Code-Aster did not pass the patch test (STANDARD up to +2.8% /
  −1.5%, PENALISATION +4.6% and penetration −2.1%); the bridge therefore now sets
  `NCOTES` for a quadratic slave face. With matching meshes `AUTO` is exact as well.
  `NCOTES` has not been tested separately on tet10 or curved faces (the Mecway samples
  PipeClip and AssemblyWithContact do run with it, see `bridge_to_aster.py`).

- **NL-03p** (penetration check): the patch test of NL-03d with kn = 1e10 Pa/m:
  penetration p/kn = 1 mm (exact), 15 % of the edge of the slave face (6.67 mm); the
  bridge gives a WARNING after the run (`contact_pene_maxi` 0.05). NL-03b/d/f (1 µm)
  give none.

### NL-70 .. NL-75 Temperature in time (thermal strain)

- **Purpose**: a *Temperature* with a time table or a formula in t, and a constant
  temperature with and without *Quasi-static*, in `STAT_NON_LINE` (`EVOL_THER` from the
  reference, [Thermal.md](Thermal.md) §3.1).
- **Model**: bar 0.1 × 0.01 × 0.01 m, 4 × 1 × 1 hex8, E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3,
  α = 1.2e-5 1/K, *Thermal stress* Tref = 293.15 K, `temperature2` on all nodes (NL-74:
  table on the nodes at x = 0 and *Node temperature* +200 K at x = L). *Quasi-static*
  with *Time period* 2 s (NL-73b and NL-75 without `<timesteps>`). Table ΔT = (0 s; 10 K),
  (1 s; 100 K), (2 s; 50 K); formula Tref + 100·t. `deformation` = `PETIT`; NL-70b with the
  default `GROT_GDEP`.
- **Boundary conditions**: symmetry planes x = 0, y = 0, z = 0 (free expansion); NL-71
  also u_x = 0 at x = L (clamped in x, transverse free).
- **Reference**: free u_x(L, t) = α·ΔT(t)·L, clamped σ_xx(t) = −E·α·ΔT(t) (Timoshenko &
  Goodier, 1970, §150; uniform strain, exact with hex8); NL-74 like LS-08c with the
  element averages; NL-70b with Green-Lagrange strain α·ΔT: L·(√(1 + 2αΔT) − 1). Also
  Mecway 34 with CCX on the same models (headless; Mecway's own solver does not solve L30
  without a window). Tolerance 1e-6 (analytical), 1e-4 and 0.5% (Mecway/CCX).
- **Result**: all checks exact (≤ 1e-6) at t = 0.5/1/2 s (table), 1/2 s (formula), the
  first step t = 0.25 s (NL-73a: full expansion) and t = 0.5 s (NL-73b: half the expansion,
  ramped). Equal to Mecway/CCX (displacement exact; stress: CCX 0.2–0.4% lower, CCX's
  hex8).
- **Interpretation**: Mecway follows the table linearly, also between the points; with
  *Quasi-static* a constant temperature acts from the first step (Mecway/CCX: full
  expansion at t = 0.25 s), without it it ramps. The bridge does the same. With
  `GROT_GDEP` the thermal strain counts as Green-Lagrange strain (0.03–0.06% less
  elongation than Mecway/CCX, NL-70b). Without *Quasi-static* Mecway/CCX takes the table
  value at t = 1 s (the step time), also when the table runs further; the bridge then
  computes up to t = 1 s (NL-75: 1.2e-4 m, exact and equal to Mecway/CCX).
### NL-40 Formula in y and t

- **Purpose**: a formula in x, y, z and t in `STAT_NON_LINE` (`FORMULE` of Y and
  `INST`) next to an ordinary force table (`DEFI_FONCTION`, `FONC_MULT`).
- **Model**: block of LS-40, Mecway L30 with *Time period* 2 s.
- **Load**: pressure `3*y^2*t` kPa on the top and a force Z from 0 (t = 0) to −2000 N
  (t = 2 s) on the same faces.
- **Reference**: reaction 8 kN·t + 1 kN·t: 9 kN at t = 1 s, 18 kN at t = 2 s; moment
  about X at t = 2 s 24 + 2 = 26 kN·m.
- **Result**: all three exact (≤ 1e-6).
- **Interpretation**: `INST` in the `FORMULE` is the solution time of `STAT_NON_LINE`
  (the solver steps are Mecway's step size, 0.5 s). Mecway 34
  with CCX computes the same formula too (displacement proportional to t, same ratio to
  the bridge as with a constant pressure). In linear static the bridge takes t = end
  time (WARNING).

### NL-80 .. NL-90 Beams with large rotations (`POU_D_T_GD`)

- **Purpose**: `elem_beam` = `POU_D_T_GD` ([Settings §2.9](Settings_contact_and_nonlinear.md#29-beams-with-large-rotations-elem_beam--pou_d_t_gd)):
  `MODELISATION='POU_D_T_GD'`, `RELATION='ELAS_POUTRE_GR'`, `DEFORMATION='GROT_GDEP'`,
  section forces from `SIEF_ELNO`, with the combinations the bridge has gained since
  V2026.1 (line3, orientation card, formulas, Mecway's time step). NL-80 to NL-82 are
  the benchmarks GB-01 to GB-03 of the fork by MishalTPC
  (`github.com/mishal0488/MecwayAsterBridge`, commit dd4c958), here as a Mecway model
  through the whole bridge. Module `tests_balken_grote_rotaties.py`.
- **Model**: cantilever L = 1 m along X, RectBar 10 × 20 mm (10 mm along V),
  E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3, 40 line2 (NL-84: 20 line3); fixed at x = 0.
- **References** (k = 5/6, `AY` = 1.2 of `RECTANGLE`):

| Case | Load | Reference |
|---|---|---|
| NL-80 | tip force 1 N in Y (small load) | F L³/(3EI) + F L/(kGA) (GB-01) |
| NL-80b | the same model in linear static | the same; the bridge makes it `POU_D_T` (WARNING) |
| NL-80c | constant tip force 1 N in Y and constant tip moment 0.5 Nm about Z (no time table) | deflection F L³/(3EI) + F L/(kGA) + M L²/(2EI), rotation F L²/(2EI) + M L/(EI) |
| NL-81 | end moment M = (π/2)·EI/L about Z in 20 steps (Mecway step 0.05 s) | circular arc: tip x = L sin θ/θ, y = L (1 − cos θ)/θ, θ = π/2 (GB-02); M constant, N = 0 |
| NL-82 | column with initial curvature e0 (1 − cos(πx/2L)), e0 = 1e-4 L in the mesh, compressive force 0.5 P_cr | total deflection / e0 = 1/(1 − P/P_cr) = 2 (GB-03) |
| NL-83 | tip force of fixed direction, PL²/EI = 1, 2, 3 at t = 1, 2, 3 s | Reissner beam (extension and shear), shooting with RK4 in the test module; inextensible equal to Mattiasson (1981) |
| NL-84 | NL-81 with 20 line3 (40 SEG2 cells) | as NL-81 |
| NL-85 | NL-81 with an *Element orientation* card V = Z and the moment about Y | as NL-81 in the XZ plane; without the card I would be four times as large |
| NL-86 | centrifugal force 300 r/min about Z (radial beam) | u(L) = ρω²L³/(3E), reaction ρAω²L²/2 |
| NL-86b | NL-86 plus a radial truss (40 line2, `BARRE`) at y = 0.5 m, nodes held in Y and Z | as NL-86 for the truss |
| NL-87 | line pressure 20 N/m and gravity in −Z, configuration GQ | w L⁴/(8EI) + w L²/(2kGA), w = q + ρAg; reaction w L |
| NL-87b | the same model, configuration G (line pressure suppressed) | the same with w = ρAg |
| NL-88 | Thermal stress, ΔT = 100 K | α ΔT L |
| NL-89 | line pressure as a formula `-30*x*t` N/m in −Z (Mecway step 0.5 s) | 11 q0 L⁴/(120EI) + q0 L²/(3kGA) (Roark), proportional to t; reaction q0 L/2 |
| NL-90 | two coincident clamped beams that only share the tip; the second ends there with a flexible joint with U, V and W free (ball joint); tip force P = 1400 N in Y | each beam P/2 (identical, tip free to rotate): Reissner beam of NL-83 with PL²/EI = 2 per beam; reaction of beam 2 −P/2 |
| NL-90b | the same, general section (A = 1e-4 m², I = 1e-8/12 m⁴, J = 1.4e-9 m⁴), tip moment M = GJ (π/2)/L about X | the joint passes no moment: beam 1 twists through π/2, reaction moment −M at root 1 and 0 at root 2 (rigid: π/4 and −M/2) |

  NL-83 also compares the section forces with statics in the deformed state (FEM
  coordinates): `bendingmomw` of element 1 = P (x_tip − x_middle), `sheary` and
  `tensileforce` of the last element = P cos φ and P sin φ (φ = angle of the chord of
  that element). NL-82 and NL-83 were also solved headless with Mecway 34 (CCX 2.23,
  B31; `TestFiles/Tools/mw_solve.py`, mean of the section nodes at x = L).
  Tolerance 0.1 %; stricter: the moment in NL-81, NL-84 and NL-85 0.01 %, the normal
  force there absolute 0.05 N (reference 0), the reactions in NL-83, NL-87, NL-87b and
  NL-89 and the extension in NL-88 1e-6 relative; in NL-90b the rotation and reaction
  0.01 %, the reaction moment of root 2 absolute 1e-6 M. NL-90 and NL-90b rebuild
  `check_nonlinear_releases.py` of the fork by MishalTPC (branch dev).
- **Result**: all checks OK.

| Case | Quantity | Deviation | Fork (GB) | Mecway 34 + CCX |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NL-80 | tip deflection | −0.0157 % | −0.0157 % | – |
| NL-80b | tip deflection | 0.0000 % | – | – |
| NL-80c | tip deflection / tip rotation | −0.0091 / −0.0001 % | – | – |
| NL-81 | tip x / y / rotation / M | +0.0112 / +0.0064 / 0.0000 / 0.0000 % | +0.0113 / +0.0064 % | CCX stops at t = 0.55 s |
| NL-82 | deflection / e0 | −0.0213 % | −0.0213 % | +0.0047 % |
| NL-83 | tip u_y at t = 1, 2, 3 | −0.0123 / −0.0079 / −0.0054 % | – | +0.0087 / +0.0125 / +0.0157 % |
| NL-83 | tip u_x at t = 1, 2, 3 | +0.0331 / +0.0247 / +0.0194 % (smaller) | – | +0.0036 / −0.0030 / −0.0085 % |
| NL-83 | section forces against statics (M, V, N) | 0.0000 / +0.0065 / −0.0029 % | – | – |
| NL-84, NL-85 | as NL-81 | the same values as NL-81 | – | – |
| NL-86 | tip extension / reaction | −0.0156 / 0.0000 % | – | – |
| NL-86b | tip extension / reaction of the truss | +0.0312 / 0.0000 % | – | – |
| NL-87, NL-87b | tip deflection / reaction | +0.0008 / 0.0000 %; +0.0002 / 0.0000 % | – | – |
| NL-88 | tip extension | 0.0000 % | – | – |
| NL-89 | tip deflection at t = 0.5 and 1 / reaction | +0.0285 / +0.0287 / 0.0000 % (smaller) | – | – |
| NL-90 | tip u_y / u_x / rotation / reaction beam 2 | −0.01 / +0.02 (smaller) / +0.01 / 0.00 % | – | – |
| NL-90b | rotation about X / reaction moment root 1 / root 2 | 0.00 / 0.00 % / 0 N m | – | – |

- **Interpretation**:
  1. Through the whole bridge the route of the fork gives the same numbers as the fork
     (to ~1e-6 relative: NL-81 tip x +0.0112 against +0.0113 %; the fork computed NL-81
     with 40 equal substeps, here Mecway's 20 steps).
  2. `POU_D_T_GD` is a linear element with one integration point: the section force is
     one value per element, at the middle; the nodal value is the mean of the two
     elements. Against statics in the deformed state it is correct to 0.01 % (NL-83), in
     the co-rotating axes of the cross-section (at the tip N = P sin φ).
  3. Line3 and the orientation card change nothing in the kinematics (NL-84, NL-85 equal
     to NL-81); the card decides which I counts.
  4. Mecway 34 computes nonlinear beams with CCX only (its own solver gave no solution
     headless). With a tip force (NL-83) and the column (NL-82) the bridge is within
     0.02 % of Mecway/CCX; CCX did not get the end moment of NL-81 beyond t = 0.55 s
     (on the circular arc up to there).
  5. A flexible joint with U, V and W free only ties DX, DY and DZ and is therefore exact
     at any rotation (NL-90, NL-90b). A joint with only some rotations free stops with an
     ERROR beforehand with `POU_D_T_GD` (KNOWN_BUGS 106).
  6. Not in these cases (WARNING in the bridge, KNOWN_BUGS 11): large imposed rotations
     (a slender beam converged up to 0.41 rad), element offset and couplings at large
     rotations (linear relations), a moment in 3D. Element failure in
     a model with `POU_D_T_GD` gives an ERROR beforehand (`CALCUL_37` after the first
     failure, [`Failure.md`](Failure.md)).

### Bolt pretension (PT)

Mecway's *Pre-tension section* ([Bolt_pretension.md](Bolt_pretension.md)): cut through
the prism, `LIAISON_DDL` to a reference node (DX = shortening), force as `FORCE_NODALE`,
length adjustment as `DDL_IMPO`; the spring element variant as two nodal forces.
Module `tests_boutvoorspanning.py` (group "Boutvoorspanning", 10 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py PT`. Mecway 34 headless with CCX (`mw_solve.py`, the same
`.liml` with the stress field, 28 October 2026), for PT-09/10 with its internal solver,
as the second reference.

| Case | Model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| PT-01 | bar 10 × 100 × 10 mm (hex20 2 × 10 × 2), frictionless at the ends and on x = 0, z = 0; force 10 kN, section at y = 50 mm | N/A = 100 MPa, reaction 10 kN, u_y(20 mm) = N·y/(EA) = 1e-5 m, u_y(80 mm) = −1e-5 m; Mecway 99.996 MPa | exact (≤ 1e-10); Mecway −0.004% (its thin element layer), displacements equal |
| PT-02 | idem, length adjustment 0.01 mm | N = EAδ/L = 2000 N (20 MPa), u_y = ±2e-6 m; Mecway 19.9992 MPa | exact; Mecway −0.004% |
| PT-05 | idem in tet4 (6 per cell), faces of layers 4..6: section at y = 55 mm inside a layer (zig-zag over faces, edges and corner nodes) | as PT-01; Mecway 100.000 MPa | exact |
| PT-03 | bolt column (10 × 10 mm) between two plates (E × 1000, ν = 0) with two sleeve columns (together 2 × the area), length 100 mm; length adjustment 0.01 mm plus 600 N upward | joint diagram: Fb = kb·kc/(kb+kc)·δ + P·kb/(kb+kc) = 1533.33 N, sleeve 933.33 N; Mecway 1531.71 N | 1531.80 N (−0.10%, plate flexibility), sleeve 933.13 N; Mecway +0.006% |
| PT-04 | idem, force 1000 N and external force 1200·t N, Nonlinear static (quasi-static, 4 steps) | force mode: Fb = 1000 N at every time (Mecway/CCX: the `*CLOAD` stays), sleeve F − P = −200 N at t = 1; Mecway 999.929 N | 1000.00 N at t = 0.5 and 1, sleeve −199.54 N; Mecway +0.007% |
| PT-06 | joint of PT-03 in Dynamic response (D31, `dyna_impl_3d`, HHT α = −0.1, Δt = 0.02 ms, 3 ms), top plate density 2e6 kg/m³ (m = 10 kg), columns 7.85 kg/m³; force 1000 N constant | mass-spring: T = 2π√(m/kc) = 0.99346 ms, top plate from 0 to 2F/kc = 5e-6 m (step from t = 0, no preload step); bolt force stays F; Mecway (CCX) 0.995693 ms, 5.0147e-6 m | T 0.99579 ms (+0.23%), peak 5.0117e-6 m (+0.23%), bolt force 1000 N; Mecway +0.01% / −0.06% |
| PT-07 | idem, length adjustment 0.01 mm constant | T = 2π√(m/(kb+kc)) = 0.81116 ms, peak 2·kb·δ/(kb+kc) = 6.6667e-6 m; Mecway 0.813096 ms, 6.66678e-6 m | 0.81328 ms (+0.26%), 6.6643e-6 m (−0.04%); Mecway +0.02% / −0.04% |
| PT-08 | idem in D30, force as formula 1000·t/0.003 N | u(3 ms) = −F/kc·(1 − sin(ωt)/(ωt)) = −2.4837e-6 m; Mecway −2.49738e-6 m | −2.4978e-6 m (+0.57%: sensitive to the period and the scheme damping of the small vibration); Mecway +0.02% |
| PT-09 | spring element variant: springs k1 = 1000 and k2 = 3000 N/m in series along X between held nodes, section on spring 1 with `force="true"`, force 0 N, length adjustment 1 mm (internal solver) | middle node −k1·δ/(k1+k2) = −0.25 mm, reaction −0.75 N; Mecway the same (it uses the adjustment whatever the mode) | exact |
| PT-10 | idem along (1, 2, 2), k2 = 4000 N/m, length adjustment 2 mm, middle node held across with oblique displacement constraints | −0.4 mm along the axis, reaction x −0.53333 N; Mecway the same | exact |

Mode attribute: Mecway 34 writes `force="true"` for the force and `adjustment="true"` for
the length adjustment (models re-saved by Mecway; with `force="false"` or without an
attribute Mecway applies the force). BoltedJoint (Samples) itself does not solve with
Code-Aster, also without the section: plate B is only held by friction contact
(`MECANONLINE9_4` in step 1, KNOWN_BUGS 43). Dynamics: the CCX deck of Mecway's D30/D31
solve has one `*DYNAMIC` step with `AMPLITUDE=STEP` and the `*CLOAD`/`*BOUNDARY` on the
pre-tension node (a formula in t as an `*AMPLITUDE`), so constant values act as a step
from t = 0, as for an ordinary load in the bridge. Spring variant: the faceid of the line
element does not matter in Mecway; in Buckling Mecway gives the tensioned spring a
geometric stiffness (cantilever beam with a pretensioned spring from base to tip: 6.991
in Mecway, 1.748 with the nodal forces in Code-Aster), so the bridge translates the
spring variant only in linear static (KNOWN_BUGS 124).

## 6. Dynamics (DY)

Mecway D31 with `<timesteps>` (output interval `timepointsincrement`). Implicit:
`dyna_impl_3d` (`DYNA_NON_LINE`, HHT or NEWMARK, `substeps_per_output` = 1, so solver
step = output interval). Explicit: `dyna_expl_3d` (`DIFF_CENT`, lumped mass, step
≤ 0.5 × the estimated critical time step, evenly distributed over the output interval).
Module `tests_dynamisch.py`, run with `python run_verificatie.py DY` (about 1 minute).

**Tolerances.** The expected deviation comes from the same time scheme, computed in
Python independently of the bridge:
- `scheme_period_ratio`: period elongation and numerical damping from the eigenvalues
  of the amplification matrix (Hughes, 2000, ch. 9; HHT: Hilber et al., 1977, with
  γ = 1/2 − α and β = (1 − α)²/4);
- `sdof_response` / `modal_step_response`: the scheme on a single-degree-of-freedom
  system or on exact eigenmodes;
- `bar_chain`: the bar as a 1D chain (consistent mass + HHT, or lumped mass + central
  differences).

Tolerance = 1.5 × the expected deviation + a margin (0.1% for period and single degree
of freedom, 0.2% for the rest), rounded to 2 digits. In addition the check "verloop
t.o.v. schema/keten" (response against scheme/chain): max |u_FEM − u_Python| / max |u|
over all output times; limit 1e-5 where the Python model is exact (DY-01, DY-03; output
in single precision, Newton `RESI_GLOB_RELA` 1e-5) and 0.2% for DY-02 (mesh error of the higher modes).

**Code-Aster behaviour the comparison needed** (measured with 17.4; not found in the
U documentation):
- The initial acceleration follows from equilibrium at t = 0: a0 = M⁻¹F(0), also
  implicit.
- HHT, first step: the term −αF₀ uses F₀ = 0, even for a load that is already on at
  t = 0. With F₀ = F, DY-01a deviates 1.5% of the amplitude, with F₀ = 0 4e-8. A step
  load therefore gets a slightly too small impulse in the first HHT step (DY-01a: peak
  −0.44% at α = −0.1 and Δt = T/20; vanishes as Δt → 0). Newmark does not have this.

### DY-01 Mass-spring system, step load

- **Purpose**: spring (`DIS_T K_T_D_L`), nodal mass (`mass2` → `POI1 M_T_D_N`), step load
  and the scheme settings.
- **Model**: line2 with Mecway *Spring* k = 1000 N/m from a fixed node to a node with
  m = kT²/4π² = 25.33 kg (T = 1 s); the mass node is free only in X.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: F = 10 N from t = 0, 5 periods. DY-01a HHT α = −0.1
  (Δt = T/20); DY-01b/c/d Newmark (trapezoidal rule) with Δt = T/10, T/20, T/40.
- **Reference**: u = F/k·(1 − cos ωt): period 2π√(m/k), maximum 2F/k (Clough & Penzien,
  1993). Period measured as the least-squares line through the crossings of F/k, peak
  with a parabola through the largest sample.
- **Result**: period +1.02% (HHT) and +3.21 / +0.81 / +0.21% (Newmark), each equal to
  the prediction of the scheme; convergence order 1.98 and 1.99 (theory 2: period error
  (ωΔt)²/12). Peak −0.44% (HHT), 0.00…−0.02% (Newmark). Response against the recursion
  of the scheme 4.5e-8.
- **Interpretation**: spring, mass, load, output times and scheme arrive in Code-Aster
  exactly; the deviations are the known period error of the scheme.

### DY-02 Cantilever, first natural period

- **Purpose**: consistent mass and stiffness of beam elements in time.
- **Model**: cantilever L = 1 m, round Ø 20 mm, steel (ρ = 7850 kg/m³), 20 line2
  (`POU_D_E`). HHT α = −0.1, Δt = T1/40, 6 periods.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: clamped; step load 10 N in Z at the tip (free
  vibration about the static position).
- **Reference**: f1 = (1.875²/2π)·√(EI/(mL⁴)) = 14.47 Hz, T1 = 0.06910 s (Blevins, 1979,
  table 8-1). Tip displacement as modal superposition Σ c_i(1 − cos ω_i t) with
  c_i = 4FL³/(EIλ_i⁴) (30 modes, Σ c_i = FL³/3EI); maximum 1.9658 × static.
- **Result**: period +0.27% (expected with HHT +0.268%), maximum −0.30% (expected
  −0.297%), response against 30 modes with HHT 2.8e-6.
- **Interpretation**: beam stiffness and mass are correct; the deviation is the HHT
  period error. Rotary inertia and mesh error are negligible here.

### DY-03 Longitudinal wave in a bar

- **Purpose**: density, lumped mass, critical time step and step division,
  `FORCE_FACE` and a prescribed velocity in explicit dynamics.
- **Model**: bar L = 1 m, 10 × 10 mm, 100 × 1 × 1 hex8 (h = 10 mm), steel with ν = 0
  (the motion is then exactly 1D, c = √(E/ρ) = 5172 m/s). `deformation` = `PETIT`: with
  the default (`GREEN_LAGRANGE` for solids) the response deviates 1.4e-4…6.9e-4 from the
  linear chain (strain ~5e-5), which is physical and not an error.
- **Boundary conditions and load**: x = 0 fixed. DY-03a explicit, step load
  F = 1000 N at x = L; DY-03b explicit, prescribed velocity v = 0.25 m/s at x = L
  (output every 5 µs, end 600 µs = 3.1 L/c, 6 solver steps per output interval); DY-03c
  implicit HHT, step load, Δt = 10 µs (T/77), end 2.4 ms.
- **Reference** (Graff, 1975): behind the front σ = F/A resp. ρcv; particle velocity
  F/(ρcA); arrival at the clamp after L/c; reflection at the fixed end doubles the
  reaction; tip displacement vt up to 2L/c, maximum 2FL/EA; period of the fixed-free bar
  4L/c.
- **Result**: DY-03a: tip velocity +0.21%, stress +0.09%, arrival +0.90% (the front lags
  ~1 element because of dispersion), reaction −0.06%, maximum −0.58%. DY-03b: stress
  +0.08%, reaction −0.04%, prescribed u and v exact. DY-03c: period +0.23%, tip velocity
  +0.76%, maximum −2.67% (HHT damps the high harmonics that form the kink of the
  triangular wave). All three follow the 1D chain to 4e-8.
- **Interpretation**: the explicit and implicit translation is exact; converting a
  prescribed velocity into an acceleration (`FORMULATION='ACCELERATION'`) works.
  Implicit gives a smoother wave front on the same mesh (peak −2.7% against −0.6%
  explicit) with ~12× fewer steps.

### DY-20 Explicit: frictionless and constraint equation as imposed motion

- **Goal**: in explicit dynamics `FORMULATION='ACCELERATION'` also makes `FACE_IMPO`,
  `LIAISON_OBLIQUE` and `LIAISON_DDL` impose the acceleration; the bridge converts those
  values (`_explicit_kinematics`), as in DY-03b.
- **Models**: DY-20a = DY-03b with frictionless on the end face and normal displacement
  −v·t (positive = into the face; `FACE_IMPO DNOR`). DY-20b = the same bar without load,
  a constraint equation 1·ux = C (C = 1e-5 m) on each node of the end face. DY-20c =
  flat plate 0.2 × 0.1 m, 4 × 2 quad4 (DKT), frictionless on the whole shell face with
  normal displacement −v·t (`LIAISON_OBLIQUE DX`).
- **Reference**: DY-20a as DY-03b; DY-20b u = C from the first solver step (the
  constant becomes a shock, like a prescribed displacement without a time history);
  DY-20c rigid motion |u| = v·t, |v| = 0.25 m/s.
- **Result**: DY-20a the same numbers as DY-03b (stress +0.08%, reaction −0.04%, u and
  v exact, 1D chain 4.7e-8). DY-20b: u = C at all output times (2.5e-8 relative); with
  the previous version 1.8e-12 m (the constant acted as an acceleration). DY-20c: u and
  v exact.
- **Interpretation**: the conversion works for all imposed motions. Mecway allows a
  non-zero constant in a constraint equation only in static analyses (manual §9.42);
  the bridge translates it anyway, with a WARNING.

### DY-70 .. DY-73 Temperature in dynamics

- **Purpose**: thermal strain in `DYNA_NON_LINE` (KNOWN_BUGS 94, fixed: the constant field from
  t = 0 gave no thermal stress, alarm `MECANONLINE2_97`) and leaving it out in D30.
- **Model**: bar 10 × 10 mm, hex8, steel (ρ = 7850 kg/m³), α = 1.2e-5 1/K,
  Tref = 293.15 K, `temperature2` on all nodes. DY-70: L = 0.2 m, 8 elements, `fixed` at
  x = 0, u_x = 0 at x = L, +50 K constant, 1 N transverse at x = L, 2 ms (the reproduction of
  KNOWN_BUGS 94, fixed). DY-71: L = 0.1 m, 4 elements, free (symmetry planes), table from Tref to
  +100 K in 10 ms, output every 0.5 ms. DY-72: same, +100 K constant, ν = 0, NEWMARK, step
  T/40 of the axial period T = 4L/c = 77 µs, 3T. DY-73: DY-72 as D30 with 1 N axial.
- **Reference**: DY-70 the same model static (S30, `MECA_STATIQUE`): mean σ_xx over the
  cross-section x = L/2 = −1.32502e8 Pa (5% above −E·α·ΔT because `fixed` prevents the
  transverse expansion, coarse mesh). DY-71 quasi-static α·ΔT(t)·L (the heating lasts 130
  axial periods). DY-72 a step load F = E·A·α·ΔT on the free end: maximum 2·α·ΔT·L,
  vibration about α·ΔT·L (Chopra, 2012, §4.3). DY-73: Mecway 34 ignores *Temperature* in
  D30 (headless, also with a time table). Tolerance 1% (DY-70/71), 3% (DY-72),
  1e-3·α·ΔT·L (DY-73).
- **Result**: DY-70 −0.18% / −0.10% (t = 0.2 / 2 ms; with the old translation σ_xx ≈ 0 and
  alarm `MECANONLINE2_97`). DY-71 −0.002% / −0.06% (t = 5 / 10 ms). DY-72 maximum −1.5%,
  mean −1.4%. DY-73 max |u_x| = 9e-9 m (only the 1 N).
- **Interpretation**: in D31 the temperature now acts as in Mecway's quasi-static L30: a
  constant temperature is a step load from the first step, a table is followed. Mecway
  does not solve D31 headless (own solver: no solution, CCX: an empty solution), so not
  compared with Mecway. The maximum of DY-72 is slightly below 2·α·ΔT·L because of the
  dispersion of 4 hex8 (with HHT α = −0.1 and ν = 0.3: −3.6%).

## 7. Modal (MO) and buckling (BU)

- **Modal**: Mecway V30 with `maxmode` → `modal_3d`: `ASSEMBLAGE` (`RIGI_MECA`,
  `MASS_MECA`, supports as `CHARGE`) and `CALC_MODES` `PLUS_PETITE`,
  `NORM_MODE='MASS_GENE'`; without supports `CENTRE` around −f_s (see below).
- **Buckling**: Mecway B30 → `buckling_3d`: `MECA_STATIQUE` with the loads →
  `SIEF_ELGA` → `RIGI_MECA` + `RIGI_GEOM` → `CALC_MODES TYPE_RESU='MODE_FLAMB'`
  `PLUS_PETITE`; buckling factor = `CHAR_CRIT`.

Modules `tests_modaal.py` (MO, 12 cases; MO-09 in `tests_scheve_opleggingen.py`) and `tests_knik.py` (BU, 7 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py MO BU` (about 1 minute). User documentation:
[Modal_and_buckling.md](Modal_and_buckling.md). Tolerances: discrete models and beams
0.01–0.2% (an Euler beam with 20 elements deviates < 0.002%); mesh series as in §4 with
p = 2. Plate edges (MO-03, BU-04): DZ = 0 and the rotation about the edge normal zero
("hard", as LS-03); MO-03 also holds DX = DY = 0 (no membrane modes), BU-04 only DX = 0
on x = 0 and DY = 0 at one corner, so the stress is purely uniaxial.

| ID | Purpose and model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| MO-01 | cantilever L = 1 m, 0.02 × 0.03 m, 20 `POU_D_E`, 6 modes | Euler-Bernoulli, λL = 1.8751 / 4.6941 / 7.8548 in both planes (Blevins, 1979, table 8-1) | all 6 frequencies < 0.002%; tip mode 1 = 2/√m (`MASS_GENE`) exact |
| MO-02 | the same beam, hex20 50 × 2 × 3 and 100 × 4 × 6 | idem | f1 +0.20% / +0.13%; the remaining 0.13% is 3D against Euler (clamp, lateral contraction); f3 −0.05% (shear, rotary inertia) |
| MO-03 | simply supported plate 0.6 × 0.4 m, t = 4 mm, DKT 12 × 8 / 24 × 16 / 48 × 32 | Kirchhoff f_mn = π/2·(m²/a² + n²/b²)·√(D/ρt) (Leissa, 1969) | 4 modes, order 1.93–2.00; finest mesh −0.05 … −0.12% |
| MO-04 | free square plate 0.4 m, t = 4 mm, DKT 24 × 24, 7 modes | 6 rigid modes; mode 7 ωa²√(ρt/D) = 13.468 (ν = 0.3; Leissa, 1969) | \|f1…f6\| < 6e-4 Hz; f7 +0.01% |
| MO-05 | mass-spring chain ground–k–m–k–m (*Spring* + `mass2`) | ω² = (k/m)·(3 ∓ √5)/2 | exact (< 1e-6) |
| MO-06 | free block 1 × 0.1 × 0.05 m from (1, 2, 3) m, hex20 10 × 2 × 2, 12 modes | sum of the rotational effective mass = I about the axis through the origin = m(b² + h²)/12 + m·d² (Steiner); I about the centre of mass m(b² + h²)/12 | RX/RY/RZ 524.151 / 450.754 / 256.564 kg·m² exact (< 1e-6); about the centre of mass exact; sum translation X = 1 |
| MO-07 | clamped beam as MO-02, hex20 50 × 2 × 3, 6 modes, `fields` with `stresses` and `vonmises` | Euler-Bernoulli: rotation fraction about the clamp mode 1 = 3(∫W₁x dx)²/L⁴ = 0.97069; sum over the modes → I = m(L²/3 + a²/12); σ_xx(L/2, a/2) = E·(a/2)·W₁''(L/2)/√(ρAL) (mass-normalised); Mecway headless | rotation mode 1 / 2 −0.03 / −0.02%; sum 6 modes −0.15%; σ_xx +0.43%; against Mecway σ_xx +0.01%, fractions 0.0000% |
| MO-08 | NAFEMS Challenge Problem 5: two line3 truss elements (1-6-2 and 3-4-5, node 5 tied to node 2 with constraint equations), steel, D = 10 mm, uz = 0 everywhere, 2 modes (GitHub issue #6) | NAFEMS CP5: 451.1 / 1049.9 Hz; Mecway 34 itself 450 / 1030 Hz | 451.15 / 1049.9 Hz (+0.01 / 0.00%) |
| MO-10 | free bar 0.8 × 0.05 × 0.05 m of two hex20 blocks (8 × 2 × 2 each, own nodes at x = 0.4 m) with bonded contact and no support, 8 modes (GitHub issue #9) | 6 rigid modes; mode 7 Euler-Bernoulli free-free (βL)² = 22.373 (Blevins, 1979, table 8-1), 415.35 Hz; Timoshenko about −1.5% (L/h = 16) | \|f1…f6\| < 1e-3 Hz; f7 409.78 Hz (−1.34%); before: `ALGELINE3_81` |
| BU-01 | clamped column, 20 `POU_D_E`, compression 1000 N | Pcr = (2i−1)²π²EI/(4L²), both planes (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961) | factors 10.363 / 23.317 / 93.268 exact; tip mode 1 = 1/√(EI(π/2L)⁴L/2) (φᵀKφ = 1) exact |
| BU-02 | pinned column, idem | Pcr = i²π²EI/L² (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961) | < 0.002% |
| BU-03 | clamped column hex20 50 × 2 × 3 / 100 × 4 × 6, `traction` on the top | Euler | +0.20% / +0.13% (same model error as MO-02) |
| BU-04 | simply supported square plate 0.4 m, t = 4 mm, line load 1 N/m in X, DKT 8² / 16² / 32² | σ_cr·t = kπ²D/b², k = 4 (m = 1) and 6.25 (m = 2) (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961) | order 2.00; finest mesh +0.09% / +0.25% |
| BU-40 | column of BU-01 with a constant compression P_d = 0.5 P_cr,1 (own selection, `#ASTER-CONSTANT`) plus a variable 1000 N ([Modal_and_buckling.md](Modal_and_buckling.md) §2.2.1) | λ_k = (P_cr,k − P_d)/P (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961; Aubry, 2019, §16.2) | factors 5.1815 / 18.135 / 88.087 (< 0.002%); checked by hand: with P_d = 1.5 P_cr,1 the job stops with the ERROR (INFO_MODE: 1 factor in [0, 1]) |

**Effective mass and stresses per mode** ([Modal_and_buckling.md](Modal_and_buckling.md)
§1.4, §1.5, §2.1): the bridge computes the rotations (about the global axes through
the origin, as Mecway) itself; the checks are in MO-01, MO-04, MO-06 and MO-07.
References: the effective mass of the Euler-Bernoulli cantilever follows from the
normalised shapes W_i (Blevins, 1979, table 8-1) as (∫W_i·R dx)² / (L·∫R² dx), with
R = 1 (translation: 0.6131 / 0.1883 / 0.0647) or R = x (rotation about the clamp:
0.97069 / 0.02472 / 0.00315; own numerical integration in `tests_modaal.py`).
Tolerance 0.2% (beam elements) and 1% (solid, sum over the modes).
- MO-01 (beam): translation Y mode 1 0.61308 (Code-Aster) and rotation Z mode 1 / Y
  mode 2 0.97069 exact (< 0.001%); Mecway headless 0.97071. Sum of rotation Z over 6
  modes −0.15% against m·L²/3 (3 modes in this plane: analytically 0.99856).
- MO-04 (free plate, DKT): sum over the 7 modes of X, Z, RX and RZ = 1 (< 1e-6; the
  rigid modes contain every rigid motion, also with the rotational degrees of freedom
  of the shell); J_Z about the origin = m·2a²/3 (< 0.001%).
- Translations unchanged: the own computation of the translations must equal
  Code-Aster's `MASS_EFFE_UN_D*` in every run (≤ 1e-4); met in all MO runs (beam,
  shell, solid, spring + `mass2`).
- Stresses per mode against Mecway headless (clamped block 1 × 0.1 × 0.05 m, hex20
  10 × 2 × 2, 12 modes): displacements equal (< 1e-6), σ_xx outside the first 20% of
  the length ≤ 1% (at mid-length for modes 1 and 2 ≤ 0.11%); at the clamp and for the
  shear stresses (2 elements over the height) up to 10–20% difference in the nodal
  value (different averaging to the nodes). Mecway's API reads the stresses per mode
  from the `.liml` filled by the bridge (`node_value` / `element_node_value` with
  `mode=`).

**Comparison with Mecway** (Mecway 33, internal solver, headless `mecway.exe … solve`,
the same `.liml` from `modellen/`):

| Case | Mode | Analytical | Bridge | Mecway |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MO-01 | 1 / 3 / 6 | 16.7103 / 104.722 / 439.836 Hz | +0.000 / +0.000 / +0.002% | +0.000 / +0.000 / +0.002% |
| MO-03 (48 × 32) | 1 / 2 / 4 | 88.782 / 170.734 / 307.322 Hz | −0.045 / −0.094 / −0.117% | +0.072 / +0.125 / +0.319% |
| MO-04 | 7 | 83.874 Hz | +0.007% | −0.024% |
| BU-01 | 1 / 2 / 3 | 10.3631 / 23.3169 / 93.2678 | +0.000% (all) | +0.000% (all) |
| BU-02 | 1 / 3 | 41.4523 / 165.809 | +0.000 / +0.001% | +0.000 / +0.001% |
| BU-04 (32²) | 1 / 2 | 303 680 / 474 500 | +0.094 / +0.248% | +0.078 / +0.317% |

Mecway samples (Samples, solved headless) against the bridge: VibratingCantileverBeam
7.07368 / 14.1474 / 44.3802 / 88.7605 / 125.082 Hz, difference < 1e-10 and shapes per
node equal (< 1e-12); VibratingTrussTower 2.90265 / 2.90268 / 7.64340 Hz equal;
VibratingFreePlate (quad8) modes 7–9 331.73 / 480.57 / 562.86 Hz against Mecway
332.65 / 480.60 / 562.87 Hz (`COQUE_3D` QUAD9 against Mecway's quad8);
VibratingCantileverSolid (8 hex20, without a Poisson's ratio in the `.liml`) 7.206
against 7.088 Hz (+1.7%) while the bridge took ν = 0.3 without a Poisson ratio; Mecway then takes ν = 0. After that fix (default ν = 0) equal: 7.08845 / 14.15294 Hz;
with ν given explicitly the two agree within 1e-8 (ν = 0: 7.08845 Hz, ν = 0.3: 7.20627 Hz); BucklingBeam 41124.699
/ 41124.699 / 371034.90 (difference < 2e-12; square cross-section, so double modes
without a unique shape) and BucklingPlate (hex20) 7.148014 / 8.600503 / 11.14455 /
11.97405 (difference < 2e-8, shapes per node equal to 4e-9). A headless Mecway script
reads `solution.modes()`, `frequency(m)` / `buckling_factor(m)` and
`node_value(.., mode=m)` from the `.liml` filled by the bridge with the same values.

**Code-Aster behaviour the translation needed** (seen with 17.4):
- `NORM_MODE='RIGI_GENE'` does not exist for `MODE_FLAMB` (`ALGELINE4_77`); the bridge
  normalises the buckling shapes itself: φᵀKφ = |CHAR_CRIT·φᵀK_gφ|, with K_g from
  `getValuesWithDescription()`.
- `CHAR_CRIT` and `MASS_EFFE_UN_D*` are not in `getAccessParameters()`, but they are in
  `RECU_TABLE`; `MASS_EFFE_UN_DRX` does not exist (`UTILITAI3_5`): the bridge computes
  the rotations itself (above).
- The Sturm check fails when a multiple mode falls on the boundary (`ALGELINE5_23`,
  BucklingBeam); after an `<E>` Code-Aster stops at the next command (`CATAMESS_6`).
  Hence `VERI_MODE STOP_ERREUR='NON'` (alarms, WARNING with an explanation).
- Free-free: shift 0 and Code-Aster's own shift (−0.01 Hz) are singular
  (`ALGELINE3_81`), so is −0.83 Hz for a thin plate, and `TRI_DIAG` with
  `MODE_RIGIDE='OUI'` as well. `CENTRE` around −f_s with (2πf_s)² =
  1e-8·max(diag K/diag M) works (MO-04, VibratingFreePlate). Always shifting does not
  work: with factor 1e-6 Sorensen found no mode for the hex20 beam (f_s = 636 Hz,
  f1 = 16.7 Hz) (`ALGELINE5_49`, `ALGELINE3_78`); hence only without supports.
  "Without supports" includes a model with only connections between parts (bonded
  contact `LIAISON_MAIL`, couplings `LIAISON_SOLIDE`/`LIAISON_RBE3`): it still has its
  rigid modes (MO-10; before `ALGELINE3_81`, GitHub issue #9).
- Sign: BucklingBeam (compression) gives `CHAR_CRIT` = +41124.7; the same column in
  tension −10.36 / −23.32 / −93.27 (Mecway itself 8.5e13; KNOWN_BUGS 46).

### Beam orientation (MO-20 … MO-29, LS-70 … LS-78, BU-20 … BU-24)

- **Goal**: check that the bridge rotates a beam cross-section exactly like Mecway, in
  modal, static and buckling: without orientation (Mecway default), with an
  *orientation vector* and with vector + *angle* (`orientationvx/vy/vz`, `angle` per
  `<elem>`; bridge: groups `BORnnnnn` with `AFFE_CARA_ELEM ORIENTATION CARA='VECT_Y'`,
  since V2026.2 for every beam, without orientation with Mecway's default V). Module `tests_balkorientatie.py`,
  `python run_verificatie.py MO-2 LS-7 BU-2` (about 2 minutes).
- **Model**: cantilever L = 1 m, 20 `POU_D_E`, steel, RectBar 40 × 10 mm (I_W = 16
  I_V), along X, Y, Z and skew (1,1,1), and an I section 100/50/10/8 mm along Y
  (vector X + 90°). Frame (MO-28, LS-78): column along Z (default), beam along X
  (vector (0,1,1) + 30°), skew member to (1, 1, 1.5) (vector Z).
- **Reference**: the V axis by hand from the rule in the Mecway manual (v33 §7.5:
  vector projected perpendicular to U, rotated right-handed about U by *angle*; without
  a vector V = Z × U, along Z V = Y), W = U × V, RectBar `recta` along V. With that:
  Euler-Bernoulli frequencies (Blevins, 1979, table 8-1) with I_V = recta·rectb³/12
  (deflection along W) and I_W = rectb·recta³/12 (along V), the direction of the tip
  displacement per mode (|u·e|/|u| = 1, tolerance 1e-4), the effective mass per
  direction (0.6131·(e·W)², MO-23/25), torsion f = √(GJ/ρI_p)/(4L) (J from Roark, 7th
  ed., table 10.7; I_p = I_V + I_W) and axial √(E/ρ)/(4L) (MO-20); static u =
  F_U·L/(EA)·U + F_V·L³/(3EI_W)·V + F_W·L³/(3EI_V)·W and σ_xx = N/A ±
  |M_V|·(rectb/2)/I_V ± |M_W|·(recta/2)/I_W at the clamp; buckling with a guide (all
  nodes fixed in one direction) Pcr = π²E·I/(4L²) with I = I_V(a·V)² + I_W(a·W)² about
  the curvature axis a (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961). In addition Mecway 34 headless on the
  same `.liml`.
- **Result**: all frequencies ≤ 0.026% from the analytical value (the torsion and
  axial modes due to the linear interpolation of 20 elements), all directions 1 to
  < 3e-7, effective mass ≤ 1e-5 absolute, static ≤ 1e-7, buckling ≤ 5e-6. Against
  Mecway: frequencies, tip displacements and buckling factors equal to ≤ 2e-7 (the frame
  as well, including the shape of modes 1–3). Rotating the section by 90° swaps the weak
  and strong axis as expected: BU-20 1.7272 → BU-21 27.635 (= 16×), BU-22 (along Z,
  default: strong axis) 27.635, BU-23 (vector X + 30°) 8.2041 = (0.75 + 0.25 × 16) ×
  1.7272.

| Case | Orientation | Analytical | Bridge | Mecway |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MO-20 | along X, default (V = Y) | f1 / f2 8.35517 (W) / 33.4207 Hz (V) | +0.000% / +0.000% | +0.000% / +0.000% |
| MO-20 | same, torsion f7 / axial f14 | 357.052 / 1293.05 Hz | +0.026% / +0.026% | equal to the bridge |
| MO-21 … MO-26 | Y, Z, skew, vector Z, skew + 30°, Z + 30° | 8.35517 / 33.4207 Hz | +0.000% | +0.000%; shapes equal |
| MO-27 | I section along Y, vector X + 90° | 32.8876 (W) / 108.253 (torsion) / 110.135 Hz (V) | +0.000 / +0.026 / +0.000% | equal (< 2e-9) |
| MO-28 | frame | – | 1.39869 / 1.67958 / 4.75549 Hz | equal (≤ 1.4e-7) |
| MO-29 | L = 0.1 m (L/h = 2.5) | 835.516 / 3342.065 Hz (no rotary inertia) | +0.000% | +0.000% |
| LS-70 … LS-77 | as MO-20 … MO-27 | u_tip, σ_xx max/min | ≤ 1e-7 | equal (≤ 1e-10) |
| LS-78 | frame | – | – | tip u and rotation ≤ 3e-8 |
| BU-20 … BU-23 | guided column, see above | 1.7272 / 27.635 / 27.635 / 8.2041 | +0.000% | +0.000% |
| BU-24 | skew, vector Z + 30°, free | 1.7272 / 15.545 / 27.635; shape 1 along W, 3 along V | ≤ 4e-6 | equal |

Findings (also measured outside the fixed cases: RectBar in 20 combinations of
direction and orientation, RectTube, UniformGeneralSection, I, T, C, L and CircularBar
along X and skew, with and without vector + 30°):
- In modal and static the bridge computes the same as Mecway in every case (relative
  ≤ 1e-7, shapes equal). Which axis carries I_V and which I_W is right for all
  sections: RECTANGLE `HY` = recta along V, `iyy` of the *UniformGeneralSection* = `IY`
  (about V), and the profiles with I about V and W as Mecway shows them (principal axes
  for L). For the CircularBar the bending modes are double, so the shape is not unique.
- Mass matrix: Mecway and `POU_D_E` include the polar inertia ρI_p (I_p = I_V + I_W)
  for torsion, but no rotary inertia of the cross-section in bending: even at L/h = 2.5
  (MO-29) the frequency is the Euler-Bernoulli value. A Rayleigh or Timoshenko beam
  would be lower there; use `POU_D_T` (setting `elem_beam`, unlike Mecway) or solid
  elements.
- Neither uses an offset shear centre (T, L, C): bending and torsion are uncoupled,
  also in buckling.
- Buckling with only axial compression equals Mecway. Differences unrelated to the
  orientation: pure torsional buckling (I section: bridge 2414 = GJ·A/I_p, Mecway 2743;
  KNOWN_BUGS 112) and buckling with bending moments in a frame (from mode 2 on;
  KNOWN_BUGS 113). In both the bridge is right, see *Buckling of beams: torsion and
  frames* below. Mecway gave no headless solution for a buckling model with an L
  section (also not on a second try); the bridge gave 135.1 there (weak principal
  axis) and 601.0 (G·J·A/I_p about the centroid; the real flexural-torsional factor of an
  angle is lower, KNOWN_BUGS 115).

### Element orientation card (MO-31, LS-79 … LS-82, BU-25, LS-31d)

- **Goal**: Mecway 34 also stores the orientation as an item in Loads & Constraints
  (*Element orientation* on an element selection): `<orientation selection="…">` with
  `<ux>`…`<vz>` and `<angleu>` (found in the fork by MishalTPC,
  github.com/mishal0488/MecwayAsterBridge). Check that the bridge uses that card exactly
  like the attributes per element. Module `tests_balkorientatie.py` (LS-31d:
  `tests_resultaatvelden.py`), `python run_verificatie.py MO-31 LS-79 LS-8 BU-25 LS-31d`.
- **Mecway (measured headless, 16 Oct 2026)**: a card gives the same displacements as
  the attributes with the same vector and angle; Mecway writes the card back into its
  own solution file with all seven children (zero vector = not given, an angle without
  unit in rad) and does not convert it to attributes. A formula
  (`unit=""`/`positionunit`) applies per element at its centre. Mecway does not solve
  with an element that has an orientation from a card and the attributes or from two
  cards, with a U axis on beams and with a V axis or angle on shells; a beam card
  without a V vector gives a singular solution; a card on a component name has no
  effect. The bridge gives a WARNING there (with overlap the card wins, of two the last
  one) or an ERROR.
- **Model**: as MO-25, LS-75, BU-23, LS-78 (one card per member) and LS-31c, now with a
  card instead of the attributes; LS-81: beam along X with V = (0, x, 1 − x) and angle
  30·x degrees as a formula; LS-82: card (vector Z) on elements 1–10 of 20.
- **Reference**: the analytical references of the twin cases and Mecway 34 headless on
  the card `.liml` itself.
- **Result**: all 60 checks OK. With a card the bridge gives exactly the same numbers as
  with the attributes (MO-31 = MO-25, LS-79 = LS-75, BU-25 = BU-23, LS-80 = LS-78,
  LS-31d = LS-31c, difference 0). Against Mecway: frequencies ≤ 1e-9, buckling factor
  5e-12, tip displacements and rotations ≤ 3e-8 (frame), formula and half card ≤ 6e-10.
  Since this version every beam gets Mecway's V axis explicitly as `VECT_Y` (also without
  orientation); all existing beam cases gave exactly the same result with it.

### Skew support next to an imposed displacement (MO-09, LS-83)

- **Goal**: GitHub issue #9 (Dlubal verification example 0109, natural vibrations of a
  clamped circular plate): the model stopped in Code-Aster with `ALGELINE3_81`. Its
  CalculiX export (rotated nodal systems per rim node) was reproduced in Mecway 34 with
  *Frictionless support* on the rim edges of the shells plus *Displacement* X, Y and Z = 0
  on the rim nodes: Mecway's own frequencies of that model equal those of the exported
  deck to 1e-9. Mecway reduces the overlap (manual §9.6); in Code-Aster the frictionless
  relation (radial normal) and DX = DY = 0 are linearly dependent and the matrix with
  Lagrange multipliers is singular. The bridge now drops the part of a skew relation
  (frictionless, symmetry, value 0) that an imposed displacement/rotation 0 already
  covers at a node (`bridge_mesh._trim_covered_relations`, INFO line). Module
  `tests_scheve_opleggingen.py`, `python run_verificatie.py MO-09 LS-83`.
- **Model**: plate a = 0.5 m, t = 1 mm, steel, quad8 O-grid (256 elements, 801 nodes,
  `verif_lib.diskmesh`; COQUE_3D), frictionless on the 32 rim edges and X, Y, Z = 0 on
  the 64 rim nodes (= clamped).

| ID | Load | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| MO-09 | modal, 10 modes | Dlubal 0109 (analytical, clamped Kirchhoff plate) 10.179 / 21.184 / 34.752 / 39.629 / 50.847 / 60.611 Hz (modes 1, 2, 4, 6, 7, 9; the others are the second of a pair); Mecway 34 headless on the same `.liml` | +0.01 / +0.05 / +0.11 / +0.04 / +0.24 / +0.11%; against Mecway +0.01 … +0.18% |
| LS-83 | pressure 10 Pa | w(0) = pa⁴/(64D) (Timoshenko & Woinowsky-Krieger, 1959, §62); reaction pπa²; Mecway 34 headless | w −0.01%, reaction −3e-6; against Mecway w −0.02% |

- With the model of the issue itself (Mecway's import of its `.inp`, 1728 quad8) the
  bridge gives 10.1797 / 21.1851 / 34.7532 / 39.6298 / 50.8491 / 60.6121 Hz (Mecway
  10.1796 / 21.1845 / 34.7518 / 39.6282 / 50.8458 / 60.6088 Hz).
- Mecway 34 itself does not solve a *Displacement* whose direction is a formula of x, y,
  z (internal solver and CCX: no solution, measured headless 28 Oct 2026); that route to
  a radial support therefore does not exist (KNOWN_BUGS 104).

### Buckling of beams: torsion and frames (BU-30 … BU-37)

- **Purpose**: find out who is right in the two buckling differences with Mecway
  (KNOWN_BUGS 112, 113), with a reference that does not come from a beam formulation: a
  volume (hex20) or shell model of the same structure, computed by the bridge
  (Code-Aster) and by Mecway 34 headless with its own solver and with CCX
  (`TestFiles/Tools/mw_solve.py`, 15 Oct 2026, the same `.liml`), or Code-Aster test case
  ssll105. Module `tests_knik_balk.py`, `python run_verificatie.py BU-3` (about 1 minute).
- **Theory**: `RIGI_GEOM` of `POU_D_E` (R3.08.01 §6) contains N, the moments, the shear
  forces, the torsion term N·(I_y + I_z)/A about the centroid and at the nodes the terms
  of moderate rotations (±M/2 between twist and bending rotation). Torsional buckling
  without warping: P = G·J·A/I_p, independent of the length (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961,
  §5.3 with C_w = 0); with warping (fixed-free, effective length 2L)
  P = (G·J + π²E·C_w/(2L)²)·A/I_p. An own beam code (Euler-Bernoulli, 3D) with and
  without the nodal term reproduces Code-Aster (test case ssll105a to 1e-4) and Mecway
  (L frame to 1e-4).

| ID | Model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| BU-30 | fixed column L = 1 m, I 100/50/10/8 mm, 20 `POU_D_E`, 1000 N | Euler (factors 1–3); G·J·A/I_p = 2413.87; hex20 BU-31 | factors 1–3 and torsion exact; mode 4 pure torsion; +1.2% from hex20 |
| BU-31 | the same column in hex20 (3000 el., foot fixed) | Mecway 34 on the same `.liml` (own solver and CCX 2384.28); G·J·A/I_p | equal to Mecway (< 1e-6); −1.2% from G·J·A/I_p |
| BU-32 | L frame of KNOWN_BUGS 113 (column Z, girder X, 1 m, RectBar 40 × 10 mm, 100 N) | Euler of the column; hex20 of the frame (Mecway, 200 × 8 × 4 el. per member: 44.86 / 124.12; Code-Aster 100 × 4 × 2: −53.54) | 17.272 exact; 44.53 (−0.7%), 122.44 (−1.4%), −53.04 (−0.9%) |
| BU-33 | right-angle frame ssll105 (legs 240 mm, 30 × 0.6 mm, E = 71 240 MPa, F_X = −1 N), 10 `POU_D_E` per leg | `VALE_REFE` 0.68 / −1.088; `VALE_CALC` of ssll105a | 0.6813 / −1.0901 (+0.2%); equal to ssll105a (< 1e-8) |
| BU-34 | the same frame as a strip in DKT (5 mm) | Mecway 34 (own solver 0.7036; with F_X = +1: 1.1894; CCX 0.7085 / 1.2045); beam reference 0.68 | −0.06% / −0.18% from Mecway; +3.4% from the beam |
| BU-35 | fixed column, C 100 × 50 × 6 mm, 20 `POU_D_E` | hex20 BU-36; G·J·A/I_p = 616.43 | factor 1 +0.45%; factor 2 616.43 against 393.03 (+57%, KNOWN, KNOWN_BUGS 115) |
| BU-36 | the same C column in hex20 (3600 el.) | Mecway 34 on the same `.liml` (393.0329; CCX 393.033); thin-walled flexural-torsional 386.1 | equal to Mecway; +1.8% from the theory |
| BU-37 | the L frame of BU-32 in hex20 (100 × 4 × 2 el. per member, 1616 el.), `lin_solver_resi` 1e-3 | Euler of the column; Mecway 34 hex20 200 × 8 × 4 per member (44.86 / 124.12) | 17.315 (+0.25%); 45.030 (+0.4%), 124.64 (+0.4%); equal to `RESI_RELA` -1 |

- **Torsional buckling (KNOWN_BUGS 112)**: Mecway's beam has no torsional buckling. Its
  fourth factor 2743.03 is the third flexural mode (25 × 109.72, mode 6 in the bridge);
  with J or A doubled (UniformGeneralSection) Mecway's factors stay the same. The bridge
  gives G·J·A/I_p as a multiple eigenvalue: with 20 elements the Sturm check counts 24
  factors in [109, 2757] where 6 were computed (`ALGELINE5_23`). Hex20: 2384.28 (3000 el.)
  and 2367.7 (12 000 el.), Mecway and CCX equal. That is below G·J·A/I_p despite the
  restrained warping at the foot: the exact J (finite differences on the section,
  h = 0.25 mm: 46.4e3 mm⁴, extrapolated ~46.3e3) is 1.7% below Mecway's formula
  (47.13e3), and the section distorts (at the top the flanges rotate ~10% less than the
  web). `POU_D_TG` with C_w = 4.22e-10 m⁶ (by hand in the `.comm`, `GRX` fixed at the
  foot) gives 2552.48 = the warping formula; for this stocky column the value without
  warping is closer to the volume model.
- **Frames (KNOWN_BUGS 113)**: the difference is in the out-of-plane buckling. Mecway's
  beam: L frame 19.71 (hex20 44.86), ssll105 0.4226 and with F_X = +1 0.5525 (reference
  0.68 and 1.088). Mecway's own solver and CCX on the hex20 model and on the DKT model do
  give the right values. Cause: Mecway's beam lacks the terms of moderate rotations at
  the nodes; the own beam code gives without those terms exactly Mecway's values
  (19.71 / 0.4225 / 0.5526) and with them those of Code-Aster (44.53 / 0.6813 / 1.0901).
  CCX with B32R (`.inp` run directly) gives 17.35 / 20.90 for the L frame: CCX's rigid
  knot is linear and lacks the same terms; Mecway with solver CCX gives no buckling
  solution for beams headless. Mecway gives only positive factors, and with its default
  shift 1 it skipped the factor 0.4226 in ssll105 (with shift 0.3 it did not).
- **Flexural-torsional buckling (KNOWN_BUGS 115)**: in a C section, bending about the
  strong axis couples with torsion about the shear centre. Theory (thin-walled,
  centre-line dimensions b = 47, h = 94, t = 6 mm, e = 3b²t/(6bt + ht),
  C_w = tb³h²/12·(3b + 2h)/(6b + h)): P_T = 490.5, P_x = 864.2, coupled 386.1; volume model
  (hex20) 393.03. The beam gives 616.43: too high. The bridge now warns for buckling with T, C
  and L profiles; with `elem_beam` = `POU_D_TG` 384.98 (BU-38, below).
- **Volume model of the L frame (BU-37)**: with the default `RESI_RELA` 1e-6 the static
  step stops with `FACTOR_57` (MUMPS error 5.0e-5) without a mechanism. The bridge then
  reports the value for `lin_solver_resi` (here 1e-3); with it the run completes and the
  factors equal a run with `RESI_RELA=-1` (and Mecway and CCX on this mesh). The same
  check with Code-Aster 17.4 on a hex20 cantilever 1000 × 40 × 10 mm (100 × 4 × 2, 100 N
  transverse, error 7.0e-6): *Static* with 1e-4 deflection 47.43 mm (PL³/3EI 47.62,
  −0.4%); *Modal* with prestress with 1e-4 f₁ = 8.3765 Hz (equal without prestress,
  analytical 8.355); *Buckling* in axial compression (error 1.1e-6, with 1e-5) 17.317
  (Euler 17.272). A real mechanism (foot held in X only) stops with `FACTOR_11`, with
  `solver_nprec` -1 with `FACTOR_57` and an error of 13 (loose part: 64): then the
  bridge gives the message about a singular matrix.

### Beams with warping (BU-38 … BU-42, LS-90, LS-91)
