- **Purpose**: `elem_beam` = `POU_D_TG` ([Modal and buckling §3.5.1](Modal_and_buckling.md#351-beam-with-warping-elem_beam--pou_d_tg)):
  shear centre (`EY`, `EZ`), warping constant (`JG`) and Wagner terms (`IYR2`, `IZR2`) from
  `bridge_warping.section_props` or an `#ASTER-SECTIE` line, and the rule for the warping
  `GRX` at supports (`beam_warping`). Module `tests_balk_welving.py`,
  `python run_verificatie.py BU-38 BU-39 BU-41 BU-42 LS-90 LS-91` (about 1 minute).
- **Theory**: flexural-torsional buckling i0²(P − P_b)(P − P_T) − P²·e² = 0 (Timoshenko &
  Gere, 1961, §5.3); lateral-torsional buckling of an I beam on fork supports under
  uniform moment M_cr = π/L·√(E·I_z·G·J·(1 + π²E·C_w/(G·J·L²))) (ibid. §6.2); torsion with
  restrained warping φ = T/(G·J)·(L − tanh(kL)/k), k = √(G·J/(E·C_w)) (ibid. §5.4; Trahair,
  1993). C_w of the I profile t_f·b³·h²/24 (centre lines).

| ID | Model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| BU-38 | C column of BU-35 (C 100 × 50 × 6 mm, 20 `POU_D_TG`, foot *Fixed*: warping fixed) | thin-walled theory 386.09; hex20 BU-36 393.03 | factor 2 384.98: −0.29% from the theory, −2.0% from hex20; factor 1 +0.11% |
| BU-39 | I 100/50/10/8 mm, L = 1.5 m, 30 `POU_D_TG`, fork supports (warping free), uniform moment 1000 N·m | M_cr = 28 607 N·m (Timoshenko & Gere §6.2) | 28 579 (−0.10%); the warping adds 5% |
| BU-41 | Code-Aster test case ssll103a: angle 120 × 120 × 8 mm, L = 1200 mm, 8 `POU_D_TG`, *General section* with `#ASTER-SECTIE` (values of the test) | `VALE_CALC` modes 1, 8, 19; `VALE_REFE` 692 532 | equal to `VALE_CALC` (< 1e-9); −0.20% from `VALE_REFE` |
| BU-41b | the same with Mecway's L profile 120 × 120 × 8 mm (section by the bridge) | `VALE_REFE` 692 532 | 677 178 (−2.2%; Mecway's J 1.9% below the test's, AY = AZ = 2.4 instead of 1) |
| BU-42 | T column 100 × 10 × 10 × 90 mm, L = 1 m, 20 `POU_D_TG`, foot *Fixed* | thin-walled theory (C_w = 0) 413.62 | 411.18 (−0.59%) |
| LS-90 | I cantilever 100/50/10/8 mm, L = 1 m, 20 `POU_D_TG`, T = 100 N·m at the tip, clamp (warping fixed) | φ = T/(G·J)·(L − tanh(kL)/k) = 0.022261 rad | 0.022240 (−0.09%) |
| LS-90b | the same with `beam_warping` = `free` | T·L/(G·J) | exact |
| LS-90c | the same with the default `POU_D_E` | T·L/(G·J) | exact (unchanged) |
| LS-91 | the same beam on fork supports, T at midspan | φ = T/(2G·J)·(L/2 − tanh(kL/2)/k) = 0.0045691 rad | 0.0045591 (−0.22%) |

- **Section**: for ssll103a's angle the bridge computes `EZ` = 40.96 mm (test `EY`
  41.012), `JG` = 4.43988e-11 m⁶ (test 4.4398819e-11) and `IZR2` = 8.4848e-8 m⁵ (test
  `IYR2` 8.4948e-8): the test's y and z axes are z and y for Mecway's L profile (Mecway's
  V is the weak axis). C profile: `EZ` = 29.33 mm (centre-line formula e + x_c = 29.375),
  primary C_w 4.0135e-10 m⁶ = the closed form, plus 1.1% warping through the plate
  thickness.
- **Rule at supports**: a *Fixed support* (`BLOCAGE`) alone does not fix `GRX` (C column
  343.8 without and 385.0 with `GRX` = 0, run 28 Oct 2026); the bridge therefore adds its
  own `DDL_IMPO GRX` on the node group `BRGRX`. LS-90/LS-90b show the difference clamp/free
  (−15%), LS-91 and BU-39 that the warping stays free at a fork support.
- **Existing cases with `POU_D_TG`** (only run, not in the series): static, modal,
  buckling, nonlinear static (`PETIT`), harmonic and dynamic compute, also with line3,
  *flexible joint*, offset and shells (LS-01, LS-30, LS-50a, LS-51a/b, LS-52a/b, LS-62,
  LS-77, MO-01, MO-08, MO-27, DY-02, DY-30, MD-01, HR-02a, NK-01, NL-32, BU-01, BU-30).
  Section forces (LS-30, NL-32) exact; BU-30 gives 2553.9 with warping (see above). Nodal
  reaction forces deviate (LS-01: 103.0 N at the clamp, −3.0 N at the loaded tip, sum
  100 N; KNOWN_BUGS 123).

### Modal with prestress (MP)

Mecway V30 with loads → `modal_3d` with `modal_prestress` on (default):
`MECA_STATIQUE` → `SIEF_ELGA` → `RIGI_MECA` + `RIGI_GEOM` (+ `RIGI_ROTA` with
centrifugal force) with `COMB_MATR_ASSE` → `CALC_MODES`
([Modal_and_buckling.md](Modal_and_buckling.md) §2.5). Module
`tests_modaal_voorspanning.py` (group "Modal (prestress)", 7 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py MP`. Beam and plate as MO-01/BU-02 and MO-03/BU-04;
Pcr1 = π²EI_z/L² = 41,452 N. The Mecway values were computed headless
(`TestFiles/Tools/mw_solve.py`, Mecway 33, internal solver) on the same `.liml` from
`modellen/` and are stored in the test module. Tolerance: analytical 0.2% (beam
elements) or 1% (solid, plate); against Mecway 1%.

| ID | Purpose and model | Reference | Analytical | Mecway |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MP-01 | pinned-pinned beam, 20 `POU_D_E`, tension P = Pcr1, 4 modes | f_n = f_n0·√(1 + P/Pcr_n) (equation of test case sdll101a; f1 = √2·f10) | ≤ 0.0006% | 0.0000% |
| MP-02 | same, compression P = −0.5·Pcr1 | same (f1 = f10/√2) | ≤ 0.0008% | 0.0000% |
| MP-03 | same as hex20 50 × 2 × 4, pinned on the line z = 0 of the end faces, tension as *traction* on both end faces | same, 2 modes | −0.05 / −0.11% (3D vs Euler) | 0.0000% |
| MP-04 | cantilever beam, 20 `POU_D_E`, *centrifugal* 200 rad/s about Z | Mecway (no closed-form solution) | own FE (consistent Hermite matrices): 20.918 / 42.635 Hz | −0.52 / 0.000 / −0.09 / 0.000% |
| MP-05 | simply supported square plate 0.4 m, t = 4 mm, DKT 40 × 40, edges fixed in-plane, ΔT = 5.27 K: biaxial compression N = 0.5·Ncr | ρtω² = π⁴D(m²+n²)²/a⁴ − Nπ²(m²+n²)/a² (Navier; Ncr = 2π²D/a²) | −0.02 / −0.05 / −0.05% | −0.07 / −0.25 / −0.25% |
| MP-06 | MP-01 with `modal_prestress` off | unloaded pinned-pinned beam | ≤ 0.0007% | – |
| MP-07 | MP-04 as hex20 50 × 2 × 3 | Mecway | – | 0.0000% (4 modes) |

Observations:
- **Sign:** tension raises and compression lowers the frequencies (MP-01, MP-02,
  MP-05); `RIGI_GEOM` with coefficient +1, as in sdll101a.
- **Compression above the buckling load** (same beam, P = −2.5·Pcr1, beyond both first
  buckling loads): the bridge gives −57.45 and −23.45 Hz (negative ω²; WARNING), Mecway
  0 / 0 Hz ("non-physical modes with zero frequencies", Mecway Ltd., 2026, §6.3); the modes above
  are equal (114.899 / 239.180 Hz).
- **Spin softening:** without `RIGI_ROTA` the own FE calculation gave 38.08 Hz instead
  of 20.9 Hz in the plane of rotation for MP-04; so Mecway includes it. With `POU_D_E`
  Code-Aster is 0.5% lower in the plane of rotation (KNOWN_BUGS 49); with hex20
  (MP-07) exactly equal.
- **Plate:** Mecway's quad4 already deviates +0.25 / +0.93% at 20 × 20 without load
  (with prestress +0.39 / +1.08%; DKT −0.07 / −0.20%), hence 40 × 40.
- **Imposed displacement ≠ 0 with a load:** Mecway gives no solution (Mecway Ltd., 2026, §6.3:
  "not allowed"), the bridge an ERROR with the solution.
- **Mecway samples:** VibratingString (hex8, force 40 N) 10 modes +0.01 … +0.22%
  (Code-Aster `3D` against Mecway's incompatible hex8); VibratingMembrane (tri6,
  thermal strain, E = 0.001 Pa) fails with the lowest modes (`ALGELINE5_49`, spurious
  rotation modes of `COQUE_3D`); with search frequency 2 Hz the drum mode 2.007456 Hz
  equals Mecway (KNOWN_BUGS 49).

### Nonlinear buckling (NK)

`nonlin_static_3d` with `crit_stab` (`CRIT_STAB` in `STAT_NON_LINE`) and/or
`imperfection_*` (linear buckling mode as pre-deformation via `MODI_MAILLAGE DEFORME`;
[Modal_and_buckling.md](Modal_and_buckling.md) §5). Module
`tests_niet_lineaire_knik.py` (group "Nonlinear buckling", 5
cases), run with `python run_verificatie.py NK` (about 1.5 minutes). Model: cantilever
column L = 1 m, 0.02 × 0.03 m (weak axis Y), steel, compressive force as a time table
P(t) = t·P_end; Euler Pcr = π²EI/(2L)² = 10 363 N (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961). The test
reads the CRIT_STAB values and the imperfection from the `.mess` with the same functions
as the bridge (`bridge_results_liml.read_crit_stab`, `crit_stab_estimate`,
`read_imperfection`). Tolerances: the completion criterion of step S10c (CRIT_STAB 2% of
Euler, amplification 5%), stricter where the solution is exact (beams 0.2%, factor with
`PETIT` and the linear buckling 0.5%: the 3D model error of BU-03 is +0.2%).

| ID | Purpose and model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| NK-01 | 20 `POU_D_E` (always `PETIT`), P_end = 0.9 Pcr, 10 steps | λ(t)·P(t) = Pcr | 10 363 N at t = 0.5 and 1 (< 0.001%); equal to BU-01 |
| NK-02a | hex20 50 × 2 × 3, default (`GREEN_LAGRANGE`) | buckling point (λ linearly to 0 from the last two steps) × P_end = Pcr | 10 402 N (+0.38%); λ from −22.6 (t = 0.1) to −2.70 (t = 1) |
| NK-02b | same with `deformation` = `PETIT` | λ(t)·P(t) = Pcr | 10 384 N (+0.20%, equal to BU-03-h20-n2) |
| NK-03 | NK-02a with imperfection 0.001 L in buckling mode 1 (unit `L`), 20 steps (stepsize 0.05 s) | linear buckling of the imperfection step; total deflection e0/(1 − P/Pcr) (Timoshenko & Gere, 1961, ch. 1); Southwell (1932) | buckling factor × P_end 10 384 N (+0.20%); buckling point 10 400 N (+0.36%); w/e0 at 0.27 / 0.495 / 0.675 Pcr −0.09 / −0.21 / −0.44%; Southwell Pcr 10 384 N (+0.20%) |
| NK-04 | NK-03 bilinear plastic (fy = 25.9 MPa: Npl = 1.5 Pcr; H = 210 MPa), e0 = 5 mm, P_end = 0.8 Pcr, 40 steps (stepsize 0.025 s) | P_u between first yield (Ayrton & Perry, 1886: P/A + P·e0/((1 − P/Pcr)·W) = fy, 0.419 Pcr) and the plastic hinge on the elastic deflection ((P/Npl)² + M/Mpl = 1, 0.563 Pcr) | stop at the limit point (`MECANONLINE9_7`, expected) at P_u = 0.488 Pcr; λ_end/λ_start = 0.007 (CRIT_STAB goes to 0) |

Observations:
- **What CRIT_STAB computes** depends on the kinematics: with small displacements
  det(K_T + λK_g) = 0 (load factor; the `.mess` shows ~35 "produits B\*X" per step),
  with large displacements det(K_T + λI) = 0 ("produits B\*X = 0"), as U4.51.03 §3.18
  describes. One elset with large displacements is enough (measured with a hex20 column
  plus a separate beam, in both orders). `PETIT_REAC` gave the same factors as `PETIT`;
  `GDEF_LOG` (NK-04) behaves like `GREEN_LAGRANGE`.
- **Above the buckling load** (NK-01 model up to 1.2 Pcr): λ = 0.926 at t = 0.9; the
  perfect column stays straight, Code-Aster gives no message and continues; the bridge
  reports "Buckling reached".
- **Extrapolation** with large displacements: the series λ(t) is slightly curved; with
  the last step at 0.27 Pcr the estimate was +4.7%, at 0.9 Pcr +0.4% (KNOWN_BUGS 53).
- **Mesh restored after the analysis**: the export checks the nodes against the `.liml`
  (`_check_node_order`); after restoring with −e0·φ the difference is at round-off level
  (NK-03, NK-04 loaded without a message).
- **With element failure** (the loop of `bridge_failure`, `reuse`): the table
  `ANALYSE_MODALE` contains all steps (NK-04 model with `failure_plastic_strain` 0.02).
- **Mecway sample BucklingPlateNonlinear** (hex20, load 6 → 8 N, perturbation 0.1 mN):
  λ changes sign between t = 0.5 and 0.6, interpolated t = 0.575, load 7.15 N (theory
  7.06 N, Mecway: "7.0–7.1 N", Mecway Ltd., 2026, §11.9); afterwards λ < 0 again (stable
  post-buckling of a plate).
- **`#ASTER-OPTIE`**: the same settings per model gave the same numbers as NK-03
  ([`ASTER_lines_examples.md`](ASTER_lines_examples.md) example 10).

### Mode superposition (MD)

Mecway D30 with *Mode superposition* → `dyna_modal_3d`: `CALC_MODES` → `PROJ_BASE` →
`DYNA_VIBRA` `BASE_CALCUL='GENE'` (NEWMARK, 50 steps per period of the highest mode) →
`REST_GENE_PHYS` ([Mode_superposition.md](Mode_superposition.md)). Module
`tests_dynamisch_modaal.py` (group "Mode superposition", 10 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py MD` (about 1 minute). The Mecway values were computed
headless (`TestFiles/Tools/mw_solve.py`, Mecway 33, internal solver, Mode
superposition) on the same `.liml` from `modellen/` and are stored as a time series
(every n-th output time) in the test module. "History" is max |u − u_ref| over all
(or the stored) output times, divided by the largest |u_ref|. Tolerance: analytical
1% (peak, history), period 0.5%, against Mecway 2%.

Model MD-01 … MD-04, MD-07 and MD-08: cantilever 1 m, 10 `POU_D_E`, 20 × 30 mm, 1 kg/m³ (own
mass 6e-4 kg), 10 kg at the tip: k = 3EI/L³ = 12,600 N/m, f = 5.649 Hz; step load
100 N (u_st = 7.937 mm) in Y at the tip, 3 modes. Reference: single degree of
freedom, exact solution (Duhamel in closed form; Clough & Penzien, 1993, chapters 3
and 4).

| ID | Purpose and model | Reference | Analytical | Mecway |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MD-01 | step load, undamped, Mecway time step 0.01 s (ω·Δt = 0.355), 1 s | u = u_st(1 − cos ωt): peak 2u_st (DAF 2), T = 2π√(m/k) | peak −0.00%, period −0.00%, history 1.2e-4 | history 1.4e-5 |
| MD-02 | step load, Rayleigh α = 2ζω (ζ = 0.05) | peak u_st(1 + e^(−πζ/√(1−ζ²))); damped step response | peak −0.00%, history 2.9e-5 | history 4.2e-6 |
| MD-03 | F = 100·sin(0.8ωt) N as a table (400 points over 2 s), ζ = 0.05, time step 5 ms | exact response from rest (transient + steady state, steady DAF 2.71) | history 1.7e-3 (the table deviates ~0.2% from the sine), peak −0.17% | history 6.9e-6 |
| MD-04 | as MD-01 with the setting `modal_damping` = 0.05 (no Mecway input) | as MD-02 | peak −0.00%, history 2.8e-5 | – |
| MD-05 | cantilever 1 × 0.1 × 0.05 m, hex20 10 × 2 × 1, 6 modes, 1000 N in Z on the end face, Rayleigh β = 1e-4 s, 0.1 s, time step 0.5 ms | Mecway | – | history tip `displz` 3.8e-5, `velz` 1.4e-4, `stressxx` (mid-span, top face) 4.0e-4 |
| MD-06a | Euler cantilever, 20 `POU_D_E`, steel, point load 100 N at the tip, α = 20 1/s, 1 mode, 2 s, *keepeverynthtimestep* 10 | final displacement = 12/λ₁⁴ · PL³/3EI = 0.97069 · u_st (contribution of mode 1; Blevins, 1979, table 8-1); 201 output times | −0.00%; 201 | history 2.7e-3 |
| MD-06b | as MD-06a with `static_correction` | final displacement = PL³/3EI | +0.00% | – (Mecway has no static correction) |
| MD-07 | model MD-01 without Mode superposition, time step 0.02 s, `dyna_impl_3d` with NEWMARK and `PETIT` | the same Newmark recursion (`sdof_response`, ω·Δt = 0.71: period error +4%) | history 1.0e-4 | history 3.1e-8 |
| MD-08a, MD-08b | as MD-07 with *Generate solution every n time steps*, n = 2 and 5 (Mecway 34 headless) | the same Newmark recursion with step 0.02 s, every n-th step | history 1.0e-4 and 7.4e-5 | history 3.1e-8 |

Observations:
- **Mecway computes with *Time step size*, also with *every n time steps*** (MD-08): the
  solution with n = 2 and 5 equals the one with n = 1 at the same times (< 1e-14). The
  bridge does the same; before that fix it computed one step per output interval
  (history 1.0 and 0.86 off Mecway).
- **Mecway integrates the modal equations exactly:** time steps 0.02 s and 0.001 s give
  the same response, within 1e-4 of u_st(1 − cos ωt) (Mecway's direct Newmark is then
  58% off). The bridge uses NEWMARK with a small step and comes within 1.4e-5 of Mecway
  (MD-01).
- **No static correction in Mecway** (MD-06a: 0.97069 · u_st, with 6 modes 0.99856 =
  the sum 12/λ_i⁴ of the 3 modes in that plane), and **no prestress from the loads**
  (10 kN tension at the tip of MD-01: period unchanged in Mecway and the bridge).
- **Mecway sample DampedVibratingStrip** (quad8 shells, `COQUE_3D`, 10 modes, Rayleigh
  α = 1, β = 0.005): disply at the table node max. 1.9% difference over 10 s, peak
  −0.6%, largest `vonmisesu1` +0.9%. The shell frequencies differ from Mecway (mode
  1 / 2: −0.6 / +0.3%; KNOWN_BUGS 2), so the phase drifts over 10 s. Mode 2
  (1.1017 Hz, ω = 6.92 rad/s) gets ξ = 0.0895; the Mecway manual gives 0.08978 at
  ω = 6.91 rad/s (Mecway Ltd., 2026, §11.24).
- **Damping ratio ≥ 1** (e.g. `modal_damping` 1.2): the job stops with
  `BRIDGE_DYNA_MODAL_FOUT DEMPING`; the bridge reports cause and solution.

### Harmonic response (HR)

Separate analysis `harmonic_3d` (Mecway has no harmonic analysis; the model is a
*Frequency* analysis): `CALC_MODES` → static enrichment → `PROJ_BASE` → `DYNA_VIBRA`
`TYPE_CALCUL='HARM'` `BASE_CALCUL='GENE'` → `REST_GENE_PHYS`, or direct
`BASE_CALCUL='PHYS'` ([Mode_superposition.md](Mode_superposition.md) §7). Module
`tests_harmonisch.py` (group "Harmonic response", 15 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py HR` (about 1 minute). U = displ + i·displi at the node;
"max. deviation" is the maximum over all frequencies of the run. Tolerance (S10h
completion criterion): amplitude 1%, phase 1°, GENE vs PHYS 1%.

Model HR-01, HR-04, HR-05: spring k = 1000 N/m (line2, *Spring*) and nodal mass 2 kg
(f_n = 3.559 Hz), the mass node free in X only, force F₀ = 10 N in X (u_st = 10 mm),
1 mode. Reference: single degree of freedom U = u_st/((1 − r²) + 2iζr), r = f/f_n
(Clough & Penzien, 1993, chapter 3). Model HR-02: cantilever 1 m, 20 `POU_D_E`,
20 × 30 mm, steel 7850 kg/m³, 100 N in Y at the tip (u_st = 7.937 mm), Rayleigh α =
4.20 1/s (ζ = 0.02 in mode 1), 8 modes (4 in the Y plane: 16.71 / 104.72 / 293.2 /
574.6 Hz), 1–200 Hz (100) + 5 per mode. Reference: exact Euler-Bernoulli receptance of
the tip w(L)/F = (sin βL cosh βL − cos βL sinh βL)/(EIβ³(1 + cos βL cosh βL)), β⁴ =
ρA(ω² − iαω)/EI (derived; Bishop & Johnson, 1960; static limit L³/3EI, poles at the
natural frequencies of Blevins, 1979, table 8-1). Model HR-03: the hex20 cantilever
of MD-05 (1000 N in Z, β = 1e-4 s, 6 modes), 1–300 Hz (60) + 5 per mode.

| ID | Purpose and model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| HR-01a | single DOF, `modal_damping` 0.05, GENE, 0.5–7 Hz (27) + 5 around f_n | exact (ζ = 0.05) | \|U\| max. 5.5e-8, phase max. 7.5e-6°, Re/Im 5.1e-8; peak 0.10013 m and peak frequency f_n√(1−2ζ²) exact; \|a\| = ω²\|U\| (1e-7); 32 frequencies |
| HR-01b | same, Rayleigh α = 2ζω_n (C = αM) | exact (ζ = α/2ω_n) | as HR-01a |
| HR-01c | same, PHYS (direct, C = αM) | exact | as HR-01a |
| HR-01d | damper (Mecway *Damper*, c = 2ζ√(km), ζ = 0.2), PHYS, list with f_n | exact (ζ = 0.2) | \|U\| 3.6e-8, phase 5.8e-6°, Re/Im 4.8e-8 |
| HR-02a | beam, GENE 8 modes with static enrichment | Euler-Bernoulli exact; PHYS (HR-02b, stored) | 6.6e-4 (phase 0.04°) over 1–200 Hz, with two resonances and the antiresonance at 73 Hz; vs PHYS 1.9e-5 |
| HR-02b | same, PHYS | Euler-Bernoulli exact | 6.6e-4 (phase 0.04°): the discretisation error of 20 elements |
| HR-02c | same, GENE without enrichment | static part of the truncated modes 1 − Σ_{i≤4} 12/λ_i⁴ = 6.226e-4 | 6.222e-4 (−0.06%); vs exact 4.6e-3 up to 45 Hz and 5.2e-3 around mode 2 (95–115 Hz) |
| HR-03a | hex20 cantilever, GENE 6 modes with enrichment | PHYS (HR-03b, stored) | tip U_z 4.3e-6, von Mises at the clamp 4.3e-5, \|σ_xx\| at mid-span 2.3e-3 |
| HR-03b | same, PHYS | peak frequency f₁√(1−2ξ₁²), ξ₁ = βω₁/2 | exact (the refinement puts a point on the peak) |
| HR-04 | single DOF, amplitude from a Mecway table F(f) = 10 + 2f N, `#ASTER-FASE Last = 90`, frequencies with `#ASTER-OPTIE`, `MEETPUNT_Massa` | exact with F(f) and phase +90° | \|U\| 2.5e-8, phase 3.6e-6°; CSV = nodal value |
| HR-05 | two forces on the mass: 10 N phase 0 and 4 N with `#ASTER-FASE Last2 = 180` | exact with 6 N | \|U\| 2.8e-8, phase 1.9e-6° |
| HR-06a | single degree of freedom without viscous damping, loss factor `loss_factor` 0.04 (spring: `AMOR_HYST` in `DISCRET`), GENE, range + refinement | exact: U = u_st/((1 − r²) + iη) (Aubry, 2023, §3.3.4); peak u_st/η | \|U\| 4.3e-8, phase 6.8e-6°; peak 0.24995 m (−0.02%, the point lies just beside f_n) |
| HR-06b | same with `#ASTER-HYST Veer = 0.04`, PHYS, list with f_n | exact | \|U\| 3.8e-8, phase 6.8e-6°; \|U(f_n)\| = 0.25000 m = u_st/η, phase −90.00° |
| HR-06c | beam of HR-02 without Rayleigh, `loss_factor` 0.02, GENE 8 modes with enrichment (`PROJ_MATR_BASE` of `RIGI_MECA_HYST`) | Euler-Bernoulli exact with E(1 + iη); peak (12/λ₁⁴)·u_st/η | 2.1e-4 (phase 0.012°) over 1–200 Hz; peak 0.99997 × (12/λ₁⁴)·u_st/η |
| HR-06d | same with `#ASTER-HYST Staal = 0.02`, PHYS | Euler-Bernoulli exact with E(1 + iη) | 2.1e-4 (phase 0.012°) |

Observations:
- **Phase convention** (measured in a prototype on the hex20 cantilever): `PHAS_DEG`
  = φ gives F·e^{iφ}, U = F·e^{iφ}/(k − mω² + iωc) and u(t) = Re(U·e^{iωt}); far below
  the resonance in phase, at the resonance −90°, above it −180° (HR-01).
- **Modal truncation:** without enrichment the modal basis misses the static part of
  the higher modes, a constant 6.2e-4·u_st (HR-02c, exactly Blevins' series). Where
  the response is small (antiresonance, above mode 2) the relative error becomes large
  (64% at the antiresonance, 7% at 200 Hz). With the static enrichment (like
  `DYNA_LINE` `ENRI_STAT`, on by default) GENE equals PHYS (HR-02a, HR-03a). If the
  static shape already lies in the modes (single DOF), `DEFI_BASE_MODALE` drops it
  ("Nombre de modes propres limité à 1").
- **Refinement:** `DEFI_LIST_FREQ` `LARGEUR_3DB` with 5 points centres on
  f_n√(1−2ζ²), the peak of \|U\|: peak and peak frequency exact (HR-01a, HR-03b).
- **Table as a function of frequency:** `FONC_MULT` with a `DEFI_FONCTION` of `FREQ`
  works in GENE and PHYS (HR-04).
- **Mecway:** no harmonic analysis, so no comparison with a Mecway solution. Mecway's
  API reads the result of HR-02a (headless `mecway.exe <model> script <script.py>`:
  120 frequencies, `node_value` of the displacement fields equal to the bridge); the
  GUI display is NOT checked (KNOWN_BUGS 54).

### Modal with damping: complex modes (MC)

Mecway V30 with Rayleigh damping and/or dampers and the setting `modal_complex` →
`CALC_MODES` with `MATR_AMOR` ([Modal_and_buckling.md](Modal_and_buckling.md) §6).
Module `tests_modaal_demping.py` (group "Modal with damping", 7 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py MC` (about 15 s). Checked per mode: the damped frequency f_d
(`<mode frequency>`), ζ (`AMOR_REDUIT`) and f_n = |s|/2π from the `BRIDGE_MODE`
lines. Tolerance (S10f completion criterion): 1% for ζ and f_d; f_n against Mecway
0.1%; MC-03 0.1%.

Model MC-01/02: ground — spring k = 1000 N/m (*Spring*, line2) — 2 kg (*mass*) —
damper (*Damper*, line2) — ground, along X (mass free in X only): f_n = √(k/m)/2π =
3.5588 Hz. Reference: single degree of freedom system (Clough & Penzien, 1993,
chapter 2).

| ID | Purpose and model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| MC-01a/b | damper c = 2ζ√(km), ζ = 0.1 and 0.5 | ζ; f_d = f_n√(1 − ζ²) = 3.5410 / 3.0820 Hz; f_n; real shape | ζ, f_d and f_n ≤ 1.3e-15; ‖Im‖/‖Re‖ ≤ 4e-17 |
| MC-02a | no damper, Rayleigh α = 2ζω_n (ζ = 0.2): C = αM on the point mass | ζ = α/(2ω_n); f_d = 3.4869 Hz | ≤ 1.3e-15 |
| MC-02b | same with β = 2ζ/ω_n: C = βK on the spring | ζ = βω_n/2 | ≤ 1.3e-15 |
| MC-03 | ground — k₁ = 2000 — m₁ = 2 kg — k₂ = 1000 — m₂ = 1 kg — c = 30 N·s/m — ground (non-proportional) | roots of (m₁s² + k₁ + k₂)(m₂s² + cs + k₂) − k₂² = 0 (Durand-Kerner; Kerner, 1966): f_d = 3.2945 / 6.6902 Hz, ζ = 0.48765 / 0.081509 | ≤ 1.1e-14; complex shape (‖Im‖/‖Re‖ = 0.16 / 0.36) |
| MC-04 | hex20 cantilever 1 × 0.1 × 0.05 m (10 × 2 × 1, as MD-05), 6 modes, Rayleigh α = 20 1/s, β = 1e-4 s | ζ_n = α/(2ω_n) + βω_n/2 and f_d = f_n√(1 − ζ_n²) with f_n from Mecway (headless; Mecway ignores the damping): ζ = 0.0509 … 0.2308 | ζ ≤ 2e-11, f_d ≤ 8.4e-11; f_n against Mecway ≤ 8.4e-11 |
| MC-05 | as MC-04 without the clamp (free-free), 10 modes requested | the 6 rigid body modes are real with damping (s = 0, s = −α) and drop out; elastic modes = Mecway's modes 7–10 | 6 modes (alarm `ALGELINE5_79`); ζ ≤ 3e-11, f_n against Mecway ≤ 3e-11 |

Observations:
- **Mecway** (headless) gives for V30 with Rayleigh damping the same frequencies as
  without (5.6494 / 8.4741 / 564.94 Hz, mass on a beam) and with a *Damper* no
  solution; the bridge's f_n is therefore equal to Mecway's frequency (MC-04/05).
- Code-Aster's `FREQ` is the **damped** frequency and `AMOR_REDUIT` = ζ = −Re(s)/|s|
  (MC-01b: ζ = 0.5 gives 3.0820 Hz = 0.866·f_n).
- With Rayleigh damping the shape is real and after normalisation equal to the real
  mass-normalised mode (measured with a tip mass on a beam: difference 2e-9); with a
  damper on one mass (MC-03) the shape is complex.
- `TRI_DIAG` (default), `QZ` and `SORENSEN` with `CENTRE` (search frequency) gave the
  same f_d and ζ (≤ 1e-9); `SORENSEN` with `PLUS_PETITE` stops (`ALGELINE2_71`).
- With β = 1e-3 s on MC-04 (ζ ≥ 1 from mode 4 on) Code-Aster gave 3 of the 6 modes
  with `ALGELINE5_79`; the bridge reports the cause (overdamping).

### Cyclic symmetry (CY)

Mecway V30 with *Cyclic symmetry* → `MODE_ITER_CYCL` on the sector
([Modal_and_buckling.md](Modal_and_buckling.md) §7). Module `tests_cyclisch.py` (group
"Cyclic symmetry", 6 cases, 171 checks), run with `python run_verificatie.py CY`
(approx. 2 min; CY-02 and CY-06 read the whole wheel of CY-01 and CY-05 from the work
folder, so run those first). Mode k of ND d is mode d·maxmode + k (Mecway's order). References: the
**full model** (the sector N times, the same mesh, ordinary modal route; CY-02,
CY-06), **Mecway 33** headless with its own cyclic symmetry (internal solver, same
`.liml`), and analytical: thin ring in its plane f_n = 1/(2π)·√(EI/(ρAR⁴))·n(n² −
1)/√(n² + 1) (Blevins, 1979, table 9-2) and the clamped circular plate (Leissa, 1969,
table 2.1). Tolerance (done criterion S10g): full model and Mecway 1%, analytical 2%;
rigid body modes |f| ≤ 0.05 Hz. Steel E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3, ρ = 7800 kg/m³.

**The whole wheel** (S10i, setting `cyclic_full_wheel`, on by default): every cyclic
case checks that each mode is on all N × n nodes (Mecway's own solution has as many)
and that the shape is continuous across the cut faces (coincident nodes of
neighbouring sectors equal, relative ≤ 1e-6). CY-02 and CY-06 compare the shape of
every mode of the wheel of CY-01 and CY-05 with the full model: the **subspace MAC**
‖P u‖²/‖u‖² with P the projection onto the modes of the full model within the
frequency band of the mode (relative 1e-4, rigid body modes < 0.05 Hz; a pair or a
double frequency gives more than one mode, because which standing wave of a pair you
get is arbitrary). Done criterion S10i: 1 − MAC ≤ 1e-3 (MAC ≥ 0.999).

| ID | Purpose and model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| CY-01 | free ring R = 0.5 m, radial 0.01 × axial 0.03 m, sector 30° (12 segments) about Z, 6 hex20, 2 modes per ND (ND 0 … 6) | Blevins (first mode of ND n = in-plane bending, n = 2 … 6); full model; Mecway; whole wheel | Blevins +0.59 … +1.18% (thin ring theory, t/R = 0.02; the full model deviates as much); full model and Mecway ≤ 2e-6; ND 0/1: rigid body modes ≤ 0.005 Hz; wheel 960 nodes, cut faces ≤ 3.7e-15 |
| CY-02 | the same ring complete (72 hex20), 40 modes, ordinary modal route | Blevins; shapes of the whole wheel of CY-01 | +0.59 … +1.18% (the same values as CY-01); 1 − MAC ≤ 5.1e-7 (14 modes, rigid body modes 0) |
| CY-03 | CY-01 with the axis along X (Mecway `axis="1"`) | as CY-01 | bit-identical to CY-01 (the bridge rotates the model to Z and back); wheel 960 nodes, cut faces ≤ 3.7e-15 |
| CY-04 | clamped circular plate a = 0.5 m, h = 0.01 m, 6 segments (16 × 6 elements: hex20 + wedge15 in the centre), nodes on the axis (interface `AXE`) | Leissa (0,1), (0,2), (1,1), (2,1), (3,1); Mecway; whole wheel | Leissa +0.58 … +0.66% (mesh; Mecway gives the same); Mecway ≤ 0.03%; wheel 4530 nodes, cut faces ≤ 9e-15, on the axis 6.2e-4 (Mecway's own solution 9.4e-4, criterion 2e-3) |
| CY-05 | rotating disc about Y (Mecway `axis="2"`), hub r = 0.1 m clamped, a = 0.5 m, h = 0.01 m, 3000 r/min, 12 segments | full model (CY-06); Mecway; whole wheel | ≤ 0.0012% and ≤ 0.0012% (prestress: stress stiffening + spin softening; pairs with a fixed hub node); wheel 1524 nodes, cut faces ≤ 1.3e-14 |
| CY-06 | the same disc complete (144 hex20), 30 modes | Mecway; shapes of the whole wheel of CY-05 | ≤ 1e-6; 1 − MAC ≤ 2.1e-8 (14 modes) |

Observations:
- **Mecway** (headless): `axis` 1 = X, 2 = Y, 3 = Z (a ring sector about Z gave the
  frequencies of the full ring with 3, not with 1 and 2); per ND *Number of modes*
  modes, in ND order; a pair of standing waves once; the solution has the whole wheel
  (its own nodes and elements in `<solution>`). A slave at −360°/N works too (the
  bridge determines the direction). Mecway's API reads the `.liml` with the bridge's
  modes (headless script: `modes()` 1 … 14, `frequency` and `node_value` equal to the
  bridge).
- **Format of the whole wheel** (S10i; headless `mw_solve.py` on CY-01, CY-04, CY-05,
  Mecway's Impeller and three variants of CY-01: two element sets with interleaved
  element numbers, a loose node, master and slave swapped): node i of sector j = i +
  j·n, rotated by +j·360°/N (right-handed, also with swapped master/slave), element e
  + j·ne, per element set the sectors one after another, all nodes copied (also on
  the cut faces and the axis). The bridge writes the same: the bridge's model copy in
  `<solution>` (fragment of CY-01, and the `<results>` of Mecway's own solutions of
  the variants and the Impeller spliced in with `splice_results`) has the same node
  numbers in the same order, the same elements and element set order, and
  coordinates ≤ 1.1e-15 m from Mecway's (only the 15th digit can differ). Mecway's API
  (headless `mecway.exe <model> script`) reads the bridge's wheel like its own: 960
  nodes, 72 elements, 14 modes, `node_value` on all nodes, `solution.volume` and
  `interpolate` in an element of the last sector; likewise Mecway's Impeller through
  the bridge (17 sectors about Y, 9996 nodes, 1479 elements, 27 modes). Because the
  numbering is the same, the shapes can be compared node by node with Mecway's own
  wheel: ND 0 of CY-05 MAC 1.000000, of the Impeller 0.99958 … 0.99994 (the elements
  differ slightly, KNOWN_BUGS 62); for a pair or a double frequency Mecway and
  Code-Aster choose a different standing wave (low MAC per mode, expected). The Mecway
  GUI has NOT been looked at.
- **Results... with an X or Y axis** (CY-05, Mecway's IronPython): the run's `.mail`
  is in the solver frame (axis → Z); `check_results_fit` rotates the coordinates back
  (`bridge_cyclic.to_model_frame`) and accepts the result of CY-05 (before the fix:
  "Node 109 is in a different position in the model than in the run (difference
  0.5)"). A node moved by 0.01 m in the model is still refused.
- **Negative check** of the MAC: a wheel without phase (sector 0 only rotated to every
  sector) gave 1 − MAC = 1.000 for ND 2, 3 and 4 of CY-01; the check thus
  distinguishes the phase of the sectors.
- **Basis**: with the default (20 sector modes) the lowest modes per ND equal the full
  model; the 4th mode of ND 1 of the ring was 1658.7 / 1657.5 / 1663.8 / 1656.7 Hz with
  10/20/40/80 sector modes (Mecway 1656.6): the highest modes per ND depend on the
  basis.
- **Double eigenvalue in ND 0**: the ring mode with n = 12 waves falls in ND 0 (Mecway
  1402.72 Hz, twice). `MODE_ITER_CYCL` gave 1420.8 Hz (+1.3%) for the second one, not
  better with 80 sector modes and `PREC_AJUSTE` 1e-12; the double eigenvalue in ND 6
  (333.089 Hz) was fine. Hence CY-01/03 compute 2 modes per ND (KNOWN_BUGS 61).
- **8 segments with nodes on the axis**: `<EXCEPTION> ALGORITH14_95` (Code-Aster
  `ctetax.F90`: cos 45° − sin 45° = 0); not with 6 and 12 segments. The bridge then
  stops with an ERROR (KNOWN_BUGS 61); CY-04 uses 6 segments.
- **Mecway's sample Impeller** (17 segments about Y, 100 000 r/min, 66 hex20 + 21
  wedge15; not in the suite, the file belongs to Mecway): the 27 modes (ND 0 … 8)
  +0.01 … +0.92% against Mecway, in the same order; without centrifugal force +0.01 …
  +0.92%, and the sector **without** cyclic symmetry (ordinary modal route) +0.02 …
  +0.79%: a difference in the (strongly curved) elements, not in the cyclic route
  (KNOWN_BUGS 62). With 60 instead of 20 sector modes mode 1 changed by 1e-5.

### Acoustics: cavity modes (AK)

Mecway A30 (*Acoustic Resonance 3D*) → `acoustic_modes_3d`: `PHENOMENE='ACOUSTIQUE'`,
`RIGI_ACOU`/`MASS_ACOU`, `CALC_MODES` ([Acoustics.md](Acoustics.md) §2). Module
`tests_akoestiek.py` (group "Acoustics", 7 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py AK`. Air c = 340 m/s; pipe 1 × 0.1 × 0.1 m (first cross mode
1700 Hz). The Mecway values were computed headless (`mw_solve.py`, Mecway 33, internal
solver) on the same `.liml` and are in the test module; also the largest ∣p∣ of mode 1
and 2 (normalisation). Tolerance: analytical 0.5% (S2 done criterion), against Mecway
1% (frequency) and 0.1% (∣p∣).

| ID | Model | Reference | Analytical (largest deviation) | Mecway |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AK-01 | pipe closed-closed, hex20 20 × 1 × 1, no pressure node | f_n = n·c/(2L), n = 0…4; ∣p∣ mode 1 = c/√V | +0.011% (680 Hz); mode 1 2.9e-5 Hz; ∣p∣ 3400 exact | < 1e-13 |
| AK-02 | pipe open-closed, *fluid pressure* 0 on the nodes at x = L | f_n = (2n − 1)·c/(4L), n = 1…5 | +0.017% (765 Hz) | < 1e-12 |
| AK-03 | pipe open-open, hex8 50 × 1 × 1, *fluid pressure* 0 on both end faces (face selections) | f_n = n·c/(2L), n = 1…4 | +0.016 / +0.066 / +0.15 / +0.26% | < 1e-13 |
| AK-04 | room 1.0 × 0.7 × 0.4 m, hex20 10 × 7 × 4, hard walls | f_lmn = c/2·√((l/Lx)² + (m/Ly)² + (n/Lz)²), 8 modes | ≤ +0.026% | < 4e-10 |
| AK-05 | pipe of two media (c = 340 / 680 m/s, 0.5 m each), hex20 | k1·tan(k1L1) + k2·tan(k2L2) = 0 (pressure and dp/dx continuous) | ≤ +0.024% | – (Mecway: one material) |
| AK-06 | AK-01 with wedge6 (40 hex8 blocks split) | as AK-01 | +0.36% (680 Hz) | < 1e-13 |
| AK-07 | AK-01 with tet4 (Kuhn split, 6 per block) | as AK-01 | +0.31% (680 Hz) | < 1e-13 |

Observations:
- **Equal to Mecway** up to rounding, including the normalisation of the pressure field
  (pᵀMp = 1, M = ∫NᵀN/c² dV): both use the same linear/quadratic elements without
  extras, hence the same discrete eigenvalues.
- **Linear elements** (hex8, wedge6, tet4): the error follows Δf/f ≈ (kh)²/24 (AK-03:
  1.645e-4 measured and computed for n = 1); hex20 gives 0.017% at 9 elements per
  wavelength (AK-02).
- **Hard walls:** mode 1 is the constant pressure (~1e-5 Hz); `PLUS_PETITE` gave
  `ALGELINE3_81` there, the bridge searches around a small negative frequency (alarm
  `ALGELINE3_82`, harmless).
- **Density:** a pipe of two media with ρ = 1.2 / 12 kg/m³ gave the same frequencies as
  without density (248.98 Hz; with the density jump 306.47 Hz is correct): Code-Aster's
  acoustic matrices do not contain ρ. The bridge therefore stops with an ERROR for
  different densities ([Acoustics.md](Acoustics.md) §3.2).
- **Impedance** (S2b): complex modes, see below (AI).

### Acoustics: impedance, complex modes (AI)

Mecway A30 with *Impedance* → `acoustic_modes_3d`: `RIGI_ACOU`/`MASS_ACOU` and per
boundary `AMOR_ACOU` (`CELE_R = 1`), the quadratic eigenvalue problem
(K + λC + λ²M)P = 0, C = Σ ρ/Z ∫NNᵀdA, in the job with scipy
(`aster_acoustic_complex.py`, [Acoustics.md](Acoustics.md) §3.3). Module
`tests_akoestiek_impedantie.py` (group "Acoustics impedance", 10
cases, 227 checks), run with `python run_verificatie.py AI`. Air c = 340 m/s,
ρ = 1.2 kg/m³ (ρc = 408 Pa·s/m); pipe 1 × 0.1 × 0.1 m, hard wall at x = 0, impedance at
x = L. Per mode the frequency f = Im λ/2π and the decay rate σ = −Re λ (the bridge mode
with λ closest to the reference), and the shape as P(L)/P(0) (magnitude and phase
difference; independent of the scale).

References: (a) **analytical** (plane waves, derived from p = cosh(λx/c) and the
boundary condition ∂p/∂x = −ρλp/Z): tanh(λL/c) = −ρc/Z, so
λₙ = (c/L)·(−artanh(ρc/Z) + iπn) — real Z > ρc: fₙ = n·c/(2L), σ = (c/L)·artanh(ρc/Z);
Z < ρc: fₙ = (2n − 1)·c/(4L), σ = (c/L)·artanh(Z/(ρc)); Z → ∞: hard wall; Z = ρc: no
root; P(L)/P(0) = cosh(λL/c); with a pressure node at x = 0: tanh(λL/c) = −Z/(ρc).
(b) **Mecway 33 headless** (`mw_solve.py`) on the same `.liml`: all modes (f, σ) and
P(L)/P(0) of one mode. Tolerance: 1% (S2b done criterion) for f and σ, 0.1% for
∣P(L)/P(0)∣ and 0.05° for the phase against analytical; against Mecway 1% (f, σ), 1e-4
(∣P∣ ratio) and 0.01° (phase).

| ID | Model | Analytical (largest deviation) | Mecway (largest deviation) |
|---|---|---|---|
| AI-01 | Z = 2000 (5ρc), hex20 20 × 1 × 1 | n = 0…5 (n = 0 non-oscillating, f = 0, σ = 70.347 1/s): f ≤ 0.026%, σ ≤ 0.097% (850 Hz); ∣P(L)/P(0)∣ = 1.02148 to 2e-8, phase 3e-7° | ≤ 8e-11 |
| AI-02 | Z = 200 (< ρc) | fₙ = 85, 255, … 765 Hz: ≤ 0.017%; σ = 182.35 1/s ≤ 0.11%; ∣P(L)/P(0)∣ = 0.5624 (−90°) to 1e-8 | ≤ 4e-10 |
| AI-03 | Z = 1e9 (→ ∞) | fₙ = n·c/(2L) ≤ 0.011%, σ = (c/L)·ρc/Z = 1.39e-4 1/s ≤ 0.09%; f₂ = AK-01 (hard walls, Mecway) to 3e-13 | f ≤ 2e-11; σ (1.4e-4 1/s) to 6e-8 1/s |
| AI-04 | Z = ρc = 408 | no root: every oscillating mode decays ≥ 10× faster than for Z = 2000 (σ ≥ 1588 1/s, Q ≤ 1.51; discretisation modes) | ≤ 6e-9 (also the discretisation modes) |
| AI-05 | Z = 2000 − 1000i | fₙ = 170n − 4.5 Hz ≤ 0.025%, σ = 55.60 1/s ≤ 0.097%; P(L)/P(0) to 5e-9, phase 7e-7° | ≤ 2e-10 |
| AI-06 | Z = 1000 + 3000i | fₙ = 170n + 6.60 Hz ≤ 0.026%, σ = 13.674 1/s ≤ 0.10% | ≤ 3e-9 |
| AI-07 | Z = 1000i (purely reactive) | fₙ = 170n + 20.96 Hz ≤ 0.028%, σ = 0 (≤ 1e-6) | ≤ 1e-11 |
| AI-08 | pressure node at x = 0, Z = 2000 | fₙ = (2n + 1)·c/(4L) ≤ 0.037%, σ = 70.347 1/s ≤ 0.14% (935 Hz) | ≤ 3e-11 |
| AI-09 | Z = 2000, hex8 20 × 1 × 1 (168 degrees of freedom linearised: dense, `scipy.linalg.eig`) | n = 0, 1: f +0.10%, σ +0.41%; ∣P(L)/P(0)∣ 4e-5 | ≤ 8e-8 (also n = 2, 3: f +0.41/+0.93%, σ +1.7/+3.8% against analytical, mesh) |
| AI-10 | room 1.0 × 0.7 × 0.4 m, hex20 10 × 7 × 4, floor Z = 1000, ceiling Z = 600 − 300i | – (3D) | 10 modes (maxmode 8: like Mecway the 2·8 smallest \|λ\| with f ≥ 0): ≤ 3e-12; shape corner-corner to 1e-12 |

Observations:
- **Equal to Mecway** up to rounding (≤ 1e-8), also the list of modes apart from the
  0 Hz modes: the same matrices (`RIGI_ACOU`, `MASS_ACOU` = Mecway's K and M, AK) and
  the same boundary term C = ρ/Z ∫NNᵀdA with Z = R + iX. Mecway drops the constant
  pressure (λ = 0) and non-oscillating modes at random because of round-off (AI-01: the
  non-oscillating one kept, the constant one dropped; AI-09 the other way round); the
  bridge gives both.
- **Decay rate and mesh:** σ is more sensitive to the mesh than f (AI-09 hex8: at
  340 Hz f +0.41%, σ +1.7%); hex20 with 20 elements per metre (≥ 8 per wavelength at
  850 Hz) ≤ 0.14%.
- **Reactance:** X > 0 raises the frequencies (AI-06, AI-07), like a mass on the
  boundary with e^{iωt}; no damping for Re Z = 0 (AI-07).
- **Without a density** Mecway uses ρ = 0 (headless: Z = 2000 gave the hard-wall
  frequencies with σ ~1e-10); the bridge stops with an ERROR (Acoustics.md §4).
- Mecway takes the 2 × maxmode eigenvalues with the smallest |λ| and shows those with
  f ≥ 0 (checked headless with maxmode 3, 6, 8 and 10); the bridge does the same.
- Computing time: a room of 13,000 nodes (hex20) with 20 modes 14 s (not in the
  regression).

### Response spectrum (SP)

Mecway *Frequency* with a Gravity table as the spectrum → `spectrum_3d`:
`CALC_MODES` → (`MODE_STATIQUE` `PSEUDO_MODE`) → `COMB_SISM_MODAL` `MONO_APPUI` →
maxima as one step ([Response_spectrum.md](Response_spectrum.md)). Module
`tests_spectrum.py` (group "Response spectrum", 9 cases), run with
`python run_verificatie.py SP` (about 1 minute). References: Code-Aster test case
sdld25a (TEST_RESU) and hand calculations (eigenvalues of 2 × 2 matrices,
Γ_i = φ_iᵀM1, response Γ_i·S_i/ω_i²·φ_i; CQC with the correlation of Der Kiureghian,
1981; Chopra, 2012, chapter 13), recomputed in the test module. Tolerance: sdld25a 1%
(acceptance criterion; the bridge reaches 1e-7), hand calculation 0.5%, solid beam
against Euler-Bernoulli 0.5% (displacement, acceleration), 1% (reaction), 2% (stress,
as MO-07).

| ID | Model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| SP-01 | sdld25a rebuilt: 8 × 10 kg (`mass2`) between 9 springs of 1e5 N/m (Mecway *Spring*), Y/Z fixed, Rayleigh β = 5e-4 s (= the dampers 50 N·s/m of sdld25a), spectrum = the `DEFI_NAPPE` of sdld25a via `#ASTER` + `#ASTER-SPECTRE X`, SRSS | TEST_RESU `VALE_CALC` of sdld25a: 8 frequencies (5.5274 … 31.347 Hz) and `ACCE_ABSOLU` DX at A, P1 … P4 (1.1364; 10.450; 19.030; 25.318; 28.945 m/s²) | frequencies ≤ 3e-14, accelerations ≤ 3.5e-8 (single precision of the result file) |
| SP-02 | ground – k1 – m1 – k2 – m2 along X (100 kg on 1e5 N/m, 5 kg tuned to 1/(1+μ)): f = 4.393 / 5.491 Hz; spectrum table in the period (0; 2), (0.1; 8), (0.3; 5), (2; 1) m/s², both periods on the sloped part: S = 6.0855 / 6.7684 m/s²; SRSS | hand calculation: u m1, u m2, reaction at the ground (Γ_i²·S_i), total reaction from the log, absolute acceleration m2 | ≤ 1e-6 (log: six digits) |
| SP-03 | as SP-02, CQC (ρ₁₂ = 0.1656 at ξ = 0.05) | same with CQC | ≤ 1e-6; CQC relative to SRSS: u m2 −6.5%, reaction +7.9% |
| SP-04 | as SP-02 with 1 mode and missing mass (default) | mode 1 plus the rest (ψ − Γ₁φ₁/ω₁²)·S(f₁), ψ = K⁻¹M1; reaction √((M_eff,1·S₁)² + ((M − M_eff,1)·S₁)²); acceleration with (1 − Γ₁φ₁)·S(f₁) | ≤ 4e-8 |
| SP-05 | as SP-04 without missing mass | mode 1 only (M_eff,1 = 56% of the mass) | ≤ 4e-8; the log gives the WARNING below 90% |
| SP-06a/b/c | 50 kg on two perpendicular springs at 30° and 120° (2e5 and 5e4 N/m), spectra in X and Y (two gravity tables); modes SRSS, directions SRSS / 100-30-30 / 100-40-40 | hand calculation per direction, then √(R_X² + R_Y²), max(R_a + 0.3 R_b), max(R_a + 0.4 R_b) | ≤ 4e-8 |
| SP-07 | clamped solid beam hex20 50 × 2 × 3 (as MO-07), flat spectrum 1 m/s² in Y (gravity without a table), 1 mode, without missing mass; frequency from the run | Euler-Bernoulli: f₁; u_tip·ω₁² = Γ₁φ₁(L)·S = 1.5660 S; σ_xx and von Mises at mid-span = Γ₁·S/ω₁²·σ(φ₁); total reaction M_eff,1·S = 0.6131 m·S; absolute tip acceleration | f₁ +0.20%, u_tip −0.02%, σ_xx +0.38%, von Mises +0.38%, reaction −0.12%, acceleration −0.02% |

Observations:
- The bridge gives exactly what `COMB_SISM_MODAL` computes; the hand calculation
  independently follows the formulas of the macro (`comb_sism_modal_ops.py`), also for
  the missing mass, the automatic rest term of `ACCE_ABSOLU` and 100-30-30 (which the
  bridge computes itself).
- With the missing mass (default) the reaction at the ground is complete, also with 1
  of the 2 modes (SP-04: 454.6 N; without missing mass 355.0 N; with both modes SRSS
  475.2 N).
- Mecway reads the result (one step under a *Frequency* analysis) through the API
  (headless `mecway.exe <model> script`: `node_value` gives the values, `modes()` and
  `time_steps()` are empty). The display in the Mecway GUI is NOT verified.

### Acoustics: harmonic pressure field (AH)

Mecway A30 model → `acoustic_harm_3d`: `PHENOMENE='ACOUSTIQUE'`, `RIGI_ACOU`/`MASS_ACOU`/
`AMOR_ACOU`, `DYNA_VIBRA` `HARM` ([Acoustics.md](Acoustics.md) §5). Module
`tests_akoestiek_harmonisch.py` (group "Acoustics harmonic", 10 cases, 103 checks), run
with `python run_verificatie.py AH`. Air ρ = 1.2 kg/m³, c = 340 m/s; duct
1 × 0.1 × 0.1 m along x, source `BRON_Zuiger` at x = 0 (1 mm/s, into the fluid), checked
along the axis y = z = 0. References: 1D Helmholtz with p(t) = Re(P e^{iωt}), k = ω/c
(derived in the test module) and test case ahlv100a (TEST_RESU). Tolerance: 1%
(completion criterion S3a), hex8 at 10 per wavelength 2%.

| ID | Model | Reference | Result (largest deviation) |
|---|---|---|---|
| AH-01 | absorbing end (`ABSORBEREND_`), hex20 20 × 1 × 1; 100/340/680 Hz (680 Hz: 10 elements per wavelength) | \|P\| = ρcv0 = 0.408 Pa, phase lag kL | \|p\| −0.018%, phase −0.011% (680 Hz); SPL 83.182 dB exact |
| AH-01b | as AH-01, hex20 10 × 1 × 1 (5 per wavelength at 680 Hz) | same | \|p\| −0.25%, phase −0.16% |
| AH-02a | as AH-01, hex8 20 × 1 × 1 (10 per wavelength) | same | \|p\| +1.7%, phase −1.6% (≈ (kh)²/24); tolerance 2% |
| AH-02b | as AH-01, hex8 40 × 1 × 1 (20 per wavelength) | same | \|p\| +0.41%, phase −0.41% |
| AH-03 | hard wall at x = L, hex20; 100/250/420 Hz (resonances 170/340/510) | P = −iρcv0·cos(k(L−x))/sin(kL) | \|P − P_exact\| ≤ 1.2e-4·max\|P\| (420 Hz) |
| AH-04 | ahlv100a rebuilt: 1 × 0.1 × 0.2 m, hex20 15 × 2 × 2, ρ 1.3, c 343, 0.014 m/s, 500 Hz | TEST_RESU of ahlv100a at NO1, NO780, NO751, NO763 (sign reversed: VNOR into the fluid) | VALE_REFE: Re −0.025%, Im +0.33% (ahlv100a itself too; PRECISION 3.3e-3); VALE_CALC: Re ≤ 2.2e-7, Im ≤ 3e-7 Pa; SPL + 3.0103 = 109.8866 dB (DB of ahlv100a) |
| AH-05 | same, cavity modes (`acoustic_modes_3d`, hard walls) | CALC_MODES part of ahlv100a, 8 modes | VALE_CALC ≤ 2e-8; analytical ≤ +0.38% (cross mode 6) |
| AH-06 | Mecway *impedance* Z = 2ρc = 816 Pa·s/m at x = L, hex20; 100/250 Hz | R = (Z − ρc)/(Z + ρc) = 1/3, P = A(e^{−ikx} + R e^{−ik(2L−x)}) | \|P − P_exact\| ≤ 6.9e-6·max\|P\|; max/min along the axis 1.994 (2 between the nodes) |
| AH-07 | *fluid pressure* 2 Pa at x = 0 (imposed pressure), absorbing end; 170/500 Hz | P = 2 e^{−ikx} | \|p\| ≤ 5e-5, phase ≤ 3e-5 |
| AH-08 | `#ASTER-BRON Luidspreker = 2 mm/s, 90` (face selection without prefix), frequencies via `#ASTER-OPTIE harm_freq_list`, microphones `MICROFOON_Eind` (1 node) and `MICROFOON_Midden` (8 nodes) | P(0) = iρcv, SPL 89.203 dB, phase 90° − kL | Im p(0) −2e-6, Re p(0) ≤ 1e-5 Pa; SPL ≤ 3e-4 dB; phase ≤ 0.017° |

Observations:
- **Sign convention:** with `EXCIT` `COEF_MULT=+1, PUIS_PULS=1, PHAS_DEG=90` (iω·v)
  `VITE_FACE` gives p = +ρcv at the source: v is the velocity into the fluid. ahlv100a
  uses −1 (v outward) and therefore has −6.2426 Pa at the source; the bridge values are
  the opposite up to rounding (AH-04).
- **Absorbing boundary and impedance:** `3D_ABSO` imposes (1/c)∫NNᵀdA with the c of the
  face's material, without ρ (measured); Mecway's impedance Z thus becomes a separate
  material with c = Z/ρ (AH-06 exact up to discretisation).
- **Mesh:** hex20 is still within 0.25% at 5 elements per wavelength; hex8 lags by
  ≈ (kh)²/24 per wavelength (1.6% at 10, 0.41% at 20), and the small impedance error
  of the discrete wave gives a slight standing wave (\|p\| +1.7% at 10). Hence the
  WARNING below 10 (linear) / 5 (quadratic) per wavelength.
- **SPL:** the bridge uses the rms value (\|P\|/√2); Code-Aster's `PRAC_ELNO` DB uses
  \|P\| itself, 3.0103 dB higher (AH-04 = ahlv100a within 2e-8).
- **Mecway:** Mecway has no harmonic acoustics, so no comparison with a Mecway
  solution. Mecway's API reads the result of AH-08 (headless script: `solution.modes()`
  = [1, 2], `frequency` 200/500 Hz, `node_value` of `pressure`, `pressurei`,
  `pressuremag`, `pressurephase`, `spl` equal to the bridge values); the GUI display has
  NOT been inspected (KNOWN_BUGS 57).

### Vibroacoustics (VA)

Mecway *Frequency* (V30) with a fluid part → `vibroacoustic_modes_3d`: `3D_FLUIDE` +
`FLUI_STRU` + the structure, `FORMULATION='U_P_PHI'`, `INFO_MODE` + `CALC_MODES`
`BANDE` ([Acoustics.md](Acoustics.md) §6). Module `tests_vibroakoestiek.py` (group
"Vibroacoustics", 8 cases, 29 checks), run with `python run_verificatie.py VA` (about
2 minutes). References: 1D wave equation in a column with hard side walls, with the
boundary conditions derived in the test module (roots by bisection), test case
adlv100a (VALE_REFE), Kirchhoff plate (Leissa §4.1) and the cavity modes f_lmn.
Tolerance: adlv100a 0.5% (done criterion S3b), the second analytical case 1% (done
criterion), otherwise 0.1–1%.

| ID | Model | Reference | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| VA-01 | adlv100a rebuilt: water 0.05 m (free surface, `OPEN_Vrij`), steel piston 0.025 m (guided sideways), air 0.075 m (closed), section 0.05 × 0.05 m; hex20 4 + 1 + 6 | adlv100a VALE_REFE 13.8285 Hz; exact root of m + ρ1·tan(k1L1)/k1 − ρ2·cot(k2L2)/k2 = 0 (13.82768 / 2266.75 Hz); p/u on both piston faces | f1 −6.0e-5 vs adlv100a, −9.8e-7 vs the root; f2 +5.1e-5; p/u air −1.9e-6, water −2.0e-6 |
| VA-01k | as VA-01, coupling faces as `KOPPEL_` face selections | same | equal to VA-01 |
| VA-02 | elastic rod (E 3 GPa, ρ 1400, ν 0, Zs/Zf = 1.38) 0.5 m, clamped and guided sideways, + closed water column 1 m; hex20 10 + 20 | Zs·cot(ksLs) + Zf·cot(kfLf) = 0 (513.85 / 960.19 / 1475.68 / 1987.83 Hz) | ≤ +2.4e-5 (mode 4) |
| VA-03 | shell piston (DKT quad4 2 × 2, t = 10 mm, translation x free, rotations fixed) on a closed air column 0.5 m; hex8 50 × 2 × 2 | m·k·tan(kL) = ρ (9.480 / 340.26 / 680.13 Hz) | +2e-7 / +1.6e-4 / +6.6e-4 (hex8: (kh)²/24) |
| VA-04 | pipe of two media (c 340 / 680 m/s, equal density), fluid only; hex20 10 + 10 | tan(k1L1)/Z1 + tan(k2L2)/Z2 = 0 (248.98 / 431.02 / 680 Hz) | ≤ +8.6e-5 |
| VA-05 | steel plate 0.5 × 0.4 m, 2 mm, simply supported (DKT 30 × 24) on a closed air cavity 0.5 × 0.4 × 0.3 m (hex8 30 × 24 × 12), air 1000× lighter | uncoupled: plate in vacuo (Leissa §4.1) and cavity with hard walls, 8 modes in order | plate −0.09 … −0.61% (DKT discretisation, 20 × 16 still gave −1.35% for (3,2)), cavity (1,0,0) +0.045% |
| VA-05b | as VA-05 with ordinary air | pumping mode (1,1) with the air spring, one mode: ω² = ω0² + ρc²(∫φ dA)²/(V·M) (approximation) = 55.23 Hz | −0.48% (54.96 Hz; in vacuo 50.52 Hz) |
| VA-05c | as VA-05b, `#ASTER-OPTIE vibro_freq_min = 100`, 3 modes | plate modes (2,1), (1,2), (2,2) in vacuo; air + discretisation < 1% | −0.66 / −0.52 / −0.69% |

Observations:
- **Formulation and eigenvalues:** `U_P_PHI` gives a spurious mode at 0 Hz per φ
  degree of freedom (adlv100a mesh: 40, `MASS_GENE` < 0); `PLUS_PETITE` stops with
  `ALGELINE3_81`, `CENTRE` first gives the spurious modes. The Sturm count of
  `INFO_MODE` above 0 Hz is right (VA-02: 6 of 6 modes up to 3 kHz in a separate run),
  so `BANDE` from `vibro_freq_min` gives only the real modes. `U_P` gave a wrong
  series with `PLUS_PETITE` (1147 Hz as the first mode of adlv100a) and
  `<F> JEVEUX_26` with `CENTRE`; not used.
- **Density:** it is in the matrices: VA-02 with ρ_water 1 instead of 1000 gave its own
  analytical roots (726.19 / 745.74 Hz …; separate run). Two fluids with a different
  density and shared nodes give a wrong result (pipe ρ 1.2/12: 188.23 Hz, analytically
  306.47 Hz; φ is continuous): the bridge then stops with an ERROR; with equal density
  it is exact (VA-04).
- **Coupling and sign:** p/u on both piston faces is right within 2e-6 (VA-01): the
  normal of the coupling faces (out of the fluid, `ORIE_PEAU`) and the sign of the
  pressure are right; the coupling also works through a shell (VA-03, VA-05).
- **Robustness:** a shell with only one rotation fixed (tilting mechanism) gave, in a
  separate run, a spurious mode at 3e-5 Hz with `MASS_GENE` < 0 in the `BANDE` output;
  normalising gave `DVP_2`. The bridge now filters first (f ≥ lower bound, mass > 0)
  and reports a shortage of modes (WARNING).
- **Mecway:** Mecway computes a material without Young's modulus as a solid with
  E = 0 (headless: modes of 1e-7 Hz), so no comparison. Mecway's API reads the result
  of VA-01 (headless script: `modes()` = [1, 2], `frequency` 13.8277 / 2266.87 Hz,
  `displx` on the piston nodes and `pressure` on the fluid nodes; on a coupling-face
  node both, p/u = 1.849e6 Pa/m). The GUI display has NOT been looked at
  (KNOWN_BUGS 59).

### Vibroacoustics harmonic (VH)

The same Mecway model as VA, now per frequency → `vibroacoustic_harm_3d`: `3D_FLUIDE` +
`FLUI_STRU` + `3D_FLUI_ABSO`, `U_P_PHI`, `DYNA_VIBRA` `HARM` `PHYS` with
`MATR_IMPE_PHI` ([Acoustics.md](Acoustics.md) §7). Module
`tests_vibroakoestiek_harmonisch.py` (group "Vibroacoustics harmonic", 13 cases, 175
checks), run with `python run_verificatie.py VH` (about 1 minute). References: the test
cases ahlv101a and ahlv100b (TEST_RESU, reproduced on the mesh of AH-04) and the 1D wave
equation with a rigid piston (mass m, spring k, force F) on a column:
U = F/(k − mω² + iω(c_s + A·Z_in)), Z_in = ρc for a ρc end and ρc(1 + R e^{−2ikL})/(1 −
R e^{−2ikL}) for an impedance Z, P(x) from the piston velocity (derived in the test
module). Tolerance: ahlv101a/ahlv100b the PRECISION 1e-3 of VALE_REFE and 1e-6 of
VALE_CALC, otherwise 1% (completion criterion S3c) and 0.5° in the phase. Piston: air ρ
1.2, c 340, column 1 × 0.1 × 0.1 m (hex20 10 × 1 × 1), piston hex20 10 mm (ρ 500: m = 0.05
kg, guided laterally), spring k = 3000 N/m (f0 = 38.98 Hz), F = 1 N, ρcA = 4.08 N·s/m
(ζ = 0.17); frequencies 5, 20, 35, 39, 45, 60, 100, 200 Hz.

| ID | Model | Reference | Result (largest deviation) |
|---|---|---|---|
| VH-01 | ahlv101a reproduced: 1 × 0.1 × 0.2 m, hex20 15 × 2 × 2, ρ 1.3, c 343; `INVALLEND_Inlaat` (`incident_pressure` 0.5 Pa = `ONDE_FLUI` `PRES` 1), `ABSORBEREND_Uitlaat`, 500 Hz | TEST_RESU of ahlv101a in NO1, NO780, NO751, NO763 (P = 0.5·e^{−ikx}) | VALE_REFE ≤ 8.0e-4 (NO751, ahlv101a itself too); VALE_CALC ≤ 1.3e-7; SPL microphone 84.9485 dB (exact within 3e-7 dB) |
| VH-01b | ahlv100b reproduced: the same with `BRON_Inlaat` (`source_velocity` 0.014 m/s) | TEST_RESU of ahlv100b (sign reversed: VNOR into the fluid) | VALE_REFE ≤ 8.9e-4; VALE_CALC ≤ 2.3e-7 |
| VH-07 | as VH-01b with `#ASTER-BRON Inlaat = 14 mm/s, 90` (face selection without prefix), frequency from `#ASTER-OPTIE` | ahlv100b VALE_CALC × i | ≤ 2.3e-7; microphone phase −74.73° exact |
| VH-08 | as VH-01 with `#ASTER-BRON Inlaat = 0.5 Pa, -30` (pressure = incident wave) | ahlv101a VALE_CALC × e^{−i30°} | ≤ 1.1e-7 |
| VH-02 | piston with ρc end (`ABSORBEREND_`), microphone x = 0.5 m, probe on the piston; stresses in the piston | U = F/(k − mω² + iωρcA), P = iωρcU·e^{−ikx} | U ≤ 1.3e-4, phase ≤ 6.8e-4°, P ≤ 2.9e-5; SPL (CSV) at the peak −4e-5 dB, \|U\| (CSV) −9e-6 |
| VH-02t | as VH-02, force from a Mecway table F(f) = 1 + f/100 N, `#ASTER-FASE Last = 90` | U·F(f)·i | ≤ 1.3e-4 |
| VH-03 | as VH-02 with Mecway *impedance* Z = 2ρc at x = L | Z_in with R = 1/3 | U ≤ 1.2e-4, phase ≤ 7.4e-4°, P ≤ 1.7e-5 (Z changes U at the peak by 7%) |
| VH-04 | as VH-02 with Rayleigh α = 200 1/s | c_s = αm = 10 N·s/m (structure only) | U ≤ 1.3e-4, phase ≤ 1.5e-3°, P ≤ 2.9e-5 |
| VH-04b | as VH-02 with Rayleigh β = 2e-3 s | c_s = βk = 6 N·s/m (the rigid piston does not deform) | U ≤ 4.5e-5, phase ≤ 2.4e-3°, P ≤ 2.8e-5 |
| VH-04c | as VH-02 with a Mecway damper 10 N·s/m parallel to the spring | c_s = 10 N·s/m | U ≤ 1.2e-4, phase ≤ 7.2e-4°, P ≤ 1.6e-5 |
| VH-05 | air only, hex20 20 × 1 × 1, `INVALLEND_Inlaat` (1 Pa), hard wall at x = L; 100/250/420 Hz | P = e^{−ikx} + e^{−ik(2L−x)} (the inlet lets the reflected wave pass) | ≤ 1.2e-4 of max\|P\| |
| VH-05b | the same with `OPEN_Einde` (p = 0) | P = e^{−ikx} − e^{−ik(2L−x)} | ≤ 1.2e-4 |
| VH-06 | shell piston (DKT quad4 2 × 2, t = 10 mm, ρ 500, rotations fixed) with spring, air hex8 20 × 2 × 2; 5/39/100/200 Hz | as VH-02 | U ≤ 2.5e-4, P ≤ 3.4e-3 (hex8, 34 per wavelength at 200 Hz) |

Observations:
- **Incident wave:** `ONDE_FLUI` with `PRES` gives a wave `PRES`/2 into the fluid
  (boundary condition ∂p/∂n + ikp = ik·`PRES`) and lets the reflected wave pass without
  reflection (VH-05/05b exact apart from discretisation); the bridge writes `PRES` = 2 ×
  `incident_pressure`, so the setting is the amplitude of the incoming wave.
- **Sign:** `VITE_FACE` in `U_P_PHI` with `COEF_MULT=+1` gives p = −ρcv (ahlv100b: `VNOR`
  outward); the bridge uses −1 (v into the fluid; VH-01b, VH-07). In acoustics
  (`ACOUSTIQUE`, AH) it is the other way round: there +1 already gives p = +ρcv.
- **Impedance:** `IMPE_MECA` of `3D_FLUI_ABSO` corresponds to Z = ρ·c of the material of
  the face; its own material with `CELE_R = Z/ρ` gives the right input impedance
  (VH-03).
- **Damping:** Rayleigh via `RIGI_MECA`/`MASS_MECA` with `GROUP_MA` of the structure
  (VH-04/04b); `AMOR_MECA` with `GROUP_MA` stopped with `DVP_1`, without `GROUP_MA`
  (with `AMOR_FLUI='NON'`) the damper is right (VH-04c). `IMPE_MECA` rejects
  `CARA_ELEM` (`SUPERVIS_4` with a spring in the model): its own `ASSEMBLAGE` without
  `CARA_ELEM`.
- **Mesh:** hex20 at 17 per wavelength gives ≤ 1.3e-4 (VH-02 at 200 Hz); the shell piston
  with hex8 air ≤ 3.4e-3 (VH-06), as in AH-02.
- **Mecway:** no comparison (Mecway has no coupled or harmonic analysis). Mecway's API
  reads the result of VH-02 (headless script: `modes()` = 1..8, `frequency` 5…200 Hz,
  `node_value` of `displx`/`displix` on the piston and `pressure`/`pressurei`/`spl` in
  the column); the GUI display has NOT been checked (KNOWN_BUGS 59).

## 8. Thermal (TH)

Bridge `ther_3d`: Mecway T30 → `THER_LINEAIRE` steady state, T31 → `THER_LINEAIRE`
transient, convection on a shell face → `THER_NON_LINE`; TH-03 with `lin_static_3d` and
`<thermalstress>` (`AFFE_VARC TEMP`). Module `tests_thermisch.py`, run with
`python run_verificatie.py TH` (groups "Thermal" and "Thermal stress"). User documentation: [Thermal.md](Thermal.md).

### TH-01 Steady-state conduction

- **Purpose**: `THER_LINEAIRE` steady state, `temperature2` (`TEMP_IMPO`),
  `heatgeneration` (`SOURCE`), `convectionbc` (`ECHANGE`), the heat flux (`FLUX_NOEU`)
  and thermal shells (`PLAN` with a thickness factor).
- **Model and load**:
  - TH-01a: wall 0.1 m, q = 1e6 W/m³, both sides 300 K, hex8 (8 elements);
  - TH-01b: idem, hex20 (2 elements);
  - TH-01c: 400 K left, convection h = 250 W/m²K to T∞ = 300 K right (Bi = 0.5);
  - TH-01d: q = 1e6 W/m³, x = 0 insulated, convection h = 500 W/m²K (Bi = 0.5);
  - TH-01e: fin as a 2 mm shell (10 quad8), convection h = 25 W/m²K on the face, base
    400 K.
- **Reference** (Incropera et al., 2007, §3.1, 3.5 and 3.6.2): T_max − T0 = qL²/8k =
  25 K and the flux at L/4 (a) or x = 0 (b); series resistance L/k + 1/h:
  T(L) = 366.67 K, q = 16 667 W/m² (c); Ts = T∞ + qL/h = 400 K, T(0) − Ts = qL²/2k =
  25 K (d); fin with insulated tip (T − T∞)/(T0 − T∞) = cosh m(L−x)/cosh mL,
  m = √(h/kt) (e).
- **Result**: (a–d) relative deviation ≤ 9e-14 (tolerance 1e-5); (e) 6e-7 (tolerance
  1e-3).
- **Interpretation**: a temperature field that depends only on x is, in a structured
  hex8 block, the solution with linear 1D elements; Galerkin gives exact nodal values
  for −kT'' = q, also with a convection boundary, and hex20 is exact for a quadratic T.
  The nodal average of the flux for two equal elements is the central difference, exact
  for quadratic T. The tolerance is therefore rounding only. TH-01e: mL = 1.58 and 10
  quad8 give a discretisation error ~(mh)⁴ < 1e-4; this also checks the thickness factor
  (`PLAN`, f = t) and the convection on the shell face (`SOUR_NL`, `THER_NON_LINE`).

### TH-02 Transient: semi-infinite body

- **Purpose**: `THER_LINEAIRE` transient: θ scheme (setting `theta`), time step, ρ·cp,
  initial and boundary temperature.
- **Model**: bar 0.1 m, 100 hex8 elements (h = 1 mm, cross-section 2 × 2 mm, side faces
  insulated), λ = 10 W/mK, ρ = 1000 kg/m³, cp = 1000 J/kgK (α = 1e-5 m²/s); initial
  temperature Ti = 300 K, boundary temperature Ts = 400 K at x = 0 from t = 0 (the bar
  is long enough to be semi-infinite). TH-02a/b/c: θ = 0.57 / 0.5 / 1.0 at Δt = 0.1 s;
  TH-02d–f: Δt = 0.4 / 0.2 / 0.05 s at θ = 0.5 and 1.0; TH-02g/h/i: coarse step
  Δt = 1 s (Δt·α/h² = 10), θ = 0.5 / 0.57 / 1.0.
- **Reference**: T − Ti = (Ts − Ti)·erfc(x/2√(αt)) (Carslaw & Jaeger, 1959, ch. 2;
  Incropera et al., 2007, §5.7), at t = 10 and 40 s (a–f) or 4 s (g–i). In addition a
  replica: `_fe1d_theta` computes the same discrete problem in 1D (linear elements,
  consistent mass, θ method, T(0) = Ts from the first step).
- **Result**: largest deviation from erfc 0.12 K (a–c, tolerance 0.5 K), 0.51 K (d–f,
  Δt = 0.4 s, θ = 1; tolerance 1 K) and 3.1 K (g–i, tolerance 5 K). Difference from the
  replica ≤ 1.3e-5 K in all 15 cases (tolerance 1e-4 K).
- **Interpretation**: the replica proves that θ, Δt, ρ·cp, λ and the initial and
  boundary temperature are in the `.comm` exactly as intended; the tolerance 1e-4 K
  follows from the single precision in which the bridge writes transient results (ulp
  at 380 K = 3e-5 K). The deviation from erfc is scheme error: the jump of the boundary
  temperature at t = 0 is smeared over the first step (order Δt, ≤ 0.25 K at
  Δt = 0.1 s); the spatial error at h = 1 mm is < 0.02 K. The tolerances 0.5 / 1 / 5 K
  were estimated with the same 1D scheme.

**Effect of θ** (setting `theta`):

| θ | T − Ti at 8 mm, 10 s (Δt 0.1 s; ref. 57.161 K) | T − Ti at 1 mm, 4 s (Δt 1 s; ref. 91.10 K) |
|---|---|---|
| 0.5 (Crank-Nicolson, = Mecway) | 57.070 | 93.89 (overshoots +2.8 K) |
| 0.57 (default of bridge and Code-Aster) | 57.065 | 91.71 |
| 1.0 (implicit Euler) | 57.037 | 90.16 |

- With a small step (Δt·α/h² = 1) θ makes little difference: the error is dominated by
  the jump at t = 0 and converges with order 1 in Δt for all θ (measured p = 0.97–1.13,
  table "Mesh-convergentie" in `resultaten.md`), also for Crank-Nicolson, which is
  second order for smooth problems.
- With a coarse step (Δt·α/h² = 10) Crank-Nicolson overshoots near the surface (no
  discrete maximum principle); θ = 0.57 damps that, θ = 1 is monotonic but slower. That
  is scheme behaviour, not a bridge error (the replica gives the same values).
- Mecway computes Crank-Nicolson with the load at the end of the step; for a constant
  boundary temperature that equals θ = 0.5 ([Thermal.md](Thermal.md) §5). To follow
  Mecway exactly, choose `theta` = 0.5; the default 0.57 is slightly damped.

### TH-03 Thermal stress

- **Purpose**: a temperature field as a load in a mechanical analysis
  (`<thermalstress>`, `AFFE_VARC TEMP`, `ALPHA`).
- **Model**: bar of 4 hex8 elements, uniform heating ΔT = 100 K relative to the
  reference temperature, α = 1.2e-5 1/K.
- **Boundary conditions**: symmetry planes x = 0, y = 0 and z = 0 (TH-03a: otherwise
  free); TH-03b also x = L held in x (clamped in x, free laterally).
- **Reference** (Timoshenko & Goodier, 1970): (a) u = αΔT·L, σ = 0; (b)
  σ_xx = −EαΔT = −252 MPa, u_y = (1+ν)αΔT·b, reaction EαΔT·A.
- **Result**: displacements, σ_xx and reaction ≤ 3e-15 relative (tolerance 1e-6);
  residual stress ≤ 1.2e-6 Pa (tolerance 252 Pa = 1e-6·EαΔT).
- **Interpretation**: uniform strain and stress are exact with hex8; `ALPHA`, the
  reference temperature and the temperature field are translated correctly.

### TH-04 Bonded contact in a thermal analysis

- **Purpose**: Mecway *Bonded contact* (`contact2`) in T30: *Tie* / *Constraint equations*
  → `AFFE_CHAR_THER` `LIAISON_MAIL`, *Elastic* (thermal resistive layer) →
  `ECHANGE_PAROI` (previously no heat crossed the bond, without a message).
- **Model**: bar 0.1 × 0.01 × 0.01 m made of two hex8 blocks with their own nodes
  (x = 0..0.05 and 0.05..0.1 m), λ = 50 W/mK, bonded at x = 0.05 m (master block A),
  300 K left and 400 K right, sides insulated. TH-04a: *Tie*, 5 × 1 × 1 elements per block
  (conforming); TH-04b: *Constraint equations*, seam 2 × 2 (master) against 3 × 3
  (slave); TH-04c: *Elastic*, h = 2000 W/m²K (`<conductanceperarea>`), conforming;
  TH-04d: two shell strips (quad4, 2 mm, `PLAN`) in one plane, edge of B on the last
  column of A, 3 against 4 nodes on the seam.
- **Reference** (Incropera et al., 2007, §3.1): T linear, 350 K at the seam, q =
  −k dT/dx = −50,000 W/m² (a, b, d); resistances in series L/2k + 1/h + L/2k: q =
  40,000 W/m², 340 / 360 K on both sides of the seam (c).
- **Result**: (a, c) relative deviation ≤ 1e-14 (tolerance 1e-6); (b) seam 350.067 K,
  flux −50,083 W/m² (+0.17%); (d) seam 350.036 K, flux −0.04% (tolerance 0.1% on T,
  0.5% on the flux).
- **Interpretation**: with conforming meshes the node-to-face coupling is exact. With
  non-conforming meshes it is not conforming in the FE sense (a small jump between the
  slave nodes), so even a linear field is not exact; Mecway 34 (internal solver, no
  GUI) computes the same coupling: difference per node 0.0024 K (b) and 0.0056 K (d).
  Mecway's internal solver gave no coupling at all for *Tie* (a) and *Elastic* (c)
  (300 / 400 K); with the CCX solver 350 K (a) and 340.0 / 360.0 K, flux −39,998 W/m²
  (c), equal to the bridge. Solids and shells together in one model cannot be coupled
  thermally by Code-Aster (CHARGES7_6 / CHARGES6_2, ERROR; [Thermal.md](Thermal.md)
  sec. 3).

### TH-40 .. TH-42 Several heat sources with a formula or time table

- **Purpose**: Code-Aster 17.4 computes two `AFFE_CHAR_THER_F` loads with `SOURCE` in
  one analysis both with the function of the first load. The bridge puts all function
  sources in one load, with per part of the mesh a `FORMULE` with the sum of the sources
  that lie there ([Thermal.md](Thermal.md) §3). Before the fix TH-40 and TH-41 gave 37.50
  instead of 39.58 K and 4.00 instead of 2.75 K (two or three times the first source).
- **Model**: wall 0.1 × 0.01 × 0.01 m, 10 hex8 in x, λ = 50 W/mK, sides insulated.
  TH-40 (T30): 300 K at x = 0 and L; three *Internal heat generation*s as a formula:
  5e5 W/m³ on everything, 2e7·x W/m³ on the left half, 1.5e6 W/m³ on the right half (two
  parts with a sum `FORMULE`). TH-41 (T31, 2 s, step 0.1 s, `theta` 0.5): insulated
  everywhere, 2e6·t W/m³ (formula) on the left and a time table 0 → 1e6 W/m³ (0..1 s),
  then constant, on the right (ρc = 1e6 J/m³K; two `SOURCE` entries without overlap).
  TH-42 (T31, 2 s): block 40 × 10 × 10 mm (hex8 1 mm, ρc = 4.68e6 J/m³K) with an
  `#ASTER-LASBRON` (Gauss r = 2 mm, Q = 100 W, `symmetrie=1`) and a time table
  0 → 2e7 W/m³ on the whole block, together in the load `lasbron`.
- **Reference**: TH-40: T(x) − T0 = 1/k ∫ G(x, s) q(s) ds with the Green's function of
  the wall (Incropera et al., 2007, §3.5); with linear elements the nodal values are
  exact in 1D (Strang & Fix §1.2). TH-41/42: energy balance ρc ∫(T − T0) dV =
  ∫∫ q dV dt; with θ = 0.5 and q linear per step the scheme is exact, and ∫T dV follows
  exactly from the nodal values (consistent capacity). TH-42: the energy of the weld
  source from its own energy check (`BRIDGE_LASBRON`, absorbed power 50.0001 W = Q/2).
- **Result**: TH-40 relative deviation ≤ 2.2e-13 (tolerance 1e-5); TH-41 0 (tolerance
  1e-4); TH-42 residual of the energy balance −2e-6 J (tolerance 0.18 J), mean rise
  9.61539 K (+1.0e-6).
- **Mecway** (34, internal solver, without GUI): TH-40 difference per node 2e-12 K.
  TH-41 0.2 K: Mecway takes the load at the end of the step (KNOWN_BUGS 14); its mean
  rise 2.875 K is exactly the sum of that scheme (2.75 K exact). A scratch model with
  three shell-face fluxes (formula, table and overlapping, `SOURCE` q/t) also gave
  exactly 2.0 K, Mecway 2.075 K (same explanation).
- **Also checked** (Code-Aster 17.4, scratch runs): two function loads with
  `FLUX_REP`, `ECHANGE` or `SOUR_NL` and two `AFFE_CHAR_MECA_F` loads (`FORCE_INTERNE`,
  `FORCE_FACE`, `PRES_REP`, `FORMULE` or `DEFI_FONCTION`) are computed correctly (energy /
  reaction force exact); only `SOURCE` has the behaviour.

### Welding: moving heat source (WE)

Module `tests_lassen.py` (group "Welding", run with
`python run_verificatie.py WE`). User documentation: [Welding.md](Welding.md).

- **Goal**: the moving weld source (`#ASTER-LASBRON`: Goldak shape (Goldak, Chakravarti & Bibby, 1984), path, η, symmetry,
  energy check in the job), the same source as a Mecway formula in x, y, z, t
  (`heatgeneration` → `FORMULE`), formulas in other thermal loads, and
  temperature-dependent λ and c (Mecway *TempDependent* → `THER_NL`, `THER_NON_LINE`).
- **Model WE-01/01b/02/03/05**: half steel plate (symmetry plane y = 0) 55 × 15 × 15
  mm, λ = 30 W/mK, ρ = 7800 kg/m³, c = 600 J/kgK (a = 6.41e-6 m²/s), insulated, T0 =
  293.15 K; hex8 with 0.5 mm along the path and up to 7 mm from it (35 600 nodes);
  source 1000 W (η·Q) at 5 mm/s over 30 mm from x = 10 mm in 6 s, checked at t = 6 s.
  WE-01: Gaussian half sphere r = 0.75 mm, Δt = 0.05 s (v·Δt = (af + ar)/6); WE-01b:
  same with Δt = 0.15 s (the limit of the time step warning); WE-02: Goldak 1/2/1/1 mm,
  Q = 1250 W, η = 0.8, Δt = 0.1 s; WE-03: the source of WE-01 as a Mecway formula on an
  element selection; WE-05: WE-02 with λ(T) 30 → 20 W/mK and c(T) 450/600/750 J/kgK
  (300/800/1300 K). WE-04a: bar 100 mm (20 hex8), 300 and 1300 K at the ends,
  λ = 20 + 0.02 (T − 300) W/mK; WE-04b: bar 20 mm (40 hex8), insulated, flux 1e7 W/m²
  on x = 0 for 2 s, λ(T) 50 → 25, c(T) as WE-05. WE-06: bar 100 × 10 × 10 mm, steady
  state, temperature 100 + y (°C, y in mm), convection h = 25(1 + 100y) (y in m), flux
  1000·x (W/m², x in mm).
- **References**: (a) Rosenthal (1946): quasi-steady point source on a semi-infinite
  insulated body, T − T0 = Q/(2πkR)·exp(−v(R + ξ)/(2a)), at six points next to, behind
  and below the source (R = 3–6 mm, ≥ 4 r); (b) the same (Goldak) source folded
  analytically with the Green's function (`goldak_exact`, Carslaw & Jaeger, 1959): exact
  in y and z, per half Gaussian with erf in x, numerically over time; (c) the energy
  balance ∫ρ(H(T) − H(T0))dV = η·Q·t/2 (half plate), from the nodal temperatures with
  the same 2 × 2 × 2 Gauss points as Code-Aster; (d) the `BRIDGE_LASBRON` line (sum of
  the nodal loads = absorbed power); (e) WE-04a: Kirchhoff transformation (∫λ dT linear
  in x, nodally exact for linear elements); (f) Mecway 33 headless (`mw_solve.py`,
  internal solver) on the same `.liml`.
- **Result**:

| Case | Check | Deviation | Tolerance |
|---|---|---|---|
| WE-01 | Rosenthal, 6 points | −0.94 … +0.90% | 5% |
| WE-01 | energy; absorbed power (job) | +0.06%; +0.09% | 2%; 0.5% |
| WE-01b | Rosenthal, 6 points (Δt at the warning limit) | −3.6 … +1.6% | 5% |
| WE-02 | Goldak analytical, 6 points | −1.2 … +0.2% | 2% |
| WE-02 | energy; absorbed power | 0.00%; 0.00% | 2%; 0.5% |
| WE-03 | Rosenthal; Gaussian source analytical | ≤ 0.94%; ≤ 1.4% | 5%; 2% |
| WE-03 | energy relative to Mecway | 1e-9 | 1e-4 |
| WE-03 | temperature relative to Mecway (2 points) | −2.9%; +1.6% | 6% |
| WE-04a | Kirchhoff (3 points); Mecway | ≤ 2e-12; 3e-12 | 1e-4; 1e-3 |
| WE-04b | energy t = 1 and 2 s; Mecway (3 points) | −0.003%, −0.002%; ≤ 0.13% | 0.5%; 2% |
| WE-05 | energy (enthalpy); absorbed power | −0.007%; 0.00% | 2%; 0.5% |
| WE-06 | Mecway (3 points) | ≤ 1e-14 | 1e-9 |

- **Interpretation**:
  - The source, η, the ff/fr distribution and the symmetry end up exactly in the mesh:
    the absorbed power is 500.00 W (WE-02) and the energy balance closes to 0.06%; the
    remainder is the integration of the Gaussian source with 2 points per direction at
    h = 0.67 r.
  - Rosenthal: the difference is the source shape (the Gaussian sphere versus a point:
    +1.4% next to the source, analytically) plus the discretisation. With Δt at the
    warning limit (v·Δt = (af + ar)/2) the weld axis 6 mm behind the source is 2.9% too
    cold; with v·Δt = af + ar (Δt = 0.3 s, outside the regression) 26%: the source skips
    nodes. With Δt = 0.05 s (WE-01) it is −0.25% relative to the same source
    analytically; the warning is therefore at ½(af + ar) ([Welding.md](Welding.md)
    §4.1).
  - Goldak: the comparison with the same source analytically checks shape, orientation
    (front/rear, transverse, depth) and normalisation; the deviation is the mesh (h =
    0.5 mm against b = c = 1 mm).
  - Mecway formula: the bridge gives the same temperatures as `#ASTER-LASBRON` (WE-03 =
    WE-01) and the same energy as Mecway. Mecway's temperatures differ because of its
    time scheme (the load at the end of the step, KNOWN_BUGS 14): the source runs
    v·Δt/2 ahead; Mecway is within 1.5% of the analytical solution at t + Δt/2.
  - Temperature dependent: λ(T) is exact (WE-04a, Mecway too). For c(T) the bridge
    refines the ρc table: with only Mecway's table points `THER_NON_LINE` missed 13% of
    the energy after the first step and 2.8% after 2 s (the enthalpy is interpolated
    linearly between the table points); now 0.003%. Mecway's own energy balance:
    −0.3% / −0.2%.
  - WE-06: formulas in x, y, z for `TEMP_IMPO`, `ECHANGE` and `FLUX_REP` with units mm,
    m and °C are equal to Mecway up to rounding.

### Welding: thermal → mechanical chain (WE-07 .. WE-10)

Module `tests_lassen_mech.py` (group "Welding", `python run_verificatie.py WE-07 WE-08
WE-09 WE-10`; WE-10b only with `VERIF_ZWAAR=1`, ~1 hour). User documentation:
[Welding.md](Welding.md) §9.

- **Goal**: the thermal → mechanical chain (T31 with a Thermal stress →
  `THER_LINEAIRE` and then `STAT_NON_LINE` with `AFFE_VARC` `TEMP`, `EVOL` = the thermal
  result; `bridge_thermomech.py`): thermal strain, restrained expansion with bilinear
  plasticity and residual stress after cooling back, temperature-dependent E, SY, α
  (`#ASTER-MATERIAL`, `ELAS_FO`/`ECRO_LINE_FO`), and a weld bead on a plate.
- **Model WE-07..09**: bar 100 × 10 × 10 mm (hex8 4 × 1 × 1), E = 210 GPa, ν = 0.3,
  α = 1.2e-5 1/K; the temperature comes from the thermal solve (imposed temperature on
  all nodes with a time table T0 → T0 + ΔT → T0, Δt = 1 s, T0 = Thermal stress =
  293.15 K). WE-07: three symmetry planes (free), ΔT = 100 K. WE-08: plus x = L fixed in
  x (uniaxially restrained, free transversely), BilinearIsotropic SY = 250 MPa, H =
  20 GPa (Mecway's tangent modulus), ΔT = 150 K (yielding from 99.2 K). WE-09: as WE-08
  with `#ASTER-MATERIAL`: E 210 → 150 GPa, SY 300 → 100 MPa, α 1.2 → 1.4e-5 1/K linear
  between 20 and 520 °C (α secant relative to 20 °C), H = 0, ΔT = 400 K.
- **Model WE-10**: half plate 40 × 15 × 5 mm (symmetry plane y = 0; hex8 1 mm, 40 × 10
  × 5), Goldak 2/4/2/2 mm, 1000 W, 5 mm/s over 30 mm (6 s), cooling until 60 s with the
  edge y = 15 mm at T0 (the rest insulated), Δt = 0.3 s; steel with E, SY and α from 20
  to 1500 °C (illustrative, trend as for S355), H = 1 GPa, `TC_CUT` = 1500 °C;
  mechanically statically determinate (symmetry plane, node (0, 0, −5 mm) x and z
  fixed, node (40, 0, −5 mm) z fixed). WE-10b: the same with `elem_hex8` = LINE_QUAD.
- **References**: (a) uniaxial hand calculation: free ε = α·ΔT, σ = 0 (Timoshenko &
  Goodier, 1970, §150); restrained σ = −E·α·ΔT up to SY, then p = (α·ΔT − SY/E)/(1 +
  H/E), |σ| = SY + H·p, transverse strain α·ΔT − ν·σ/E + p/2; cooling back elastic,
  σ_res = E·p (no reverse yielding as long as E·p < SY + H·p); (b) WE-09: the same
  formulas with E(T), SY(T), α(T) along the path (heating, cooling, reverse yielding at
  σ = +SY(T)), numerically in small steps (`tdep_path`); (c) WE-10: equilibrium of the
  residual stress in a free plate (net force and moment over the cross-section x =
  20 mm, trapezoidal rule on the nodes), yielding in the weld (von Mises = SY + H·p),
  and the signs of the pattern and the shrinkage (Radaj, 1992; Masubuchi, 1980); the
  transverse shrinkage between 0.2 and 1 × the upper bound α·q/(v·ρ·c·t) = 0.103 mm.
- **Result**:

| Case | Check | Deviation | Tolerance |
|---|---|---|---|
| WE-07 | u_x and u_y at ΔT = 40 and 100 K | ≤ 4·10⁻⁸ | 0.1% |
| WE-07 | max \|σxx\|; u_x after cooling back | 3·10⁻⁷ Pa; 4·10⁻²² m | 1e-4·E·α·ΔT; 1e-4·α·ΔT·L |
| WE-08 | σxx elastic (ΔT = 50 K); plastic (150 K); p; transverse displacement | ≤ 3·10⁻⁸ | 1% |
| WE-08 | residual stress +116.87 MPa; ε_p,xx; transverse displacement; reaction force after cooling back | ≤ 4·10⁻⁸ | 1% |
| WE-09 | σxx elastic −124.44 MPa; plastic −140 MPa (= −SY(T)); cooling elastic +107.15 MPa | ≤ 2·10⁻⁸ | 1% |
| WE-09 | residual stress +300 MPa (= SY(T0), reverse yielding); ε_p,xx −1.4286e-3 | ≤ 1·10⁻⁸ | 1% |
| WE-10 | equilibrium: net force / (SY·A); moment / (SY·A·t) (x = 20 mm) | −0.0002; −0.010 | ±0.03 |
| WE-10 | von Mises in the weld / (SY + H·p) | 1.002 | 5% |
| WE-10 | tension in the weld, compression at y ≥ 8 mm, angular distortion towards the weld side, longitudinal and transverse shrinkage | all correct | sign |
| WE-10 | transverse shrinkage full plate / (α·q/(v·ρ·c·t)) | 0.75 | 0.2 … 1.0 |
| WE-10b | the same with LINE_QUAD; σxx in the weld / SY(T0) | equilibrium 0.0005 / −0.0004; von Mises 1.014; transverse shrinkage 0.83; σxx 1.13 | as WE-10; 1 ± 35% |

- **Interpretation**:
  - The chain is exact for homogeneous states: the temperature goes unchanged per step
    from the thermal to the mechanical result (`ARCHIVAGE` at the thermal times),
    `VALE_REF` and the functions of TEMP are in °C, and Mecway's *tangent modulus* is
    the plastic modulus H here too.
  - WE-09 confirms the convention of `ELAS_FO`: α is the secant coefficient relative to
    `TEMP_DEF_ALPHA` (= the reference temperature), and Code-Aster scales the stress
    with a changing E (elastic cooling: σ = E(T)·(p − α(T)·ΔT)). Reverse yielding during
    cooling gives the classic residual stress equal to the yield strength at room
    temperature.
  - WE-10 (hex8 `3D`, 9.9 min): the residual stress is in equilibrium and the material
    in the weld yields (von Mises = SY + H·p). The pattern agrees with the literature:
    tension in the welding direction in the weld, compression next to it (−220 … −370 MPa
    at y = 8 … 15 mm: the plate is narrow, the compressive zone has to balance the
    tension within 15 mm), the edges rotate 8.9 mrad towards the weld side,
    longitudinal shrinkage 0.013 mm on the weld axis (40 mm) and transverse shrinkage
    0.077 mm (full plate, mid-thickness; 0.126 mm at the top). The magnitude of σxx in
    the weld is not reliable with linear hex8: 466 / 641 / 923 MPa top / middle / bottom
    (1.3–2.6 × SY) with σyy up to 418 and σzz 194 MPa in the middle of a 5 mm plate, a
    triaxial state that indicates volumetric locking (KNOWN_BUGS 64; Radaj and Masubuchi
    report longitudinal stresses of the order of the yield strength). WE-10b (LINE_QUAD)
    gave with quadratic elements (53 min) the longitudinal stress in the weld
    400–450 MPa (1.13 × SY(T0), equal to SY + H·p), σzz ≈ 0 and equilibrium to 0.05%;
    the angular distortion is 39% larger (12.4 mrad): [Welding.md](Welding.md) §9.5.
  - Without `TC_CUT` and line search the LINE_QUAD variant did not converge (Newton
    diverged in the second step, also after cutting three times): the thermal strain in
    the weld pool kept growing to 4.6% at 3400 K. Both are now in the bridge (`TC_CUT`
    as a key, `RECH_LINEAIRE` with plasticity).

### Welding: metallurgy (MT-01 .. MT-08)

Module `tests_lassen_meta.py` (group "Metallurgy", `python run_verificatie.py MT`).
User documentation: [Welding.md](Welding.md) §10.

- **Goal**: the phase transformations of `#ASTER-METALLURGIE` (`CALC_META`, steel model
  `ACIER`, with the standard datasets 16MND5 and HSNV101A or an own `DEFI_TRC`), the
  hardness, and the metallurgical law in the thermal → mechanical chain
  (`mechanica=ja`: `ELAS_META_FO`, `META_P_IL`, `AFFE_VARC` `M_ACIER`).
- **Model MT-01..05, MT-08**: bar 100 × 10 × 10 mm (hex8 4 × 1 × 1) with an imposed
  temperature on all nodes (time table), so that every point has the same history (as
  in hsnv101a and mtlp100b). MT-01: 900 → 0 °C in 180 s (Δt 1 s), `staal=HSNV101A,
  begin=austeniet, mechanica=ja`, Thermal stress T_ref = 900 °C, traction 0 → 360 MPa
  (0..60 s) on x = L, symmetry planes (hsnv101a in 3D). MT-02: 900 → 20 °C at
  1000 °C/s (Δt 0.02 s), `staal=16MND5, begin=austeniet`. MT-03: 600 → 1000 °C at
  10 °C/s (Δt 0.1 s), `begin=ferriet`. MT-04: 20 → 1000 °C (10 s), 2 s hold, quench at
  1000 °C/s (Δt 0.02 s), `mechanica=ja, ALPHA_F=1.4e-5, ALPHA_A=2.2e-5, DEPS=1e-2,
  SY=1e10` (elastic), free bar; MT-05: the same with x = L fixed. MT-08: 900 → 20 °C at
  100 °C/s, an own `DEFI_TRC` in `#ASTER` code (two histories without transformation)
  with `MS0=300, ALPHA=-0.011`.
- **Model MT-06, MT-07**: the weld bead of WE-10 (half plate 40 × 15 × 5 mm, Goldak
  1000 W, 5 mm/s, Δt 0.3 s). MT-06 thermal only with `staal=16MND5, begin=ferriet,
  TAUX_1=TAUX_3=0.001`; MT-07 adds the chain with `mechanica=ja` (ALPHA_A 2.35·10⁻⁵,
  DEPS 1·10⁻², yield strength per phase illustrative) and the `#ASTER-MATERIAL` of WE-10.
- **References**: (a) MT-01: the test case hsnv101a itself (AXIS, Code-Aster 17.4, run
  with an extra `CREA_TABLE` of `META_NOEU` at node N13: the test case has no TEST_RESU
  on the phases) and the TEST_RESU values of hsnv101a (SIYY, EPYY at N13 at 47, 48, 64
  and 114 s); (b) Koistinen-Marburger Z_M = Z_a(Ms)·(1 − exp(ALPHA·(MS0 − T)))
  (Koistinen & Marburger, 1959; with the minus-sign convention of `META_ACIER`); (c)
  austenitisation dZ/dt = (Z_eq(T) − Z)/τ with Z_eq linear between Ac1 and Ac3,
  analytical for a linear temperature and constant τ; (d) the strain of `ELAS_META_FO`
  with `PHASE_REFE='FROID'`: ε = (1 − Z_a)·α_f·ΔT + Z_a·(α_a·ΔT − DEPS), and σ = −E·ε for
  restrained expansion; (e) MT-06/MT-07: consistency with Ac1/Ac3, equilibrium of the
  residual stress and the sign compared with WE-10.
