- **Result**:

| Case | Check | Deviation | Tolerance |
|---|---|---|---|
| MT-01 | bainite at 64 .. 112 s and austenite at 100 s against hsnv101a (6 digits) | ≤ 1.3·10⁻⁶ | 1% |
| MT-01 | σxx (47, 64, 114 s) against TEST_RESU SIYY | ≤ 5·10⁻⁷ | 1% |
| MT-01 | εxx (47, 48, 64, 114 s) against TEST_RESU EPYY (3.47·10⁻² with yielding) | ≤ 5.2·10⁻⁵ | 1% |
| MT-02 | martensite at 400 .. 240 °C (Koistinen-Marburger); hardness (mixture) | ≤ 4·10⁻⁸ | 1% |
| MT-02 | martensite at 20 °C (KM 0.99995) | −0.68% (Code-Aster stops at 0.69% austenite) | 1% |
| MT-03 | austenite at 740, 780, 820, 840 °C | ≤ 2·10⁻⁸ | 1% |
| MT-03 | austenite at 850 / 860 °C (just above Ac3) | +0.22% / +0.01% | 1% |
| MT-04 | εxx at 1000, 600, 400, 340, 300 °C (heating, austenite, martensite) | ≤ 6·10⁻⁶ | 1% |
| MT-04 | εxx after quenching; max \|σxx\| | −6.9·10⁻⁵ (−0.0069·DEPS); 1·10⁻⁵ Pa | 0.01·DEPS; 0.1 MPa |
| MT-05 | σxx = −E·ε at the same temperatures (−2312 .. −106 MPa) | ≤ 6·10⁻⁶ | 1% |
| MT-06 | nodes inconsistent with Ac1/Ac3; ferrite in the base; sum of phases; austenite after cooling | 0; 1; 1; 0.005 | 0; 1e-9; 1e-6; 0.01 |
| MT-06 | hardness in the weld > base; width of the austenite zone | 423 HV > 184 HV; 3 mm | sign; 1 .. 5 mm |
| MT-07 | equilibrium net force / moment; martensite + bainite in the weld | 0.0009 / −0.0039; 0.995 | ±0.03; ±0.02 |
| MT-07 | σxx weld axis top < WE-10 (466 MPa) | 176 MPa | sign |
| MT-08 | martensite at 250, 150, 50, 20 °C with own CCT (MS0 300 °C, ALPHA −0.011) | ≤ 3·10⁻⁸ | 1% |

- **Interpretation**:
  - The bridge gives `CALC_META` the same input as the test cases: MT-01 reproduces
    hsnv101a in 3D (phases and the mechanical TEST_RESU values, including yielding
    during the transformation). The temperature is in °C, like AR3/MS0/AC1/AC3 and the
    CCT diagram.
  - Koistinen-Marburger and austenitisation on slow heating are exact; the formula of
    `ELAS_META_FO` (transformation strain) is confirmed by hand and determines both the
    strain of a free bar and the stress of a clamped one.
  - Measured limits (KNOWN_BUGS 72, [Welding.md](Welding.md) §10.5): martensite
    formation stops at ~0.7% austenite; with fast heating and large steps the
    austenitisation lags behind (MT-06 with the dataset τ 0.34 s: weld axis 6%
    austenite; with τ = 0.001 s fully). With τ = 0.001 s MT-06 gives a plausible
    heat-affected zone: fusion zone and coarse-grained HAZ 82% martensite and 18%
    bainite (423 HV), intercritical at 3 mm (peak 796 °C) 44% ferrite, 20% bainite, 36%
    martensite (304 HV), outside that ferrite (184 HV).
  - MT-07 (hex8, 13.6 min): the martensite expansion lowers the longitudinal stress in
    the weld (176 / 4 MPa top / mid-thickness against 466 / 641 MPa in WE-10) and moves
    the tensile peak to the edge of the transformed zone; the angular distortion drops
    from 8.9 to 5.2 mrad. Qualitative (illustrative phase data, locking of hex8).

### Welding: filler material that is born (WE-11 .. WE-13)

Module `tests_lassen_geboorte.py` (group "Welding", `python run_verificatie.py WE-11
WE-12 WE-13`). User documentation: [Welding.md](Welding.md) §11.

- **Purpose**: filler material that only takes part once the source passes
  (`#ASTER-LASBRON birth=`, "quiet element"): thermally no heat storage and conduction
  before birth (ρ·c × 10⁻⁴, λ × 10⁻⁸, `THER_FO` of `INST` and `NEUT1`, or a quiet
  `THER_NL` material and `THER_NON_LINE` per birth step), mechanically no stiffness and
  stress before birth and stress-free at birth (`STAT_NON_LINE` per birth step,
  `ETAT_INIT` with the stress of the new elements set to zero).
- **Model WE-11**: half plate 30 × 8 × 4 mm (symmetry plane y = 0, hex8 0.75 mm) with
  a 30 × 2.25 × 1.5 mm bead on top in two pieces (`LASNAAD_1` x 0..15 mm, `LASNAAD_2`
  x 15..30 mm), each with its own Goldak source 1.5/3/1.5/1.5 mm, 500 W (250 W in the
  half plate), 5 mm/s: piece 1 from t = 0 to 1.8 s (x 3..12 mm), piece 2 from 4 to 5.8
  s (x 18..27 mm); cooling to 9 s; Δt = 0.1 s, θ = 0.57; all faces insulated; steel
  λ = 30 W/m/K, ρ = 7800 kg/m³, c = 600 J/kg/K. WE-11b: same with λ 30 → 25 W/m/K and
  c 500 → 800 J/kg/K (300 .. 1500 K; `THER_NON_LINE`).
- **Model WE-12**: bar 100 × 10 × 10 mm (hex8 4 × 1 × 1); part A (x 0..50 mm) exists
  from the start, part B (`LASNAAD_B`) is born at once at t = 6 s (`birth=yes,
  lead=1`); imposed temperature on all nodes T0 → T0 + 100 K (5 s), constant until 6
  s, back to T0 (11 s); x = 0 and x = L fixed in x, transversely free. E = 210 GPa, α =
  1.2e-5 1/K; WE-12b bilinear SY = 250 MPa, H = 20 GPa; WE-12c a hardening curve 250 →
  270 MPa at plastic strain 0.01 (`TRACTION`).
- **Model WE-13**: half plate 30 × 8 × 4 mm (hex8 1 mm along the weld), layer 1 (30 ×
  2.25 × 1.5 mm, `LASNAAD_1`) and layer 2 on top (30 × 1.5 × 1.5 mm, `LASNAAD_2`),
  each born with its own source (500 W, 5 mm/s, x 5..25 mm; layer 1 from 0 to 4 s,
  layer 2 from 6 to 10 s), cooling to 30 s with the edge y = 8 mm at T0, Δt = 0.2 s;
  thermal → mechanical chain with E/SY/α(T) as WE-10 (H = 1 GPa, `TC_CUT` = 1500 °C);
  symmetry plane + statically determinate; hex8 `3D` (1 140 elements).
- **References**: (a) energy balance of the living volume (insulated): ∫ρ(H(T) −
  H(T0)) dV = the input energy according to the θ scheme with the source on over [t0,
  t_end] (Σ Δt (θ F_k + (1 − θ) F_k−1): after the end of a source q (t_on + (1 − θ)
  Δt)); which elements are alive is computed by the test itself from the birth rule
  (§10.3 of Welding.md), independently of the bridge; (b) uniaxial hand calculation
  (Timoshenko & Goodier, 1970, §150): A expands freely (α·ΔT·L/2), after birth at T1
  and cooling to T0 σ = E·α·(T1 − T0) in A and B, with linear hardening p = (α·ΔT −
  SY/E)/(1 + H/E), σ = SY + H·p; (c) WE-13 qualitative (Radaj, 1992; Masubuchi, 1980):
  melting, cooling between the layers, reheating, tension in the bead, compression next
  to it, equilibrium.
- **Result**:

| Case | Check | Deviation / value | Tolerance |
|---|---|---|---|
| WE-11 | energy of the living volume t = 3.5 s (piece 2 unborn) / 5 s (piece 2 being born) / 9 s | +1.19 / +1.49 / +1.59% | 2% |
| WE-11 | heat in the unborn piece 2 as if it were real material / input (3.5 s) | 0.50% | 1% |
| WE-11 | max. T on top of the unborn piece 2 (3.5 s), next to the hot piece 1 | T0 + 0.49 K | 1 K |
| WE-11 | absorbed power source 1 / source 2 (job) | −0.18 / −0.10% | 2% |
| WE-11b | same with λ(T), c(T) (`THER_NON_LINE` per birth step) | +1.12 / +1.40 / +1.54%; 0.50%; T0 + 0.62 K | as WE-11 |
| WE-12 | u_x(L/2) before birth (α·ΔT·L/2) | −0.02% | 1% |
| WE-12 | \|σxx\| in the unborn B / (E·α·ΔT) | 1.8·10⁻⁴ | 10⁻³ |
| WE-12 | σxx in B 1 s after birth (E·α·20 K = 50.4 MPa) | −0.04% | 1% |
| WE-12 | residual stress B / A (E·α·ΔT = 252 MPa) | −0.01 / −0.01% | 1% |
| WE-12b | residual stress B (SY + H·p = 250.17 MPa); p | 0.00%; −0.7% | 1% |
| WE-12c | residual stress B with hardening curve (250.02 MPa); p | 0.00%; +0.01% | 1% |
| WE-13 | top of layer 1: peak / 1773 K; cooled between the layers; reheated | 1.52; 530 K; 1 750 K | 1 ± 0.5 × …; sign |
| WE-13 | layer 2 before birth: max. von Mises / SY; max. T | 4.7·10⁻⁴; T0 + 0.03 K | 10⁻³; 1 K |
| WE-13 | equilibrium net longitudinal force / (SY·A) (x = 15 mm); tension on top of the bead; compression at the edge | −0.0025; all right | ±0.03; sign |

- **Interpretation**:
  - Before birth the filler takes up no heat: the energy of the living volume follows
    the input energy, and even with the real ρ·c the unborn piece would hold less than
    1% (only the nodes on the interface are hot; actually stored 10⁻⁴ of that). At
    birth an element takes the temperature of its nodes; those on the interface are
    already hot: hence the energy after birth lies ~1% above the input (tolerance 2%).
  - WE-11 and WE-11b compute along the two routes (`THER_FO` in one solve;
    `THER_NON_LINE` per birth step, with c(T)); with constant tables the second route
    gave the same energy as the first in a prototype (to 10⁻⁶).
  - WE-12 proves the stress-free birth: without birth the bar would be stress-free after
    cooling back; with birth at T0 + 100 K, B shrinks α·ΔT relative to its birth
    temperature and σ = E·α·ΔT = 252 MPa follows. The residual stress in A is 30 kPa
    lower than in B: at birth A had the stress with which it compressed the quiet B
    (E × 10⁻⁴) (−60 kPa); B starts at zero.
  - Found and fixed in this step: (1) Code-Aster 17.4 computes two `AFFE_CHAR_THER_F`
    loads with `SOURCE` in one analysis both with the function of the first (with two
    sources WE-11 received 2 × the energy of source 1 and source 2 never acted; also
    with constant `FORMULE`s): the weld sources are now in one load (now also the
    Mecway formula sources, TH-40 .. TH-42); (2) with the consistent heat capacity a quiet node
    followed the temperature rate of its hot neighbours through the mass coupling
    (WE-13: +138 K before birth, with a dip below T0 while they heated): the filler now
    uses `3D_DIAG`; (3) the source was sometimes off at the end time by rounding (v·
    (t_end − t0) just above L): now with a margin of 10⁻⁹.

## 9. Assumptions and not covered

**Assumptions of the verification**
- The reference is an analytical solution of the idealised problem (linear elasticity,
  Euler or Timoshenko beam, Kirchhoff plate, 1D wave, semi-infinite body). Where the FE
  model describes something slightly different (3D clamp, Mindlin shell), this is named
  as a model error and included in the tolerance.
- Quantities are read at nodes (`displ*`, `stress*` = `SIGM_NOEU`, von Mises of shells,
  `temperature`, `heatflux*`), i.e. as the user sees them in Mecway. Nodal stresses are
  extrapolated and averaged and converge more slowly than displacements.
- Transient results are stored in single precision in the results file (see TH-02);
  steady-state and mechanical results in double precision.
- Mecway comparisons were solved headless with Mecway 33 (`mecway.exe model.liml solve`
  with `<solutionfile>`); Mecway's internal solver does not compute plasticity, CCX was
  used for that.

**Not covered by these tests** (partly compared elsewhere; checked against §4–§8):
- radiation (`RAYONNEMENT`; Code-Aster test tpna01b in §10 and the comparison with
  Mecway in [Thermal.md](Thermal.md) §5), `heatflow` on nodes, time tables for thermal
  loads other than an imposed temperature (that one is covered: WE-07 .. WE-10, MT) and
  thermal shells outside Z = constant (idem);
- kinematic hardening and Ramberg-Osgood (bilinear isotropic is covered: NL-01, WE-08,
  VM-11a; a hardening curve: WE-12c; see the variants in [Failure.md](Failure.md) §5),
  element failure ([Failure.md](Failure.md) §5);
- friction (NL-03 is frictionless), bonded contact except shell on shell (LS-07),
  contact in dynamics (implicit and
  explicit);
- DKT at large displacements (KNOWN_BUGS 11; NL-02 only solids and `COQUE_3D`; beams
  with large rotations: `POU_D_T_GD`, NL-80 .. NL-90), and with `POU_D_T_GD` large imposed
  rotations, element offset and couplings (a flexible joint only with U, V and W free,
  NL-90);
- gap and tension-only elements, dampers in transient dynamics (DY, MD; dampers are
  covered in harmonic response and complex modes: HR-01d, MC, VH-04c).

Covered and therefore no longer in this list: a temperature field as a load in a
mechanical analysis (TH-03; from a thermal analysis: WE-07 .. WE-10, MT), centrifugal
load (MP-04, CY), dampers (HR-01d, MC, VH-04c) and a hardening curve (WE-12c).

## 10. Official Code-Aster validation tests

The bridge verification tests the translation Mecway → Code-Aster. This addition tests
the Code-Aster installation itself (17.4, Windows build `v2025`) on the parts the bridge
uses, with the official validation tests from `share/aster/tests`. Each test has a V
document of the same name in the Code-Aster documentation (V2 linear dynamics, V3
linear statics, V4 thermal, V6 nonlinear statics; e.g. SSLL102 = V3.01.102; EDF R&D,
n.d.).

```
cd TestFiles/Verificatie/code_aster_testcases
python run_code_aster_tests.py            # all 19, about 1 minute
python run_code_aster_tests.py -j 3 ssn   # selection, in parallel
```

Per test: `run_aster.bat --ctest <installation>/share/aster/tests/<test>.export` in
`%TEMP%/ca_testcases/<test>` (as ctest does); mesh and `.comm` stay in the
installation. From the `.mess` come the diagnosis and every TEST_RESU line (OK/NOOK per
reference type), from the `.code` the options actually used (MODELISATION, RELATION,
DEFORMATION, SCHEMA, FORMULATION, SOLVEUR METHODE). Output:
`TestFiles/Verificatie/code_aster_testcases/resultaten.md`, `.csv` and `.json`.

**Outcome: 19 of 19 tests OK, 1598 TEST_RESU checks, 0 NOOK.**

| Test | Used by the bridge | Subject | Reference (count) | Max. error (tol.) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sslv04b | `MECA_STATIQUE` 3D, hex20/penta15, `PRES_REP` | thick-walled cylinder under internal pressure (Lamé) | ANALYTIQUE 77 | 4.8% (6%) |
| ssll102a | `POU_D_E` | cantilever, unit forces and moments | ANALYTIQUE 318 | 9e-7 (1e-6) |
| ssls101b | DKT, `FORCE_COQUE`, `PESANTEUR` | circular plate under pressure | ANALYTIQUE 35 | 5.6% (6%) |
| ssls105a | DKT doubly curved | NAFEMS LE3, pinched hemisphere | SOURCE_EXTERNE 2 | 0.66% (1%) |
| ssls114a | `COQUE_3D`, `PRES_REP`/`FORCE_COQUE` | cylindrical shell under pressure | ANALYTIQUE 48 | 27% (28%), see below |
| ssnp15a | `VMIS_ISOT_LINE` | plate, non-proportional tension + shear | ANALYTIQUE 44, AUTRE_ASTER 34 | 2.4% (3%) |
| ssnp14b | `VMIS_CINE_LINE` | NAFEMS tension + shear, kinematic | NON_REGRESSION 13 only | – |
| hsnv100g | `VMIS_ISOT_TRAC` + `ALPHA`/`AFFE_VARC TEMP` | thermoplasticity | ANALYTIQUE 12 | 0.001% (0.1%) |
| ssnp159a | `GDEF_LOG` (also `PETIT`, `SIMO_MIEHE`) | tension to large strain | ANALYTIQUE 6 | 0.15% (1%) |
| ssnv122a | `GREEN_LAGRANGE`, follower `PRES_REP` | rigid rotation + follower pressure | ANALYTIQUE 18 | 0.004% (0.1%) |
| ssnv167d | `DEFI_CONTACT` CONTINUE (Newton) | two blocks in contact | ANALYTIQUE 20 | 4e-16 (1e-8) |
| sdls115a | `DYNA_NON_LINE` `DIFF_CENT` and HHT | plate strip with sine solution | ANALYTIQUE 8, AUTRE_ASTER 3 | 5.5% (10%) |
| sdlv121d | `DYNA_NON_LINE` HHT/NEWMARK, continuum | plane wave in a column | ANALYTIQUE 8 | 2.1% (2.2%) |
| tpll01e | `THER_LINEAIRE` steady state | `TEMP_IMPO`, `FLUX_REP`, `ECHANGE` | ANALYTIQUE 7 | 4e-9 (1e-3) |
| ttlv01a | `THER_LINEAIRE` transient, θ | body with convection in time | ANALYTIQUE 50 | 3.3% (5%) |
| tpna01b | `THER_NON_LINE`, `RAYONNEMENT` | conduction with radiation | ANALYTIQUE 5 | 0.087% (0.2%) |
| tplv101c | `THER_NON_LINE`, `PLAN` (thermal shell) | sources, flux, wall contact | ANALYTIQUE 70 | 1e-14 (1e-3) |
| sslv153a | `CALC_ERREUR` `ERME` (error estimator) | block hexa/penta/pyram/tetra, `LINE_QUAD`, free boundaries with pressure ~0 | NON_REGRESSION 8 only | – (also through the bridge: FS-03) |
| sslv111f | `CALC_ERREUR` `ERME` and `ERZ1` in 3D | plate with a hole (Kirsch), hexa8/pyram5/tetra4, `FORCE_FACE` | ANALYTIQUE 17 | 12% (15%) |

"Max. error" is the largest relative error of a reference check (not NON_REGRESSION),
with the tolerance the official test itself sets. MUMPS is the solver in 17 of the 19
tests (tpll01e and part of sdlv121d choose LDLT, ssls105a solves with
`FACTORISER`/`RESOUDRE` itself).

Remarks:
- **No NOOK, no missing modules**: MUMPS, MED and the Python helper files work in the
  Windows build. The list of tests that fail on Windows (`validation.md` in the
  installation, 17 of 3769) contains none of these tests.
- **Alarms** (diagnosis `<A>_ALARM`, test passed): ssls114a `MODELISA8_14`,
  `UTILITAI_4`; ssnv122a `CHARGES9_53`; sdls115a `MODELISA8_14`; sdlv121d
  `MECANONLINE_23`. All intended by the test itself.
- **ssls114a, `COQUE_3D`**: the displacements agree to 3.5%, but `SIGM_NOEU` SIXX
  deviates 25–27% from the analytical value at some points; the official test accepts
  that (tolerance 27–28%). The cause (surface layer, extrapolation to the nodes or thin
  against thick shell theory) is NOT verified. For the bridge, which sends shell
  stresses to Mecway as `SIGM_NOEU`: use nodal stresses of `COQUE_3D` on curved shells
  with care.
- **ssnp14b (NAFEMS)**: the `.comm` only compares with its own earlier results
  (NON_REGRESSION); for kinematic hardening this is no independent verification.
- **Not chosen**: explicit dynamics in 3D (no 3D `DIFF_CENT` test with an analytical
  reference in 17.4), penalty contact in 3D (the tests with penalisation are
  2D/axisymmetric or only have SOURCE_EXTERNE), `GROT_GDEP` for cables and shells.

Conclusion: the installation computes the parts used (3D, DKT, `COQUE_3D`, `POU_D_E`,
von Mises plasticity isotropic/kinematic/tensile curve, `GDEF_LOG`, `GREEN_LAGRANGE`
with follower pressure, continuous contact, HHT and `DIFF_CENT`, linear and nonlinear
thermal with radiation and `PLAN`, MUMPS) according to its own validation tests.
Deviations in the bridge verification therefore come from the translation or the
discretisation, not from the installation.
